# Majmu' al-Fatawa - Tome 15

## Page 7562

Volume Fifteen
The Book of Quranic Commentary
Part Two
From Surah al-A‘rāf to Surah al-Ahzāb

In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Especially Merciful.

Surah al-A‘rāf

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—said:

Section

The argument of Iblīs expressed in his words,

“I am better than him. You created me from fire and created him from clay,”

is invalid because he opposed the revealed text with analogical reasoning. Hence, some of the early generations said: “The first to reason by analogy was Iblīs, and the sun and moon were worshipped only through analogies.” The invalidity of his argument is also evident through reason in five respects.

First: He claimed that fire is better than clay. This may be disputed, for clay possesses tranquillity, dignity, stability, firmness, retentiveness, and similar qualities, whereas fire possesses lightness, fierceness, and impetuosity. Moreover, clay contains water and earth.

Second: Even if fire were better than clay, it would not necessarily follow that

---

## Page 7563

what is created from the better substance is itself better. A derivative may possess qualities absent from its source. From this very earth are created animals, minerals, and plants that are better than it. Arguing that the human being is superior to other beings because his origin is superior to theirs is a fallacious argument—the very argument used by Iblīs and by those who boast of their ancestry. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Whoever falls short through his deeds will not be advanced by his lineage.”

Third: Although Adam was created from clay, he was ennobled by having the sanctified spirit breathed into him. Hence Allah said:

“When I have fashioned him and breathed into him of My spirit, fall down before him in prostration.”

Thus, prostration was made conditional upon His breathing into him of His spirit. What establishes his superiority is this noble quality, the like of which Iblīs does not possess.

Fourth: He was created with Allah Most High’s two hands, as He said:

“What prevented you from prostrating to what I created with My two hands?”

This accords with the report transmitted from the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, through a disconnected chain, and also from ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Amr, concerning Adam’s superiority over the angels. The angels said:

“Our Lord, You have created the worldly life for the children of Adam, in which they eat, drink, clothe themselves, and marry. So grant us the Hereafter, just as You have granted them the worldly life.”

He said, “I will not do so.” They repeated their request, and He said, “I will not do so.” They repeated it again, and He said, “By My might, I will not equate a righteous one whom I created with My two hands with one to whom I said, ‘Be,’ and he was.”

Fifth: Even if it were supposed that Iblīs was superior, it could still be said that there is nothing objectionable in the superior honouring the inferior.

---

## Page 7564

The shaykh—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked about the noble verse in which Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, he and his tribe see you from where you do not see them.”

Does this apply universally, so that no one sees them, or do some people see them while others do not? Are the jinn and devils a single kind—the offspring of Iblīs—or two kinds: his offspring and those who are not his offspring?

Shaykh al-Islām Abū al-‘Abbās Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah—may Allah have mercy on him and be pleased with him, amen—replied:

Praise be to Allah. What the Quran states is that they see human beings from where human beings do not see them. This is true, and it means that they see human beings under circumstances in which human beings do not see them. It does not mean that no human being ever sees them under any circumstances. Rather, righteous people may see them, as may those who are not righteous; but they do not see them under all circumstances. The devils are the rebellious among humankind and the jinn, and all jinn are the offspring of Iblīs. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 7565

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—said:

Concerning His words:

“When they commit an indecency, they say, ‘We found our fathers doing it, and Allah has commanded us to do it.’ Say, ‘Allah does not command indecency. Do you say about Allah what you do not know?’”

The indecency meant here is exposing the private parts. This verse may therefore be cited as evidence that evil actions possess qualities that preclude their being commanded by divine law. For, in rebuking them, Allah states that He does not command indecency. This indicates that He is exalted above doing so. Were it admissible for Him to do so, He would not have declared Himself above it. It is thus established that commanding indecency is inadmissible for Him. This can only be so if the action is evil in itself. It follows that whatever is inherently indecent cannot be commanded by Allah.

This is the position of those who affirm that actions possess inherent qualities of good and evil, as most scholars do, including the Tamīmīs and Abū al-Khattāb. It stands in contrast to the view of those who say that such qualities are never established except through divine address.

Likewise, He says:

“Do not approach unlawful sexual intercourse. Indeed, it is an indecency and an evil way.”

He explains the prohibition by the qualities inherent in it: that it is an indecency and an evil way. For if

---

## Page 7566

it had become an indecency and an evil way only because it was prohibited, this reasoning would not hold, for a cause precedes its effect rather than follows it. There are many such examples in the Quran.

As for commands, His words:

“Fighting has been prescribed for you, though it is hateful to you. Yet you may hate something that is good for you, and you may love something that is bad for you. Allah knows, while you do not know.”

show that He commanded it because it is good for us, and because Allah knew about it what we did not know. Likewise, His words in the verse on purification:

“But He intends to purify you and complete His favor upon you, so that you may be grateful.”

show that He commanded purification because of the benefit it holds for us. This, too, occurs frequently in the Quran.

---

## Page 7567

Shaykh Taqī al-Dīn Ahmad ibn Taymiyyah said concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic:

“Call upon your Lord humbly and privately. Indeed, He does not love transgressors.”

“And do not cause corruption on the earth after it has been set right, and call upon Him in fear and hope. Indeed, Allah’s mercy is near to those who do good.”

These two verses encompass the etiquette of both kinds of invocation: invocation through worship and invocation through petition. In the Quran, invocation sometimes means the former, sometimes the latter, and sometimes both together. The two are inseparable. Invocation through petition means seeking what benefits the one invoking and seeking the removal and prevention of what harms him. Whoever possesses power over harm and benefit is the one worshipped; the one worshipped must necessarily possess power over benefit and harm. Thus, the Most High rebuked those who worshipped besides Him that which possesses no power to harm or benefit.

This occurs frequently in the Quran, as in His words:

“Do not invoke besides Allah that which neither benefits you nor harms you.”

And He said:

“They worship besides Allah that which neither harms them nor benefits them.”

He thus denied that these objects of worship have any power to harm or benefit, whether concerning themselves or extending to others: they possess no such power for themselves or for their worshippers. This occurs frequently in the Quran, where the Most High explains that the one worshipped must possess power

---

## Page 7568

over benefit and harm. Thus, seeking benefit and protection from harm is invocation through petition, while calling upon Him in fear and hope is invocation through worship. It is therefore clear that the two kinds are inseparable: every invocation through worship necessarily entails invocation through petition, and every invocation through petition includes invocation through worship.

Accordingly, His words:

“When My servants ask you about Me, I am indeed near. I respond to the invocation of the supplicant when he calls upon Me.”

Encompass both kinds of invocation. The verse has been explained in terms of each: “I give to him when he asks Me,” and “I reward him when he worships Me.” These two interpretations are inseparable. This is not a case of using a word with two distinct meanings in both senses, nor of using a word in both its literal and figurative senses. Rather, it is being used in its literal sense, which encompasses both matters together. Reflect on this, for it is a point of immense benefit that is seldom recognized. Most Quranic verses convey two or more meanings and belong to this category.

An example is the Most High’s saying:

“Establish prayer from the sun’s decline until the darkness of night.”

“Decline” has been interpreted as the sun’s passing its zenith and as its setting. These are not two conflicting interpretations; rather, the word encompasses both. For “decline” means inclination, and the sun’s decline is its inclination. This inclination has a beginning and an end: it begins when the sun passes its zenith and ends when it sets. In this respect, the word encompasses both.

Another example is the interpretation of “the darkening one” as night and as the moon. For this

---

## Page 7569

is not a disagreement; rather, the term encompasses both because they are inseparable, for the moon is the sign of night. There are many similar examples.

Among them is the Most High’s saying:

“Say, ‘My Lord would not care for you were it not for your invocation.’”

This means your invocation of Him. Another interpretation is His calling you to worship Him, in which case the verbal noun is annexed to its object. In the first interpretation, it is annexed to its subject, and this is the stronger of the two views. On this understanding, both kinds of invocation are intended, though the reference to invocation through worship is more evident. That is, He would not care for you were it not for your hope in Him. Worshipping Him necessarily entails petitioning Him, so both kinds are included.

Another example is the Most High’s saying:

“Your Lord has said, ‘Call upon Me; I will respond to you.’”

Invocation here encompasses both kinds, though the reference to invocation through worship is more evident. This is why He follows it with:

“Indeed, those who are too arrogant to worship Me…”

The verse continues. Invocation in this verse is explained in both senses.

Al-Tirmidhī narrated that al-Nu‘mān ibn Bashīr said: “I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say from the pulpit, ‘Indeed, invocation is worship.’ He then recited the Most High’s saying, ‘Your Lord has said, “Call upon Me; I will respond to you,”’ continuing the verse.” Al-Tirmidhī said: “A good and authentic hadith.”

---

## Page 7570

As for the Most High’s saying:

“Indeed, those you invoke besides Allah will never create a fly, even if they all gathered for that purpose.”

The verse continues. And His saying:

“They invoke none but female deities besides Him.”

The verse continues. And His saying:

“And those they used to invoke before will have vanished from them.”

The verse continues. In these and every other passage mentioning the polytheists’ invocation of their idols, what is meant is invocation through worship, which includes invocation through petition. The reference to invocation through worship is more evident, for three reasons:

First: They said:

“We worship them only that they may bring us nearer to Allah.”

They thus acknowledged that their invocation of them was their worship of them.

Second: Allah Most High explained this invocation elsewhere, as in His saying:

“And it will be said to them, ‘Where are those you used to worship besides Allah? Can they help you, or even help themselves?’”

And His saying:

“Indeed, you and what you worship besides Allah are fuel for Hell. You will enter it.”

And His saying:

“I do not worship what you worship.”

Thus, their invocation of their gods is their worship of them.

Third: They used to worship them in times of ease, but when hardship befell them, they invoked Allah alone and abandoned them. Nevertheless, they would ask them to meet some of their needs and make requests of them. Their invocation of them was therefore both invocation through worship and invocation through petition.

As for the Most High’s saying:

“So invoke Allah, devoting your religion sincerely to Him.”

This is invocation through worship. It means: worship Him alone and devote your worship sincerely to Him; do not worship anyone else alongside Him.

---

## Page 7571

As for the words of Abraham, peace be upon him:

“Indeed, my Lord is the Hearer of supplication.”

What is meant here is hearing in its special sense: hearing with acceptance and response, not hearing in its general sense, for He hears everything that can be heard. Accordingly, invocation here includes both invocation through worship and invocation through petition. The Lord Most High’s hearing consists in His rewarding praise and answering requests. He hears both.

As for the words of Zechariah, peace be upon him:

“Never, my Lord, have I been disappointed in my supplication to You.”

It has been said that this refers to invocation through petition, meaning: You have always answered me and have never disappointed me through rejection or deprivation. He thus seeks to draw near to Him, glorified and exalted is He, by invoking His previous answers and kindness. This meaning is evident here.

As for the Most High’s saying:

“Say, ‘Call upon Allah or call upon the Most Merciful.’”

The verse continues. The well-known interpretation is that this refers to invocation through petition, which was the occasion of the verse’s revelation. They said: The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would invoke his Lord, sometimes saying, “O Allah,” and sometimes, “O Most Merciful.” The polytheists therefore thought that he was invoking two gods, so Allah revealed this verse.

As for His saying:

“Indeed, we used to invoke Him before. He is indeed the Most Kind, the Most Merciful.”

This is invocation through worship, which includes pursuing His path in hope and fear. It means: We used to devote our worship sincerely to Him. It was through this that they merited Allah’s protection from the torment of the scorching wind, not merely through asking, which is common to both those who are saved and others; for He, glorified is He, is petitioned by those in the heavens

---

## Page 7572

and on earth.

“We will never invoke any god besides Him.”

That is, we will never worship anyone other than Him. The same applies to His saying:

“Do you invoke Baal?”

The verse continues.

As for His saying:

“And it will be said, ‘Call upon your partners,’ and they will call upon them.”

This is invocation through petition. Allah will confound and disgrace them on the Day of Resurrection by showing them that their partners do not answer their call. What is meant is not, “Worship them.” This resembles the Most High’s saying:

“And on the Day He says, ‘Call upon those you claimed to be My partners,’ they will call upon them, but they will not answer them.”

With this understood, the Most High’s saying:

“Call upon your Lord humbly and in secret.”

encompasses both kinds of invocation. Its apparent meaning, however, is invocation through petition, which includes invocation through worship. Hence His command to offer it privately and secretly.

Al-Hasan said:

“Supplication in secret surpasses supplication in public seventyfold. The Muslims would strive earnestly in supplication, yet no sound could be heard from them; it was only a whisper between them and their Lord, mighty and majestic is He. That is because Allah, mighty and majestic is He, says:

‘Call upon your Lord humbly and in secret.’

He also mentioned a righteous servant and expressed approval of his action, saying:

‘When he called upon his Lord with a quiet plea.’”

Offering supplication in secret has many benefits.

First, it reflects greater faith, for the person doing so knows that Allah hears supplication offered in secret.

Second, it shows greater courtesy and reverence, for, before kings, one does not raise

---

## Page 7573

one’s voice, and they detest anyone who raises his voice in their presence. To Allah belongs the highest description. Since He hears supplication offered in secret, proper courtesy before Him requires that one lower one’s voice when supplicating.

Third, it more fully expresses the humility and submissiveness that are the spirit, essence, and purpose of supplication. A humble, submissive person asks as a destitute, lowly person would: his heart is broken, his limbs subdued, and his voice hushed. His lowliness, stillness, and humble pleading may become so profound that his tongue almost falters and will not obey him in speech. His heart continues to ask, seek, and implore, while his tongue remains silent out of intense humility. Such a state simply cannot accompany raising one’s voice in supplication.

Fourth, it is more conducive to sincerity.

Fifth, it more fully gathers the heart in humility during supplication, whereas raising the voice scatters its attention. The more a person lowers his voice, the more completely he directs his resolve and purpose solely towards the One upon whom he calls, glory be to Him.

Sixth—and this is a particularly subtle and beautiful point—it indicates the supplicant’s nearness to the One who is Near; his petition is not the call of someone far away to another who is far away. Hence Allah praised His servant Zechariah, saying, mighty and majestic is He:

“When he called upon his Lord with a quiet plea.”

---

## Page 7574

Thus, when the heart becomes mindful of Allah’s nearness, mighty and majestic is He, and that He is nearer to it than anyone else, it makes its supplication as quiet as possible.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, pointed to this very meaning in the authentic hadith:

“When the Companions raised their voices proclaiming Allah’s greatness while travelling with him, he said: ‘Be easy on yourselves. You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent. You are calling upon One who is All-Hearing and Near. The One upon whom you call is nearer to each of you than the neck of his riding animal.’”

Allah Most High says:

“When My servants ask you concerning Me, I am indeed near. I respond to the call of the supplicant when he calls upon Me.”

This nearness to the supplicant is a special nearness, not a general nearness to everyone. He is near to those who call upon Him and near to those who worship Him. The closest a servant is to his Lord is while prostrating. His words, Most High,

“Call upon your Lord humbly and quietly,”

direct attention to this nearness and make it known.

Seventh, it is more conducive to sustained petition and supplication: the tongue does not grow weary, nor do the limbs tire. By contrast, when a person raises his voice, his tongue may grow weary and his strength wane. This is like someone who recites and repeats what he recites: if he raises his voice, he cannot continue for long, unlike someone who lowers it.

Eighth, keeping supplication quiet better protects it from interruptions and distractions;

---

## Page 7575

for when a supplicant keeps his supplication quiet, no one knows of it, so no distraction or other disturbance arises on that account. But when he supplicates aloud, other human souls inevitably rush towards him, resisting and opposing him. Even if nothing else occurs, their attention to him unsettles his concentration, weakening the effect of his supplication. Those who have experienced this recognize it. By supplicating quietly, he protects himself from this harm.

Ninth, the greatest blessing is turning to Allah and devoting oneself to His worship. Every blessing, small or great, attracts envy in proportion to its worth. No blessing is greater than this one, and the souls of the envious fix upon it. Nothing is safer for the person envied than concealing his blessing from the envious. Jacob said to Joseph, peace be upon them both:

“Do not relate your dream to your brothers, lest they plot against you”—to the end of the verse.

How many a person has enjoyed presence of heart, inward collectedness, and a spiritual state with Allah Most High, only to speak of it and disclose it, whereupon others have robbed him of it! This is why those who know Allah and the spiritual teachers counsel keeping one’s inner life with Allah Most High secret, letting no one know of it. These people take the greatest care to conceal their states with Allah, mighty and majestic is He, and the love for Him, intimacy with Him, and collectedness of heart that He has bestowed upon them. Such concealment is especially important for the rightly guided wayfarer.

But once one of them becomes firmly established and strong, and has secured within his heart the roots of that good tree whose root is firm and whose branches reach into the sky, so that he no longer fears that storms will harm it, he is unconcerned about revealing his state with Allah Most High so that others may follow his example and take him as their guide. This is a matter of immense benefit, understood only by those who have experienced it.

---

## Page 7576

Since the supplication that we are commanded to keep quiet encompasses petition, praise, love, and turning to Allah Most High, it is among the great treasures most deserving of concealment from envious eyes. This is a valuable and beneficial insight.

Tenth, supplication is remembrance of the One being invoked—glorified and exalted is He—together with petition and praise of Him through His attributes and names. It is therefore remembrance and more. Likewise, remembrance is called supplication because it includes petition, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The best supplication is ‘Praise be to Allah.’”

He thus called “Praise be to Allah” a supplication, although it is pure praise. This is because praise encompasses love and commendation, and love is the highest form of seeking. The one who praises seeks the Beloved, and so deserves to be called a supplicant even more than someone asking for something. Praise and commendation themselves encompass the greatest form of seeking. They are therefore truly supplication—indeed, more deserving of that name than other, lesser forms of seeking.

The point is that supplication and remembrance each encompass the other and are included within it. Allah Most High says:

“And remember your Lord within yourself, in humility and fear.”

Allah Most High thus commanded His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to remember Him within himself. Mujāhid and Ibn Jurayj said: “They were commanded to remember Him in their hearts, with humble entreaty and submission, without raising their voices or shouting.”

Reflect on how He says in the verse concerning remembrance:

“And remember your Lord”—to the end of the verse.

And in the verse concerning supplication:

“Call upon your Lord in humility and in secret.”

He mentions humble entreaty in both: lowliness, meekness, and brokenness of heart.

---

## Page 7577

and this is the spirit of remembrance and supplication. He specified secrecy for supplication because of the wisdoms we have mentioned and others besides, and fear for remembrance because the one who remembers Allah needs fear. Remembrance necessarily entails love and nurtures it; whoever remembers Allah abundantly will inevitably develop love for Him through it. Yet unless love is accompanied by fear, it does not benefit the one who possesses it; rather, it harms him. For it breeds laxity and undue familiarity, and has sometimes led many deluded, ignorant people to consider it sufficient in place of their religious obligations. They say:

“The sole purpose of acts of worship is the heart’s worship, its turning towards Allah, and its love for Him. Once that purpose has been attained, occupying oneself with the means is pointless.”

A man once told me that he had reproached one of these people for entering a retreat during which he abandoned the Friday prayer. The shaykh said to him, “Do the jurists not say that if someone fears for some of his property, his obligation to attend the Friday prayer is waived?”

He replied, “Yes.”

The shaykh said, “The seeker’s heart is dearer to him than ten dirhams”—or words to that effect—“and if he goes out, his heart becomes distracted. Protecting his heart is therefore a valid excuse exempting him from the Friday prayer.”

The man replied, “You are being deluded. Your duty is to go out in obedience to the command of Allah, Mighty and Majestic.”

Consider this tremendous delusion and how it has led to casting off Islam altogether. Whoever follows this path sheds Islam in its general sense just as a snake sheds its skin, all the while imagining himself to be among the elite of the elite.

---

## Page 7578

This arises because fear of Allah is not joined to love for Him and the desire for Him. Hence one of the early generations said:

“Whoever worships Allah through love alone is a heretic; whoever worships Him through fear alone is a Harūrī; whoever worships Him through hope alone is a Murji'ite; and whoever worships Him through love, fear, and hope is a believer.”

The point is that separating love and remembrance from fear leads to these perils. When fear accompanies them, it keeps a person on the path and brings him back whenever something causes him to falter. He is like a fearful traveller carrying a whip with which he strikes his mount to keep it from leaving the path. Hope is the camel-driver whose song urges it onward, while love is its guide and the rein that stirs its longing. If the mount has neither a whip nor a staff to turn it back when it veers off the path, it will leave the path and go astray.

Nothing safeguards Allah’s limits and prohibitions, or enables those who draw near to Him to reach their goal, like fear of Him, hope in Him, and love for Him. Whenever the heart is devoid of these three, it becomes so corrupt that there is no hope of its ever being set right. Whenever any one of them weakens within it, its faith weakens accordingly.

Reflect, then, on the Quran’s subtleties and wisdom in pairing fear with remembrance and secrecy with supplication, while also indicating the pairing of secrecy with supplication and fear with remembrance. Reflect, too, on its mention of eager expectation—which is hope—in the verse on supplication, for supplication rests upon it. Unless the supplicant hopes to obtain what he asks and seeks, he will feel no impulse to seek it: seeking something one has no hope of obtaining is impossible. He mentions fear in the verse on remembrance because of the fearful person’s great need

---

## Page 7579

for it. Thus, in each verse He mentions what is appropriate to it of fear and hope. Blessed is He who sent down His words as a healing for what is in the hearts.

Regarding His words, Exalted is He:

“Indeed, He does not love the transgressors,”

it has been said that this means He does not love those who transgress in supplication, such as someone who asks for what is not fitting for him, including the ranks of the prophets and other such things. Abū Dāwūd narrated in his Sunan that ‘Abd Allāh ibn Mughaffal heard his son saying, “O Allah, I ask You for the white palace on the right side of Paradise when I enter it.” He said, “My son, ask Allah for Paradise and seek refuge in Him from the Fire, for I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say: ‘There will be people in this community who transgress in purification and supplication.’”

According to this interpretation, transgression in supplication sometimes consists of asking for something one is not permitted to request, such as assistance in committing forbidden acts.

At other times, it consists of asking for something Allah will not do, such as asking Him to keep one alive until the Day of Resurrection, or to remove the necessities of human existence, such as the need for food and drink. It also includes asking Him to disclose His unseen to one, to make one infallible, or to grant one a child without a wife, and similar requests that constitute transgression. Allah loves neither such requests nor those who make them. Transgression has also been interpreted as raising one’s voice in supplication.

Yet the verse is broader than all of this; even if transgression in supplication is intended

---

## Page 7580

by it, that is only part of what is meant. Allah does not love transgressors in anything, whether in supplication or otherwise, as He, Exalted is He, says:

“And do not transgress. Indeed, Allah does not love the transgressors.”

On this understanding, He commands people to supplicate to Him and worship Him, and declares that He does not love those who transgress—those who invoke others alongside Him. These are the most grievous transgressors, for the greatest transgression is associating partners with Allah: directing worship where it does not belong. This transgression must therefore be included in His words, Exalted is He:

“Indeed, He does not love the transgressors.”

Another form of transgression is to supplicate to Him without humble entreaty, like someone who considers himself free of need and presumes upon his Lord. This is among the gravest forms of transgression, because it is contrary to the supplication of one who humbles himself. Whoever does not ask as a needy person would, humbly and fearfully, is a transgressor.

Another form of transgression is to worship Him in ways He has not prescribed, and to praise Him in terms He has neither used in praising Himself nor authorized. This is transgression in invoking Him through praise and worship, just as one may transgress in supplication through asking and requesting.

On this understanding, the verse points to two things:

“First”: something beloved to the Lord, Glorified is He—supplication with humble entreaty and in private.

“Second”: something He hates and is displeased with—transgression. He thus commands and encourages what He loves, and warns against and forbids what He hates, using the most emphatic means of restraint and warning

---

## Page 7581

namely, that He does not love the one who commits it. And what good can anyone attain whom Allah does not love?

His words, Exalted is He,

“Indeed, He does not love the transgressors,”

coming immediately after His words,

“Call upon your Lord humbly and in private,”

show that whoever does not call upon Him humbly and in private is among the transgressors whom He does not love. The verse thus divides people into two groups: those who call upon Allah humbly and in private, and those who transgress by failing to do so.

Regarding His words, Exalted is He,

“And do not spread corruption on earth after it has been set right,”

most Quran commentators said: “Do not spread corruption there through sins.” Anyone who calls people to anything other than obedience to Allah, after Allah has set the earth right by sending the messengers, explaining His revealed law, and calling people to obey Him, is spreading corruption. Worshipping others besides Allah, calling people to others besides Him, and associating partners with Him constitute the gravest corruption on earth. Indeed, corruption on earth consists, in reality, of associating partners with Allah and disobeying His commands.

Allah, Exalted is He, says:

“Corruption has appeared on land and sea because of what people’s hands have earned.”

ʿAtiyyah said concerning the verse: “Do not disobey Allah on earth, lest Allah withhold the rain and destroy the crops because of your sins.”

Several of the early generations said: “When rain fails, the animals curse the disobedient among the children of Adam, saying: ‘O Allah, curse them! It is because of them that the earth has become barren and the rain has failed.’”

In short, associating partners with Allah, calling people to others besides Allah, and setting up an object of worship other than Him, or someone to be obeyed and followed other than the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, constitute the gravest corruption

---

## Page 7582

on earth. Neither the earth nor its inhabitants can be set right unless Allah alone is worshipped, people are called to Him and no other, and the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is obeyed and followed. Anyone else must be obeyed only when he commands obedience to the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. If he commands disobedience to him, he is neither to be heard nor obeyed.

Allah has set the earth right through His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, His religion, and the command to worship Him alone. He has forbidden corrupting it by associating partners with Him and opposing His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Whoever reflects on the state of the world will find that everything that sets the earth right stems from affirming Allah’s oneness, worshipping Him, and obeying His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Every evil, turmoil, affliction, drought, domination by an enemy, and the like stems from opposing the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and calling people to others besides Allah. Whoever reflects on this as he should will find it holds true in his own life and in the lives of others, both generally and in particular cases. There is no power nor strength except through Allah.

As for His words, Exalted is He,

“And call upon Him in fear and hope,”

the command to supplicate is repeated here because of the fear and hope mentioned alongside it. First, He commanded that He be called upon humbly and in private; then He also commanded that supplication be offered in fear and hope.

He separated these two statements with two intervening statements. The first is declarative and implies a prohibition, namely His words:

“Indeed, He does not love the transgressors.”

---

## Page 7583

and the second is an injunction, namely His words, Exalted is He:

“Do not cause corruption on earth after it has been set right.”

Both statements reinforce the first statement and confirm its meaning. Once He had fully established it and explained what contradicts it, He commanded that He be called upon in fear and hope, since His words, “Indeed, He does not love the transgressors,” relate to His words, Exalted is He, “Call upon your Lord humbly and in private.”

Since His words, “And call upon Him in fear and hope,” encompass all the stations of faith and excellence—love, fear, and hope—He followed them with His words:

“Indeed, Allah’s mercy is near to the doers of good.”

That is, His mercy reaches only those who call upon Him in fear and hope. Such a person is a doer of good, and mercy is near to him, for excellence revolves around these three foundations.

Since supplication offered humbly and in private stands opposed to the transgression of supplicating without humility or privacy, He followed this with His words, Exalted is He:

“Indeed, He does not love the transgressors.”

The expressions “humbly and in private” and “in fear and hope” are in the circumstantial accusative; that is, call upon Him humbly, privately, fearfully, and obediently.

His words, “Indeed, Allah’s mercy is near to the doers of good,” clearly indicate that carrying out this command is the excellence required of you, while what you yourselves seek from

---

## Page 7584

Allah is His mercy, and His mercy is near to the doers of good who have fulfilled His command to call upon Him humbly and in private, with fear and hope. He thus established that what you seek from Him—mercy—is commensurate with your fulfillment of what He requires of you. If you do good, you do good for yourselves.

His words, Exalted is He:

“Indeed, Allah’s mercy is near to the doers of good,”

convey meaning through their explicit wording, through the causal relationship they suggest, and through their implication. Their explicit wording indicates that mercy is near to those who do good. The causal relationship they suggest indicates that this nearness is merited through doing good, which is the reason mercy is near to them. Their implication indicates that mercy is far from those who do not do good. These, then, are three meanings conveyed by this statement.

Those who do good are singled out for the nearness of mercy because mercy is an act of goodness from Allah, Mighty and Majestic, the Most Merciful of those who show mercy. His goodness, Blessed and Exalted is He, is bestowed only upon those who do good, for recompense corresponds to deeds. Whenever they do good through their deeds, He does good to them through His mercy. As for someone who is not among those who do good, his distance from goodness causes mercy to be distant from him: distance is met with distance, and nearness with nearness. Whoever draws near to Him by doing good, Allah draws near to him with His mercy; and whoever distances himself from doing good, Allah distances His mercy from him.

Allah, Glorified is He, loves those who do good and hates those who are not among them. For anyone Allah loves, His mercy is the nearest thing to him; while for anyone Allah hates, His mercy is the furthest

---

## Page 7585

thing from him. Doing good here means carrying out what is commanded, whether the good is done to others or to oneself. The greatest forms of goodness are faith, affirming Allah’s oneness, turning back to Allah Most High, devoting oneself to Him, relying upon Him, and worshipping Him as though one sees Him—with reverence, awe, modesty, love, and fear. This is the station of “excellence,” as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said when Gabriel, peace be upon him, asked him about excellence:

“To worship Allah as though you see Him.”

Since this is excellence, His mercy is near to the person who embodies it. “Is the reward for good anything but good?” This means: is the reward for someone who worships his Lord well anything but that his Lord should bestow goodness upon him? Ibn ‘Abbās, may Allah be pleased with them both, said: “Is the reward for someone who says, ‘There is no god but Allah,’ and acts upon what Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, brought anything but Paradise?”

Ibn Abī Shaybah and others related a hadith through al-Zubayr ibn ‘Adī, from Anas ibn Mālik, may Allah be pleased with him, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, recited, ‘Is the reward for good anything but good?’ Then he said, ‘Do you know what your Lord has said?’

“They replied, ‘Allah and His Messenger know best.’

“He said, ‘Is the reward for someone upon whom I have bestowed the blessing of affirming My oneness anything but Paradise?’”

Here ends the discussion of the two verses. All praise belongs to Allah, Lord of the worlds. May Allah bless Muhammad, his family, and his Companions, and grant them peace.

---

## Page 7586

Shaykh al-Islām, may Allah have mercy upon him, said:

Concerning His words, glorified be He:

“The arrogant chiefs among his people said, ‘We will surely expel you, O Shu‘ayb, and those who believe with you from our town, unless you return to our religion.’ He said, ‘Even if we are unwilling?’”

“We would have fabricated a lie against Allah if we returned to your religion after Allah has saved us from it. It is not for us to return to it unless Allah, our Lord, should will.”

The apparent meaning of this is evidence that Shu‘ayb and those who believed with him had followed their people’s religion. This is indicated by their words, “Unless you return to our religion,” and by Shu‘ayb’s words: “Are we to return to it, ‘even if we are unwilling?’” It is also indicated by his words, “We would have fabricated a lie against Allah if we returned to your religion,” which show that they had previously followed it.

Likewise, his words, “After Allah has saved us from it,” indicate that Allah saved them from it after they had been tainted by it. There are also his words: “It is not for us to return to it unless Allah, our Lord, should will.”

The pronoun cannot refer to his people alone, because he is explicitly addressed in the words, “We will surely expel you, O Shu‘ayb.” Moreover, it is he who addresses them with the words, “Even if we are unwilling?” and the remainder of the passage. This must include the speaker himself.

A similar passage occurs in Surah Ibrāhīm:

“Those who disbelieved said to their messengers, ‘We will surely expel you from our land, unless you return to our religion.’ Then their Lord revealed to them, ‘We will surely destroy the wrongdoers’”—to the end of the verse.

---

## Page 7587

Shaykh al-Islām also said:

This is an explanation of verses that have proved so difficult that some Quran commentaries contain only erroneous interpretations of them. Among these are His words:

“We will surely expel you, O Shu‘ayb, and those who believe with you from our town”—to the end of the verse—and other passages of similar meaning.

The correct understanding is that Allah, glorified be He, chooses for His message only those who are among the best of their people, even in lineage, as indicated in the hadith of Heraclius. It is no fault in someone raised among ignorant polytheists to follow a religion like theirs, provided he is known for truthfulness, trustworthiness, doing what they recognize as obligatory, and refraining from what they recognize as reprehensible.

Allah Most High says:

“We never punish until We have sent a messenger.”

Thus, these people did not deserve punishment, and there is nothing in this to deter others from accepting what they convey. For this reason, none of the polytheists cited it as grounds for discrediting them. There is agreement that a messenger may be sent without knowing about the prophethood and revealed laws of earlier messengers, and that whoever does not acknowledge these after the message has come is a disbeliever,

---

## Page 7588

a disbeliever, while the messengers themselves had no knowledge of it before revelation, let alone acknowledged it.

Allah Most High says:

“He sends down the angels with revelation by His command”—to the end of the verse.

He also says:

“He sends revelation by His command to whomever He wills of His servants, to warn of the Day of Meeting.”

Thus, He placed their warning concerning monotheism on the same footing as their warning of the Day of Meeting: they learned of both through revelation. As for the report that idols were made hateful to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, this need not have been the case for every prophet, for he is the foremost of the children of Adam. A messenger who grows up among disbelieving people among whom there is no prophethood is more perfect than others by virtue of Allah’s support through knowledge and guidance, victory and ascendancy, as was the case with Noah and Abraham.

This is why Allah mentions the two of them in connection with this matter in such statements as:

“We certainly sent Noah and Abraham”—to the end of the verse.

“Indeed, Allah chose Adam, Noah, and the family of Abraham”—to the end of the verse.

Noah was the first messenger sent to polytheists, and their polytheism began with the veneration of the righteous dead. As for Abraham’s people, their polytheism began with the worship of celestial bodies. The former was earthly polytheism, and the latter heavenly polytheism. This is why the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, blocked the avenues leading to both.

---

## Page 7589

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—also said:

Allah has stated in several verses that He blessed the land of al-Shām (Greater Syria). Among them is His saying:

“And We caused the people who had been oppressed to inherit the eastern and western parts of the land We had blessed.”

And His saying:

“And We delivered him and Lot to the land We had blessed for all peoples.”

And His saying:

“Blowing at his command toward the land We had blessed. And We have knowledge of all things.”

And His saying:

“And We placed between them and the towns We had blessed other towns, clearly visible.”

The blessed towns are those of al-Shām, while the others are those of Yemen. The towns between them are those of the Hijāz and nearby regions, and they have perished.

And His saying:

“To al-Aqsā Mosque, whose surroundings We have blessed.”

---

## Page 7590

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—said:

Section

Allah Most High said:

“And remember your Lord within yourself, in humility and fear, without speaking loudly, morning and evening.”

He was thus commanded to remember Allah within himself. It may be said that this means remembering Him in his heart without using his tongue, because Allah then says:

“Without speaking loudly.”

Or it may be said—and this is more correct—that it means remembering Allah within himself with both tongue and heart. His words, “Without speaking loudly,” are like His words:

“Do not raise your voice in prayer, nor lower it too much, but seek a way between the two.”

An authentic report from ʿĀ’ishah states that she said: “This was revealed concerning supplication.” Another authentic report relates that Ibn ʿAbbās said:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to recite the Quran aloud. When the polytheists heard him, they would revile the Quran, the One who revealed it, and the one to whom it was revealed. So Allah said: ‘Do not recite the Quran so loudly that the polytheists hear it and revile it, nor so quietly that your Companions cannot hear it.’”

He thus forbade him both loud recitation and quiet utterance. Quiet utterance is remembering Him within oneself, while the loudness forbidden here is that mentioned in His words:

“Without speaking loudly.”

---

## Page 7591

For loudness means forceful projection of the voice: one speaks of a man with a booming voice or a loud-voiced man. The same applies to Ā’ishah’s statement concerning supplication, for Allah Most High says:

“Call upon your Lord humbly and secretly.”

And He says:

“When he called upon his Lord in a quiet call.”

A quiet utterance may still be audible to someone nearby; this is speaking in confidence, whereas speaking loudly is like calling out in its unqualified sense. Likewise, when his Companions raised their voices proclaiming Allah’s greatness, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“O people, be gentle with yourselves! You are not calling upon one who is deaf or absent. You are calling upon One who hears and is near. The One you call upon is nearer to each of you than the neck of his mount.”

A parallel to His words, “And remember your Lord within yourself,” is the saying of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, reporting from his Lord:

“Whoever remembers Me within himself, I remember him within Myself; and whoever remembers Me in an assembly, I remember him in an assembly better than it.”

This includes remembering Him privately with the tongue, for He contrasts it with remembrance in an assembly. It parallels His words, “Without speaking loudly.” The evidence for this is that He says, “Morning and evening.” It is well known that the remembrance of Allah prescribed morning and evening, both within prayer and outside it, involves the tongue together with the heart. Examples include the dawn and afternoon prayers, the remembrance prescribed after these two prayers, and the transmitted formulas of remembrance and supplication that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, enjoined, taught, and practised as prescribed daily and nightly devotions

---

## Page 7592

at the two ends of the day, morning and evening. This may also include remembering Allah with the heart alone. However, remembrance within oneself may be complete or incomplete: complete remembrance involves the tongue together with the heart, whereas incomplete remembrance involves the heart alone.

Similar to this are His words, Most High:

“And they say to themselves, ‘Why does Allah not punish us for what we say?’”

Those who hold that speech, without qualification, means inner speech have cited this verse as evidence. Our fellow scholars and others have given two answers to this argument. First, they spoke quietly with their tongues. Second, He qualified their saying as being “to themselves”; when speech is qualified in this way, its meaning differs from that of speech without qualification. This resembles the saying of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Allah has pardoned my community for what passes through their minds, so long as they neither speak of it nor act upon it.”

His words, “what passes through their minds, so long as they do not speak of it,” demonstrate that inner speech is not speech in the unqualified sense and does not involve the tongue.

Some of these people have also appealed to His words:

“Whether you conceal your speech or proclaim it aloud, He surely knows what is within the hearts.”

They have taken concealed speech to mean speech within the heart rather than with the tongue, on account of His words:

“He surely knows what is within the hearts.”

This is a very weak argument, because

---

## Page 7593

His words:

“Whether you conceal your speech or proclaim it aloud”

demonstrate that speech is sometimes concealed and sometimes proclaimed aloud. This applies only to speech composed of audible letters.

His subsequent words:

“He surely knows what is within the hearts”

draw attention to the greater by mentioning the lesser: if He knows what is within the hearts, then He certainly knows speech that is concealed or proclaimed aloud.

Similar to this are His words:

“It is all the same whether any of you conceals his speech or proclaims it aloud, and whether he hides by night or moves about openly by day.”

## Page 7594

Surah al-Anfāl

Shaykh al-Islām said:

Section

The Exalted said concerning the events at Badr:

“When you sought your Lord’s help, He answered you: ‘I will reinforce you with a thousand angels, following one another.’”

“Allah made this only as good news, and so that your hearts might be reassured by it.”

He promised them reinforcement with a thousand angels unconditionally, and stated that He made this reinforcement good news, without qualification. Concerning the events at Uhud, He said:

“When you said to the believers, ‘Is it not enough for you that your Lord should reinforce you with three thousand angels sent down?’”

“Yes indeed! If you remain steadfast and mindful of Allah, and the enemy comes upon you suddenly, your Lord will reinforce you with five thousand angels bearing distinguishing marks.”

There are, I believe, two views on this. The first is that it relates to Uhud, in view of His subsequent words:

“So that He might cut off a section of those who disbelieved”—to the end of the verse—

and because it is a conditional promise. And His words in that account:

“And not

---

## Page 7595

for any purpose but to give you good news and reassure your hearts by it did Allah make it”—imply that this good news was specifically for them. In the account of Badr, however, the good news is general. This thus serves as a kind of evidence for the report that the thousand angels of Badr remain among the Muslim community. For He left both the reinforcement and the good news unrestricted, and placed *bihi* (through it) before *lakum* (for you), emphasizing the thousand angels. At Uhud, however, the emphasis was on them, provided they remained steadfast; but that condition was not fulfilled.

---

## Page 7596

He—may Allah have mercy on him—said:

Section

There are three interpretations of His words:

“You did not kill them,” and the remainder of the verse.

The first is that this rests on the view that an act generated by another act is not a human act, but an act of Allah. Killing is the extinguishing of life, which is a generated effect. Someone who denies generated causation may hold this view. It is weak, however, because He also negated the throwing, which is a directly performed act. Moreover, He said:

“Kill the polytheists wherever you find them.”

And He said:

“Whoever kills a believer intentionally.”

Thus, He did attribute killing to human beings. Furthermore, killing denotes an act capable of extinguishing life, not the departure of life itself, unlike causing death.

The second is that this rests on the doctrine that Allah creates acts. Many Sufis may hold this view, and I think it is reported from al-Junayd: attribution of the act is denied to the servant in view of the underlying reality, since Allah is the Creator of every maker and what he makes. This interpretation is weak for two reasons.

The first is that, even though we affirm that the act is created, the servant is not denied attribution of it; rather, it is attributed

---

## Page 7597

to him as well. Thus, one cannot say, “You did not believe, pray, fast, affirm the truth, or possess knowledge,” for this would be obstinate denial: at the very least, he is characterized by these acts, and that is established. Moreover, this negation occurs with none of the commanded acts except the killing and throwing at Badr. Had it been based on Allah’s creation of all human acts, it would not have been confined to Badr.

The third interpretation is that Allah, glorified be He, disrupted the ordinary course of events in this instance: the polytheists’ heads would fly off at a mere gesture, before weapons reached them, and a palm frond would become a sword used for killing. Likewise, the Messenger of Allah’s throw—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—struck those whom he had no power to reach. The killing and the impact of the throw thus exceeded their ordinary capabilities, so attribution of these effects was denied to them because they lacked the power to produce them. This is the soundest interpretation, and it properly reconciles the negation with the affirmation:

“You did not throw”—that is, you did not hit the target—“when you threw”—that is, when you cast—“but Allah threw”—that is, He hit the target.

The same applies to every act Allah brings about beyond ordinary capabilities through a slight cause, such as causing water to gush forth and other extraordinary occurrences, or things beyond the power of the agent. This is clear: the verse provides no evidence either for compulsion or for denying generated causation.

---

## Page 7598

He—may Allah have mercy on him—said:

Section

On the words of the Most High:

“Allah would not punish them while you are among them, nor would Allah punish them while they seek forgiveness.”

This verse is discussed from two perspectives: first, the seeking of forgiveness that averts punishment; and second, the punishment averted by seeking forgiveness.

As for the first: punishment is only inflicted for sins, and seeking forgiveness brings about the forgiveness of sins, which are the cause of punishment. Thus, punishment is averted, as the Most High says:

“Alif Lām Rā. A Book whose verses have been perfected and then explained in detail, from One who is All-Wise, All-Aware: that you worship none but Allah. Indeed, I am a warner and a bearer of good tidings to you from Him. And seek your Lord’s forgiveness, then repent to Him; He will grant you good enjoyment for an appointed term and bestow His bounty upon everyone of merit.”

He, glorified be He, thus explains that if they do this, they will be granted good enjoyment for an appointed term. Then, if they possess merit, they will be granted bounty.

---

## Page 7599

The Most High says, relating the words of Noah:

“My people, I am a clear warner to you: worship Allah, fear Him and obey me. He will forgive you of your sins and grant you respite until an appointed term.”

The passage continues to His words:

“Seek your Lord’s forgiveness, for He is ever All-Forgiving. He will send down abundant rain upon you,” and so on.

The Most High also says:

“My people, seek your Lord’s forgiveness, then repent to Him. He will send down abundant rain upon you and add strength to your strength.”

This is because the Most High has said:

“Whatever calamity befalls you is because of what your own hands have earned, and He pardons much.”

The Most High says:

“Those of you who turned away on the day the two armies met—it was Satan who caused them to slip because of some of what they had earned.”

The Most High says:

“When a calamity befell you, although you had inflicted twice as much, did you say, ‘How has this happened?’ Say, ‘It is from yourselves.’”

The Most High says:

“And if a misfortune befalls them because of what their own hands have sent forth.”

The Most High says:

“Whatever good befalls you is from Allah, and whatever misfortune befalls you is from yourself.”

As for the punishment averted, it encompasses both punishment sent from heaven and that inflicted by human beings. Allah has called all of this punishment, as the Most High says concerning the second kind:

“And when We saved you from Pharaoh’s people, who subjected you to terrible torment, slaughtering your sons and sparing your women.”

The Most High says:

“Fight them: Allah will punish them at your hands, disgrace them and grant you victory over them.”

Likewise:

“Say, ‘Are you waiting for anything to befall us other than one of the two best outcomes? We, however, are waiting for Allah to afflict you with punishment from Himself or at our hands.’”

The implied meaning is: with punishment from Himself or with punishment at our hands, as the Most High says:

“Fight them: Allah will punish them at your hands.”

---

## Page 7600

On this reading, then, the punishment is inflicted through human action. It might also be said that the implied wording is:

“We, however, are waiting for Allah to afflict you with punishment from Himself,”

or to cause something to befall you at our hands. The first reading, however, is more sound, because saying that something befalls people at the believers’ hands does not indicate that it is harmful. One may say, “He brought him good,” or, “He brought him harm.” The Most High says:

“If He intends good for you, none can repel His bounty. He bestows it upon whomever He wills of His servants.”

The Most High also says:

“Then you see the rain coming forth from within it. When He causes it to fall upon whomever He wills of His servants, they rejoice.”

The Most High says:

“Thus We established Joseph in the land, to settle wherever he wished. We bestow Our mercy upon whomever We will.”

Moreover, if the word isābah (causing something to befall someone) inherently meant inflicting harm, it would have sufficed for Him to say:

“that Allah should cause something to befall you.”

The Most High also says:

“If good befalls them, they say, ‘This is from Allah.’ But if misfortune befalls them, they say, ‘This is from you.’ Say, ‘All is from Allah.’ What is the matter with these people that they can hardly understand what they are told?”

“Whatever good befalls you is from Allah, and whatever misfortune befalls you is from yourself.”

Another example is the saying of the Most High:

“The woman and the man who commit unlawful sexual intercourse—flog each of them a hundred lashes,”

through to His words:

“And let a group of the believers witness their punishment.”

Likewise, the Most High says:

“If they commit sexual immorality, they shall receive half the punishment prescribed for free women.”

---

## Page 7601

Another example is what is said of Bilāl and others like him: “They were among those tortured for Allah’s sake.” It is also said that Abū Bakr bought seven of those tortured for Allah’s sake. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Travel is a kind of torment.”

Since this is so, consider the statement of the Most High:

“Say, ‘He is able to send punishment upon you from above you or from beneath your feet, or to divide you into factions and make you taste one another’s violence.’”

Together with this is the report established in the two Sahīh collections from Jābir concerning the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“When the words, ‘Say, “He is able to send punishment upon you from above you,”’ were revealed, he said, ‘I seek refuge in Your Face.’ At the words, ‘or from beneath your feet,’ he said, ‘I seek refuge in Your Face.’ At the words, ‘or to divide you into factions and make you taste one another’s violence,’ he said, ‘These two are less severe.’”

This implies that our being divided into factions and made to taste one another’s violence is among the punishments averted by seeking forgiveness. As He says:

“Beware of a trial that will not afflict only the wrongdoers among you.”

Such trials are averted only by seeking forgiveness for sins and doing righteous deeds. As for the statement of the Most High:

“If you do not go forth, He will punish you with a painful punishment and replace you with another people,”

the punishment may come from Him, or it may come at the hands of people. When people abandon jihad in Allah’s cause, He may try them by sowing enmity among them until strife breaks out between them, as indeed happens. For when people occupy themselves with jihad in Allah’s cause, Allah unites their hearts, brings them together, and directs their might against Allah’s enemy and theirs.

---

## Page 7602

and when they do not go forth in Allah’s cause, Allah punishes them by dividing them into factions and making them taste one another’s violence.

The same applies to His words:

“We will surely make them taste the lesser punishment before the greater punishment, so that they may return.”

The lesser punishment includes what is inflicted at the hands of people, just as some of the punishment Allah threatened the polytheists with has been interpreted as the Battle of Badr.

## Page 7603

Surah al-Tawbah

He also said:

Allah’s words:

“Do not take your fathers and your brothers as allies if they prefer disbelief over faith”

may be cited as evidence that a child is deemed a believer by virtue of his father’s faith. For He did not mention children preferring disbelief over faith, although they would have been more deserving of mention. This is only because the ruling concerning a child differs from that concerning a father or brother.

This is the distinction between someone subject to legal restrictions because of minority or insanity and someone with independent standing. Similarly, Sufyān ibn ʿUyaynah and others cited His words:

“Nor is there any blame upon you for eating from your own houses or your fathers’ houses”

as evidence that a child’s house is included in “your own houses,” because he and his property belong to his father.

His words:

“Why do you not fight in Allah’s cause and for the oppressed men, women, and children who say, ‘Our Lord, take us out of this town whose people are oppressors’?”

may also be cited as evidence that a child’s acceptance of Islam is valid. For He included children among those who speak as people seeking to emigrate, and seeking to emigrate is only valid after faith. If a child’s declaration in this matter is recognized, his standing in it is independent rather than derivative. This contrasts with a child who has no discernment, whose status is derivative and whose own declaration carries no weight.

---

## Page 7604

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was asked:

Regarding the Most High’s words, “The Jews say, ‘Ezra is the son of Allah’”: did all of them say this, or only some of them? And regarding the Prophet’s statement (may Allah bless him and grant him peace), “The Jews will be brought on the Day of Resurrection and asked, ‘What did you worship?’ They will say, ‘Ezra’”—the hadith continues—does this address encompass all of them or not?

He replied:

Praise be to Allah.

“The Jews” refers to Jews in a generic sense, as in the Most High’s words:

“Those to whom people said, ‘The people have gathered against you.’”

He did not say “all the people,” nor did He say, “All the people have gathered against you.” Rather, the expression is used generically. This is like saying, “Such-and-such a group does such-and-such,” or “The people of so-and-so do such-and-such.” If some of them make a statement while the rest remain silent and do not denounce it, they share in the sin of that statement.

And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 7605

He also said, commenting on His words:

“Say, ‘Was it Allah, His signs, and His Messenger that you were mocking?’”

This indicates that mocking Allah is disbelief and that mocking the Messenger is disbelief. The reasoning is that mocking Allah alone is necessarily disbelief, so mentioning His signs and His Messenger was not a condition for this ruling. Thus, mocking the Messenger is itself disbelief; otherwise, mentioning him would serve no purpose. The same applies to His signs.

Moreover, mocking these things is inseparably connected. Those who are astray make light of worshipping Allah alone, while holding supplication to others among the dead in great esteem. When commanded to worship Allah alone and forbidden to associate partners with Him, they make light of it, as the Most High says:

“When they see you, they do nothing but ridicule you”—and the rest of the verse.

They mocked the Messenger—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—when he forbade them to associate partners with Allah. The polytheists have always reviled the prophets and accused them of foolishness, misguidance, and madness whenever they called them to worship Allah alone, because of the immense polytheism within their souls. Likewise, you find that those who resemble them ridicule anyone they see calling people to worship Allah alone, because of their own polytheism. Allah Most High says:

“And among the people

---

## Page 7606

are those who take others as equals to Allah, loving them as they love Allah.” Whoever loves a created being as he loves Allah is a polytheist. A distinction must be made between loving for Allah’s sake and loving others as rivals to Allah.

You find that those who have made graves into idols ridicule what belongs to worshipping Allah alone, while venerating those they have taken as intercessors besides Allah. One of them will knowingly swear a false oath, yet not dare swear falsely by his shaykh. Among many different groups, you find people who believe that seeking their shaykh’s aid, whether at his grave or elsewhere, benefits them more than supplicating to Allah in the mosque before dawn. They ridicule anyone who abandons their way to worship Allah alone. Many of them ruin mosques while building up shrines. Is this anything but their making light of Allah, His signs, and His Messenger, while exalting polytheism? When both have endowments, they hold the endowment devoted to polytheism in greater esteem, imitating the Arab polytheists whom Allah mentions in His words:

“And they assign to Allah a share of the crops and livestock He has created”—and the rest of the verse.

They thus give preference to what is dedicated to others besides Allah over what is dedicated to Allah, saying, “Allah is free of need, while our gods are poor.” When one of these people visits the grave he venerates, he weeps there and humbles himself

---

## Page 7607

and implores with a fervor he never experiences in Friday prayer, the five daily prayers, or night prayer. Is this anything but the condition of polytheists, rather than those who worship Allah alone? Likewise, when one of them hears poetry recited, he experiences a humility and attentiveness that he never experiences when hearing the Quranic verses. Indeed, they find these verses burdensome and ridicule both them and those who recite them. Thus they are among those to whom His words apply most fully:

“Say, ‘Was it Allah, His verses, and His Messenger that you were mocking?’”

Among those who consider supplicating to the dead better than supplicating to Allah are people who relate that a certain disciple sought Allah’s help, but He did not help him; then he sought his shaykh’s help, and the shaykh helped him. They also relate that a certain captive called upon Allah, but He did not free him; then he called upon one of the dead, who came to him and brought him out to the lands of Islam.

Another said, “So-and-so’s grave is the tried and tested antidote.” Some of them, when hardship befalls them, call upon none but their shaykh, constantly repeating his name as a child constantly calls for his mother.

Yet Allah Most High has said to those who worship Him alone:

“When you have completed your rites, remember Allah as you remember your fathers, or with even greater remembrance.”

And Shu‘ayb said:

“O my people, is my clan more respected by you than Allah?”

And Allah Most High said:

“Indeed, they fear you in their hearts more than they fear Allah.”

---

## Page 7608

Shaykh al-Islām was asked:

What is the meaning of Allah Most High’s words,

“Allah has certainly turned in mercy to the Prophet, the Emigrants, and the Helpers,”

and the remainder of the verse? Repentance concerns something a servant has done, whereas the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is protected from major and minor sins.

Shaykh al-Islām Ibn Taymiyyah replied:

Praise be to Allah. The prophets, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon them, are protected from being allowed to persist in sins, whether major or minor. Through the repentance that Allah has related concerning them, He raises their ranks and increases their good deeds. Indeed, Allah loves those who repent and loves those who purify themselves. Repentance is not a deficiency; rather, it is among the highest forms of perfection. It is obligatory upon all people, as Allah Most High says:

“And man undertook it. Indeed, he was unjust and ignorant.”

“So that Allah may punish the hypocrites, men and women, and the polytheists, men and women, and turn in mercy to the believers, men and women.”

Thus, the ultimate aim of every believer is repentance. Repentance, moreover, takes different forms, as expressed in the saying: “The good deeds of the righteous are the sins of those brought near to Allah.”

Allah Most High has told us of repentance and seeking forgiveness among the prophets generally—Adam, Noah, Abraham, Moses, and others. Adam said:

---

## Page 7609

“Our Lord, we have wronged ourselves. If You do not forgive us and have mercy upon us, we will surely be among the losers.”

Noah said:

“My Lord, I seek refuge in You from asking You for that of which I have no knowledge. Unless You forgive me and have mercy upon me, I will be among the losers.”

Allah’s close friend said:

“Our Lord, forgive me, my parents, and the believers on the Day the reckoning takes place.”

He and Ishmael said:

“Our Lord, make us both submissive to You, and from our descendants a community submissive to You. Show us our rites of worship and accept our repentance. You are indeed the One who accepts repentance, the Most Merciful.”

Moses said:

“You are our Protector, so forgive us and have mercy upon us. You are the best of those who forgive.”

“And decree good for us in this world and in the Hereafter. Indeed, we have turned to You.”

Allah Most High said:

“When he regained consciousness, he said, ‘Glory be to You! I repent to You, and I am the first of the believers.’”

Allah, glorified be He, has mentioned the repentance of David, Solomon, and other prophets. Allah Most High “loves those who repent and loves those who purify themselves.”

Among the last revelations Allah sent down to His Prophet were these words:

“When Allah’s help comes and the conquest, and you see people entering Allah’s religion in multitudes, then glorify your Lord with praise and seek His forgiveness. Indeed, He is ever accepting of repentance.”

It is related in both Sahih collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would say at the beginning of the prayer:

“O Allah, distance me from my sins as You have distanced the east from the west. O Allah, cleanse me of sins as a white garment is cleansed of dirt. O Allah, wash away my sins with snow, hail, and cold water.”

It is also related in the Sahih that he would say in the opening supplication:

“O Allah, You are the King; there is no god but You

---

## Page 7610

; You are my Lord, and I am Your servant. I have wronged myself and acknowledged my sin, so forgive me all my sins, for none forgives sins but You.”

It is also related in the Sahih that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would say:

“O Allah, forgive me all my sins, small and great, public and secret, first and last.”

It is related in both Sahih collections that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would say:

“O Allah, forgive me my sins, my ignorance, my excesses in my affairs, and whatever You know better than I do. O Allah, forgive me what I have done in jest and in earnest, unintentionally and deliberately—all of these are found in me. O Allah, forgive me what I have done before and what I have done later, what I have concealed and what I have done openly, my excesses, and whatever You know better than I do. You are the One who brings forward and the One who puts back. There is no god but You.”

There are many such examples in the Quran and Sunnah. Allah Most High has said:

“And seek forgiveness for your sin and for the believing men and believing women.”

The believers’ repentance and seeking forgiveness are among their greatest good deeds, their highest acts of obedience, and their noblest acts of worship, through which they attain the greatest reward and are spared whatever punishment these acts avert.

If someone asks, “What need do the prophets have of worship and obedience?” he is ignorant, for they attained what they attained only through their worship and obedience. How, then, can it be said that they have no need of these? Repentance and seeking forgiveness are the finest of their acts of worship and obedience.

And if someone says, “But repentance can only be for a sin, and the same applies to seeking forgiveness,”

---

## Page 7611

he should be told:

The sin that harms a person is one for which he has not repented. As for a sin from which he has repented, he may be better after repenting than he was before committing it. As one of the early generations said, “David was in a better state after repentance than before his sin.” This holds even when the repentance is from disbelief and major sins. The foremost pioneers among the Emigrants and the Helpers are the best of creation after the prophets, and they attained this standing only through repenting of their former disbelief and sins. What preceded their repentance was neither a deficiency nor a blemish upon them. Rather, when they repented of it and did righteous deeds, they became greater in faith and stronger in worship and obedience than those who came after them and did not know the pre-Islamic age of ignorance as they had known it.

For this reason, ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb said:

“The bonds of Islam will be undone one by one when people who have never known the pre-Islamic age of ignorance grow up within Islam.”

Allah Most High has said:

“And those who do not invoke another god besides Allah, nor take a life Allah has made sacred except with just cause, nor commit unlawful sexual intercourse. Whoever does these things will incur a penalty.

The punishment will be doubled for him on the Day of Resurrection, and he will remain in it in humiliation,

except those who repent, believe, and do righteous deeds. For them Allah will replace their evil deeds with good deeds. And Allah is ever Forgiving, Merciful.”

It is authentically reported in the Sahih from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Allah will call His servant to account on the Day of Resurrection. He will show him his minor sins and conceal his major sins from him, saying, ‘On such-and-such a day, did you do such-and-such?’ He will reply, ‘Yes, my Lord,’ while he is apprehensive

---

## Page 7612

that his major sins might be exposed. Then He will say, ‘I have forgiven them for you and replaced each evil deed with a good deed.’ At that point he will say, ‘My Lord, I have other evil deeds that I do not yet see.’”

When a believing servant repents and Allah replaces his evil deeds with good deeds, those evil deeds that once harmed him become, through his repentance, good deeds by which Allah benefits him. After repentance, his sins no longer harm him; rather, repenting of them is among the most beneficial things for him. What counts is the perfection of the outcome, not the deficiency of the beginning. Thus, someone who forgets the Quran and then memorizes it, becoming better than he was when he first memorized it, has not been harmed by forgetting. Likewise, someone who falls ill and then recovers and grows strong has not been harmed by that passing illness.

Allah Most High tests His believing servant with things of which he repents, so that through this he may attain a degree of perfection in servitude, humble entreaty, humility before Allah, turning to Him, utmost vigilance in the future, and diligence in worship that he would not have attained without repentance. This is like someone who experiences hunger, thirst, illness, poverty, and fear, then experiences fullness, quenched thirst, health, wealth, and security. He comes to love these blessings, appreciate their sweetness, take pleasure in them, desire them, thank Allah for bestowing them upon him, and guard against returning to his former condition in ways he would not have done without that experience. This has been discussed at length elsewhere.

It should be understood that repentance is indispensable for every believer. Only through it can anyone attain perfection, achieve complete nearness to Allah, and be freed of everything he dislikes.

---

## Page 7613

Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the most perfect of all creation and the most honoured by Allah. He surpasses all creation in every form of obedience: he is the foremost of those who love Allah, trust in Him, worship Him, know Him, and repent to Him. His repentance is more perfect than anyone else’s; hence Allah has forgiven his past and future sins.

Through this forgiveness, he attained the right to intercede on the Day of Resurrection, as established in the authentic report:

“On the Day of Resurrection, people will seek intercession from Adam, but he will say, ‘I was forbidden to eat from the tree, yet I ate from it. Myself! Myself! Myself!’ They will seek it from Noah, but he will say, ‘I invoked a supplication against the people of the earth which I had not been commanded to make. Myself! Myself! Myself!’ They will seek it from Abraham, Allah’s close friend, then from Moses, then from the Messiah, who will say, ‘Go to Muhammad, a servant whose past and future sins Allah has forgiven.’

“He said, ‘They will come to me, and I will set out. When I see my Lord, I will fall down before Him in prostration and praise Him with words of praise that He will inspire me to utter, which I cannot offer now. He will say, “O Muhammad, raise your head.
