They also maintained: “Whoever deserves punishment does not deserve reward.”

---

## Page 7852

For this reason, the Sunnah teaches that someone upon whom the prescribed punishment or other penalties have been carried out, without the believers being overcome by pity for him, should nevertheless be shown mercy in another respect: he should be treated kindly and supplicated for. This merciful aspect predominates in the Sharia, just as it predominates in the attributes of the Lord, glorified is He. Thus, the two Sahīh collections relate:

“Allah wrote a decree, which is kept with Him above the Throne: ‘My mercy prevails over My wrath.’” Another narration reads: “preceded My wrath.”

He says:

“Tell My servants that I am indeed the All-Forgiving, the Most Merciful, and that My punishment is the painful punishment.”

He also says:

“Know that Allah is severe in punishment, and that Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”

He thus made mercy an attribute of His, expressed in His most beautiful names. As for torment and punishment, He made them things He brings about, without including them in His names.

This also encompasses Allah’s command to be stern towards the disbelievers and hypocrites. He, Most High, says:

“O Prophet, strive against the disbelievers and the hypocrites, and be stern with them.”

He also says:

“Do not take My enemies and yours as allies, offering them affection.”

The verses continue until His words in the account of Abraham:

“Until you believe in Allah alone.”

The same applies to the end of Sūrat al-Mujādilah. It is authentically reported in Sahīh Muslim, from al-Hasan, from Hittān ibn ‘Abd Allāh, from ‘Ubādah ibn al-Sāmit, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Take this ruling from me: Allah has now provided a way for them. For a virgin with a virgin: one hundred lashes and exile for a year; for a previously married person with a previously married person: one hundred lashes and stoning.”

The two Sahīh collections also relate, on the authority of Abū Hurayrah and Zayd ibn Khālid, that he

---

## Page 7853

—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—“was approached by two men with a dispute. One of them said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, judge between us according to the Book of Allah.’ The other, who was more knowledgeable than him, said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, judge between us according to the Book of Allah, and allow me to speak. My son was a hired worker for this man and committed adultery with his wife. I paid him a ransom of one hundred sheep and a slave girl. I then asked the people of knowledge, who said, “Your son must receive one hundred lashes and be exiled for a year.”’

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘I shall certainly judge between you according to the Book of Allah. The hundred sheep and the slave girl must be returned to you, and your son must receive one hundred lashes and be exiled for a year. Unays, go to this man’s wife in the morning; if she confesses, stone her.’ She confessed, and he stoned her.”

This woman was among those whom the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had stoned. He also stoned the two Jews at the door of his mosque, Māʿiz ibn Mālik, the Ghāmidī woman, and others besides them.

This hadith accords with the verse’s explanation of the way Allah provided for them: one hundred lashes and a year’s exile for a virgin, and stoning for a previously married person. However, this hadith specifies flogging and exile for a male virgin, whereas the verse mentions confinement in houses specifically for women.

Some jurists of Iraq do not consider exile obligatory in addition to the prescribed punishment, while others distinguish between men and women. Likewise, most of them do not consider one hundred lashes obligatory in addition to stoning, while some require both, as ʿAlī did with Shurāhah al-Hamdāniyyah: he flogged her and then stoned her, saying:

“I flogged her according to the Book of Allah and stoned her according to the Sunnah of His Prophet.”

---

## Page 7854

Reported by al-Bukhārī. Two views are reported from Ahmad on this matter.

In Sūrat al-Nisāʾ, Allah, glorified be He, mentioned the punishment specific to women: confinement in houses until death or until He provided a way for them. He then mentioned what applies to both sexes, saying:

“If two among you commit it, punish them both.”

Punishment here applies to both sexes, whereas confinement is specific to women. Women were thus to be punished and confined, unlike men, for whom He did not command confinement. This is because a woman must be protected and safeguarded in ways not required for a man. For this reason, she is specifically required to observe seclusion, refrain from displaying her adornment, and avoid ostentatious display. She must therefore remain covered by clothing and sheltered within houses to an extent not required of a man, because women’s appearing in public is a cause of temptation, and men are responsible for their care.

His words,

“Call four witnesses from among yourselves against them,”

indicate two things: that the required number of witnesses to this indecency is four, and that those who testify to it against our women must be from among us. Thus, the testimony of unbelievers against Muslims is not admissible. There is no disagreement concerning this. The disagreement concerns only the admissibility of unbelievers’ testimony against one another. Two views are reported from Ahmad. The better-known view from him and among his followers is that it is inadmissible, as is the position of Mālik and al-Shāfiʿī. The second reported view is that it is admissible. This was preferred by Abū al-Khattāb, one of Ahmad’s followers, and it is the position of Abū Hanīfah. It accords more closely with the Book and the Sunnah. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “The testimony of the followers of one religion against the followers of another is not admissible, except

---

## Page 7855

my community, whose testimony against others is admissible.” He did not rule out the admissibility of testimony among followers of the same religion. Rather, his words imply that their testimony against one another is admissible. They also make clear that believers’ testimony against others is accepted, as Allah Most High says:

“Thus We have made you a just community, so that you may be witnesses over mankind.”

A similar statement occurs at the end of Sūrat al-Hajj. It is also established in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, on the authority of Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Noah will be summoned on the Day of Resurrection and asked, ‘Did you convey the message?’ He will reply, ‘Yes.’ His people will then be summoned and asked, ‘Did he convey the message to you?’ They will reply, ‘No bearer of good tidings or warner came to us.’ Noah will then be asked, ‘Who will testify for you?’ He will reply, ‘Muhammad and his community.’ You will then be brought forward and testify that he conveyed the message.”

Likewise, the Sahīhs of al-Bukhārī and Muslim contain the hadith of Anas concerning their testimony about the two funeral processions: they spoke well of one deceased person and ill of the other. The Prophet said:

“You are Allah’s witnesses on His earth.”

The hadith continues.

For this reason, since the people of the Sunnah and the community have kept Islam pure, without mixing it with anything else, their testimony against all the other groups within the Muslim community is accepted. This is unlike the people of innovation and vain desires, such as the Khawārij and the Rāfidah: the enmity and injustice among them prevent them from fully embodying this quality that Allah has granted the people of the Sunnah. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said of them:

“The upright members of each succeeding generation will bear this knowledge, removing from it the distortions of extremists, the fabrications of those who promote falsehood, and the interpretations of the ignorant.”

---

## Page 7856

Those who allow protected non-Muslims to testify against one another have also cited this verse from Sūrat al-Mā’idah, in which Allah says:

“O you who believe, when death approaches any of you, let two just men from among you, or two others from outside your community, bear witness when you make your bequest.”

And so the verse continues. Those scholars of Kūfah who adopted the apparent meaning of this verse then said: “This verse establishes the admissibility of protected non-Muslims’ testimony against Muslims. It therefore indicates, by an argument of greater reason, that their testimony against one another is admissible. Moreover, abrogating its apparent meaning does not entail abrogating its implied meaning and indication.”

The evidence supplied by this verse in support of the explicit rulings of Imam Ahmad and other hadith authorities—who follow the early generations in applying this verse and the hadiths that accord with it—is more cogent and compelling. His position is that protected non-Muslims’ testimony against Muslims is admissible concerning bequests made while travelling, because this is a situation of necessity. If their testimony concerning others is admissible, then its admissibility against one another is all the more evident.

Thus, necessity permits forms of testimony that would otherwise be inadmissible. For example, women’s testimony is accepted concerning matters that men cannot observe. Ahmad even expressly ruled that their testimony is admissible in cases involving prescribed punishments arising at gatherings exclusive to women, such as bathhouses, weddings, and the like. There is therefore even greater reason to accept the testimony of unbelievers against one another when Muslims do not mingle with them, if we adjudicate between them.

Allah has commanded us to judge between them. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stoned the two Jewish adulterers without hearing a confession from either of them or the testimony of any Muslim against them. Had their testimony against one another not been admissible, that would not have been permissible. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 7857

There is also disagreement over their administering one another’s property. May an unbeliever who is upright according to his own religion administer the property of his unbelieving child? There are two opinions on this within the school of Ahmad and elsewhere. The unquestionably correct view is that they have a greater right to guardianship over one another. This was the established practice of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and of his caliphs.

Allah’s words, “Punish them both,” command the infliction of hurt in general terms, without specifying its manner, form, or extent. He simply states that both must be made to suffer. The term adhā (hurt) is frequently used in reference to speech, as in His words:

“They will not harm you beyond causing hurt.”

“Indeed, those who offend Allah and His Messenger…”

“And those who harm believing men and believing women for something they have not done…”

“Among them are those who offend the Prophet.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said: “No one is more patient than Allah with the hurtful things He hears.” There are many similar examples, which we have discussed in Kitāb al-Sārim al-Maslūl (The Unsheathed Sword). This is likewise illustrated by his words, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning someone who drinks wine: “Punish him and cause him distress.”

Allah says: “But if they repent and mend their ways, leave them alone.” Leaving them alone means ceasing to cause them distress. Thus, the sinner continues to be subjected to distress, forbidden from wrongdoing, admonished, rebuked, and addressed sternly until he repents and obeys Allah. At the very least, he is to be shunned and not addressed with kind words, just as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and the believers shunned the three who were left behind until their repentance and reform became evident.

This verse remains in force and has not been abrogated. Any man or woman who commits indecency must therefore be subjected to distress through words that deter them from sin, until

---

## Page 7858

he repents. No fixed degree or manner is prescribed for this, except that it must deter him and lead to the intended aim: his repentance and reform. Allah, Exalted is He, has made leaving him alone conditional upon these two things: repentance and reform. If they are absent, the command to leave him alone cannot apply; rather, he is to be subjected to distress.

The verse establishes the obligation to cause distress to the two among us who commit indecency, and the obligation to cease causing distress to anyone who repents and reforms. As for someone who repents by ceasing to commit indecency but has not reformed, the jurists have disagreed over whether righteous conduct is a condition for accepting repentance. There are two opinions on this within the school of Ahmad and elsewhere.

This resembles the words of Allah, Exalted is He:

“When the sacred months have passed, kill the polytheists wherever you find them,”

through to His words:

“But if they repent, establish prayer, and give zakah, then let them go their way.”

He commanded fighting them, then made letting them go conditional upon repentance and righteous deeds—namely, establishing prayer and giving zakah. Nevertheless, once they pronounce the two testimonies of faith, it becomes obligatory to desist from attacking them. If they then pray and give zakah, well and good; otherwise, they are subsequently punished for failing to do so. For it is prescribed to refrain from causing distress to someone who has begun to repent, while the matter remains contingent upon his completing that repentance.

Similarly, it is prescribed to refrain from causing distress to someone who repents of indecency pending his reform. If he reforms, it becomes obligatory to cease causing him distress. If he does not reform, refraining from causing him distress is not obligatory; rather, causing him distress is permissible or obligatory.

This verse is among those cited as evidence for discretionary punishment through causing distress. Although distress is

---

## Page 7859

frequently spoken of in connection with someone who commits indecency, it is not confined to that context. Thus, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said to someone who spat in the direction of prayer:

“You have caused distress to Allah and His Messenger.”

Likewise, he said concerning his daughter Fātimah:

“What troubles her troubles me, and what causes her distress causes me distress.”

He also said concerning those who ate garlic and onions:

“The angels are distressed by the same things that distress the children of Adam.”

He said to someone carrying arrows:

“Hold them by their points so that you do not hurt any Muslim.”

Allah, Exalted is He, has said:

“When you have eaten, disperse, without lingering for conversation. Indeed, that was causing the Prophet distress.”

Regarding His words, Exalted is He, “But if they both repent and reform”: does repentance include acknowledging one’s sin? If the sin is established by a person’s confession, but he denies having confessed and accuses those who witnessed his confession of lying—or if it is established through the testimony of witnesses—is he regarded as repentant in such circumstances? There is disagreement on this. Imam Ahmad stated that there is no repentance for someone who denies his sin; rather, repentance is for someone who acknowledges it and repents. He cited as evidence the account of ʿAlī ibn Abī Tālib, before whom a group of people were brought after testimony had been given accusing them of heresy. Some acknowledged it and repented, and he accepted their repentance. Others denied it, and he put them to death.

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said to ʿĀʾishah:

“If you have committed a sin, then seek Allah’s forgiveness and repent to Him. For when a servant acknowledges his sin and then repents, Allah accepts his repentance.”

Narrated by al-Bukhārī.

Whoever sins secretly should repent secretly; he is not required to disclose his sin. As stated in the hadith:

“Whoever falls into any of these foul sins should avail himself of Allah’s concealment

---

## Page 7860

, for whoever reveals his sin to us, we shall enforce Allah’s Book against him.” In the Sahīh:

“All of my community will be pardoned except those who publicize their sins. One form of publicizing sin is for a man to spend the night having committed a sin that Allah has concealed, then himself remove Allah’s concealment.”

When a servant’s sin becomes public, his repentance must likewise become public. Repentance is not evident while he denies the sin, for the one who denies it claims that he has not sinned. Thus, the early generations applied this to anyone who openly manifested religious innovation or wickedness: the former displays the condition of those who have gone astray, and the latter that of those who have incurred wrath. Such censure includes barring him—where one has the power—from leadership, adjudication, issuing fatwas, transmitting reports, and giving testimony. Where one lacks that power, one should do whatever is within one’s ability.

As for His words:

“The two among you who commit it: punish them both.”

He commanded that both be punished, without making this conditional upon calling four witnesses, as He did concerning the women and their confinement to their homes. Nor did He command it here as He did there. This is not a case of interpreting an unrestricted text in light of a restricted one, since that requires the ruling to be the same, as in the case of freeing a slave. When the rulings share a general category but differ in their specific form, scholars disagree about this. Examples include mentioning the hands without qualification in dry ablution, while specifying washing up to the elbows in ablution with water; and mentioning sixty needy people without qualification when prescribing feeding, while stipulating that a slave to be freed must be a believer—even though both are acts of worship involving wealth, intended to benefit people.

The Muslims among the Companions and their successors did not interpret the unrestricted wording in light of the restricted wording in His statement:

“And the mothers of your wives, and your stepdaughters under your care, born of your wives

---

## Page 7861

with whom you have consummated marriage”—and the rest of the verse—and in His words, Exalted is He:

“Do not marry women whom your fathers married, except what has already passed.”

The Companions, the Successors, and all the other imams of the religion said that the condition applies only to stepdaughters. They said: “Leave unrestricted what Allah has left unrestricted.” What is left unqualified is unrestricted, whereas what is subject to a condition is delimited and restricted. Thus, wives’ mothers and the wives of fathers and sons become forbidden in marriage through the marriage contract alone, whereas stepdaughters become forbidden only when marriage with their mothers has been consummated. However, scholars disagreed over whether death has the same effect as consummation; there are two opinions within Ahmad’s school.

This is because the rulings differ, and the restriction does not apply identically to the persons concerned. Prohibiting one category is not the same as prohibiting another, different category. Similarly, blood, carrion, and swine’s flesh belong to different categories: restricting the prohibition of blood to blood that has been shed does not entail applying that same restriction to carrion and swine. Here, the restriction is that marriage with the stepdaughter’s mother must have been consummated. Consummation with the mother has no exact counterpart in the cases of fathers’ and sons’ wives or a wife’s mother: in the case of a wife, consummation is with the woman herself, whereas in the case of a wife’s mother, it is with her daughter.

Likewise, the Muslims did not interpret unrestricted wording in light of restricted wording concerning the required number of witnesses. Rather, when Allah mentioned two men, or one man and two women, in the verse on debt, and two men for taking back a divorced wife, they retained each prescription as it stood. This is because the basis of the ruling differs—property in one case and marital relations in the other—and this difference affects the required number of witnesses.

Similarly, four witnesses are required in connection with enforcing the prescribed punishment for sexual immorality and with accusations of it; the contracting of oaths and marriages is not to be judged by analogy with that. He mentioned three rulings concerning the prescribed punishment for accusations of sexual immorality:

---

## Page 7862

eighty lashes, the permanent rejection of their testimony, and their being deemed defiantly disobedient:

“Except for those who repent thereafter and reform; indeed, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”

Repentance does not waive the lashes if the person accused demands them, but it unquestionably removes the status of defiant disobedience. Does it also remove the bar to accepting their testimony? Most scholars said that it does.

If a person is widely reputed among people to have committed sexual immorality, he is not stoned on that account. This is established by the authentic report from Ibn ‘Abbās: when he related the account of the spouses’ mutual oaths and the Prophet’s words, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“If she gives birth to a child resembling her husband, he has falsely accused her; but if she gives birth to a child resembling the man with whom he accused her of committing adultery, his accusation is true.”

She then gave birth to a child matching the unwelcome description, and the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Were it not for the oaths, I would have dealt with her differently.”

Ibn ‘Abbās was asked: “Was this the woman about whom the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘If I were to stone anyone without proof, I would stone her’?”

He replied: “No. That was a woman who openly engaged in wickedness while a Muslim.”

He thus stated that he would not stone anyone without proof, even if that person’s wickedness was apparent.

This hadith also shows that resemblance carries weight in this matter, even in the absence of proof. Likewise, it is authentically reported that when that funeral procession passed by him and people praised the deceased—and so on to the end of the report—he said:

“You are Allah’s witnesses on His earth.”

It is also reported from him in al-Musnad that he said:

“Soon you will be able to distinguish the people of Paradise from the people of Hell.”

He was asked: “O Messenger of Allah, how will that be?”

He replied: “By the good and bad things said about them.”

Thus, he regarded widespread reports

---

## Page 7863

as valid proof and evidence in these rulings, but not as proof warranting stoning. Likewise, according to Ahmad, the testimony of the People of the Book against Muslims is accepted concerning a bequest made while travelling. Children’s testimony concerning injuries is also accepted, provided they give it before dispersing, according to one of the two reports from him.

If a witness testifies that he saw a man, a woman, and a boy beneath a blanket or in a privy, or saw the two of them naked or with their trousers undone, and there are accompanying indications supporting his testimony—such as the blanket being found, contrary to customary practice, where the two were; or a light being with one or both of them, brought into view, seen, and then extinguished—then extinguishing that light is evidence that the person was concealing what he was doing. If there was nothing to conceal other than what the witness testified to, this constitutes exceptionally clear evidence supporting his testimony.

This subject is immensely beneficial in matters of religion. It is among the teachings brought by the Sharia that many judges and students of jurisprudence have neglected, claiming that no one may be punished except on the basis of eyewitness testimony or a confession heard directly. This contradicts what is established through mass transmission in the Sunnah and the practice of the Rightly Guided Caliphs. It also contradicts the innate disposition of hearts that recognize right and reject wrong. People of sound judgment know that no just system of governance would reject such evidence, let alone the perfect Sharia. This is supported by the words of Allah Most High:

“O you who believe, if a defiantly disobedient person brings you news, verify it, lest you harm people out of ignorance.”

The verse contains several points of evidence.

---

## Page 7864

One is His saying:

“If a defiantly disobedient person brings you news, verify it.”

He thus commands verification whenever any defiantly disobedient person brings any report. Yet there are reports that one is forbidden to investigate, others that one may leave unverified, and others that entail punishment for certain people. For He explains that the command to verify, when a defiantly disobedient person brings us a report, is motivated by the fear that we might harm people out of ignorance. Were this equally true of anyone harmed on the basis of a report, there would be no distinction between an upright person and a defiantly disobedient one. Rather, this clearly indicates that acting against people on the report of a single upright person is not categorically prohibited. This, in turn, indicates that the testimony of a single upright witness is admissible in matters of punishment in general. The occasion of the verse’s revelation supports this: it was revealed concerning one person’s report that a people had engaged in hostilities through apostasy or breach of a covenant.

It also indicates that whenever a report from a defiantly disobedient person is accompanied by other evidence establishing its truthfulness, the matter has become clear and the requirement of further verification no longer applies. It is therefore permissible to act against people and punish them on the basis of such a person’s report together with corroborating circumstances, when the two make the facts clear. How much more so when the report comes from a single upright person and is accompanied by further evidence! For this reason, the sounder of the two opinions is that evidence of this kind constitutes grounds for suspicion in cases involving collective oaths concerning homicide. When the oaths of those swearing them are added, this constitutes evidence permitting the execution of the person against whom they swear.

As for His saying:

“Lest you harm people out of ignorance,”

He identifies the danger to be avoided as harming people without knowledge. Whenever action is taken against them with knowledge, that danger is removed. This is the criterion indicated by the Quran, as He says:

“Except those who testify to the truth while they know.”

And He says:

“Do not pursue that of which you have no knowledge.”

---

## Page 7865

Moreover, He explains this by the fear of regret, and regret arises only from punishing someone innocent of the offense, as stated in Sunan Abī Dāwūd:

“Avert the prescribed punishments when there are doubts, for it is better for the ruler to err in pardoning than to err in punishing.”

Thus, when the choice lies between mistakenly punishing an innocent person and mistakenly pardoning a guilty person, the latter is the better of the two errors. But if he knows that he has punished only someone guilty, he will have no regret, nor will there have been any error. And Allah knows best.

Al-Shāfi‘ī and Ahmad mentioned that banishment appears in the Sunnah in two instances. The first is the Prophet’s statement, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning someone guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse who has not previously consummated a valid marriage:

“One hundred lashes and banishment for one year.”

The second is the banishment of effeminate men, as related by Umm Salamah:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, entered her home while an effeminate man was with her, saying to her brother ‘Abd Allāh: ‘If Allah grants you the conquest of al-Tā’if tomorrow, I shall direct you to the daughter of Ghaylān: when she approaches, she displays four folds of flesh, and when she turns away, eight.’ The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: ‘Expel them from your homes.’”

All the principal hadith compilers except al-Tirmidhī reported it. A version in the Sahīh reads:

“These people must not enter your homes.”

Another version reads:

“I see that this man understands such things. He must not enter your homes after today.”

Ibn Jurayj said that the effeminate man was Hīt, and others identified him likewise. It has also been said that he was Hanb; some claimed that he was Māti‘, and another account names him Hawān.

All the principal hadith compilers except Muslim reported:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, cursed effeminate men,

---

## Page 7866

and women who adopt masculine ways, and said: ‘Expel them from your homes, and expel so-and-so and so-and-so’—meaning effeminate men.”

Some have mentioned that there were three such men during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace: Bahm, Hīt, and Māti‘. They were not accused of the gravest form of sexual indecency. Their effeminacy and feminine manner consisted only in softness of speech, dyeing their hands and feet as women did, and engaging in amusements like theirs.

In Sunan Abī Dāwūd, Abū Yasār al-Qurashī relates from Abū Hāshim, from Abū Hurayrah:

“An effeminate man whose feet and hands were dyed with henna was brought before the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. He asked: ‘What is the matter with this man?’ They replied: ‘O Messenger of Allah, he imitates women.’ He ordered that the man be banished to al-Naqī‘. They asked: ‘O Messenger of Allah, should we not kill him?’ He replied: ‘I have been forbidden to kill those who pray.’”

Abū Usāmah Hammād ibn Usāmah said: “Al-Naqī‘ is an outlying area away from Medina; it is not al-Baqī‘.” It has been said that this was the place the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, reserved as pasture for the camels collected as charity, and which ‘Umar subsequently reserved. It lies twenty parasangs from Medina; another account says twenty miles.

Naqī‘ al-Khudummāt is another place near Medina. It has also been said that this was the place ‘Umar reserved as pasture. A naqī‘ is a place where water collects, as in the hadith:

“The first Friday prayer held in Medina was at Naqī‘ al-Khudummāt.”

Thus, if the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, ordered people such as these to be expelled from homes, it is clear that a man who makes himself available to other men, allowing them to take pleasure in him and in the beauty they see in him, and to commit the gravest form of sexual indecency with him, is worse than these, and is

---

## Page 7867

more deserving of being banished from the midst of the Muslims and expelled from their company. For an effeminate man has a corrupting influence on both men and women. When he imitates women, women may associate with him and learn from him, although he is a man, and he thus corrupts them. When men become attracted to him, they may turn away from women. Moreover, when a woman sees a man adopting feminine ways, she may herself adopt masculine ways and imitate men, associating with both sexes. She may choose intercourse with women just as he chooses intercourse with men.

As for his corrupting men, it consists in allowing them to do with him what they do with women—by looking at him, having intimate contact with him, and becoming infatuated with him. If he is expelled from among the people and travels to another inhabited town, where he finds someone who commits sexual indecency with him, then his banishment in this case takes the form of confinement in a place where no one else is with him. If there is a fear that he will leave, he is to be restrained, for this is what banishing him and removing him from among the people means.

For this reason, scholars have differed over what it means to banish a brigand from the land: does it mean driving him away so that he finds refuge in no town, imprisoning him, or applying either measure as the ruler sees fit? There are three opinions reported from Ahmad on this question. The third is the fairest and best. Banishing him so that he finds refuge in no town is impracticable, because the population is dispersed and people differ in their inclinations. Indeed, expelling him may lead him to engage in highway robbery. Imprisoning him may also be impracticable, since it requires expenditure on food, drink, and a guard. There is no doubt that banishment is easier, if it is possible.

---

## Page 7868

It has also been reported:

“When Hīt complained of hunger, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, instructed him to enter Medina every Friday to ask for enough to sustain him until the following Friday.”

It is clear that Allah’s words, “or be banished from the land,” do not mean banishing him from the entire earth, but rather removing him from among the people. This is achieved by expelling him and confining him. Such banishment, as prescribed by the Sharia, is a form of separation—that is, shunning the offender. This is not like the banishment of the three who were left behind, nor is shunning him like shunning them. The Prophet prohibited people—even their wives—from associating with them or speaking to them, but did not prevent them from seeing people or attending their gatherings for prayer and other purposes. This falls short of the prescribed banishment, which combines both measures.

Allah created human beings dependent upon one another’s assistance for their religious and worldly welfare. Anyone whose association with people offers no support in religious matters, but instead corrupts them and harms their religious and worldly affairs, deserves to be removed from among them. He brings harm without benefit: his association with them corrupts both them and their children. When a boy sees another boy like himself doing something, he imitates him and follows his ways in the company of the wicked. Associating with those who engage in unlawful sexual intercourse and sodomy brings the gravest corruption and harm upon women, boys, and men. Thus, those who commit sodomy and unlawful sexual intercourse must be punished in a manner that separates them from others and keeps them away.

The essence of separation is to shun evil deeds and those who commit them. The same applies to shunning those who call to

---

## Page 7869

religious innovations, and to shunning sinners and anyone who associates with any of these people or assists them. The same applies to anyone who abandons jihad when their welfare cannot be secured without it: he is punished by their shunning him because he failed to assist them in righteousness and piety. Those who engage in unlawful sexual intercourse, those who practise sodomy, those who abandon jihad, proponents of religious innovations, and wine-drinkers—all these people, and association with them, are harmful to the religion of Islam. They offer no assistance in righteousness or piety. Whoever does not shun them neglects what is commanded and does what is forbidden. Thus, in one case the commanded form of association is neglected, while in the other a forbidden form is practised. Each is therefore punished in a manner appropriate to his offence, for punishment is imposed only for neglecting a command or committing a prohibition. As the jurists have said:

“Discretionary punishment is prescribed only for a sin for which there is no prescribed fixed punishment. If it entails an expiation, there are two opinions within the school of Ahmad and among others.”

He said:

The commands, punishments, expiations, and other provisions laid down by the Sharia are to be implemented according to one’s ability. If a Muslim cannot wage jihad against all the polytheists, he wages jihad against those whom he can. Likewise, if he cannot punish all transgressors, he punishes those whom he can. If banishment and confinement away from everyone are not possible, then banishment and confinement are carried out to the extent possible. For example, he may be confined to a house, having contact only with its residents and not leaving it, or he may have contact with only one or two people. Since this is what is possible, it is what is commanded. If he can be placed somewhere where reprehensible conduct is reduced, though not eliminated altogether, then that is what is commanded, for the Sharia came to secure

---

## Page 7870

benefits and perfect them, and to eliminate harms and minimize them. A little good is better than forgoing it, and averting some evil is better than leaving it entirely unchecked. Likewise, a woman who imitates men is confined, much as she would be if she committed unlawful sexual intercourse, whether a virgin or not, for confinement is among the measures prescribed for sexual immorality in general.

Another example is that of ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb, who banished Nasr ibn Hajjāj from Medina, his homeland, to Basra when he heard of women’s amorous verses about him and of his imitation of them. He had initially ordered his hair to be cut to remove the beauty with which he tempted women. But when he then saw that his cheeks were among the most handsome of anyone’s, this troubled him, so he banished him to Basra. This man had committed no sin or indecency for which he deserved punishment. Some women, however, were infatuated with him, so ʿUmar ordered that his alluring beauty be removed. Leaving his homeland weakens his resolve and his body, and it is known that he is being punished. This falls under separating those who are feared likely to succumb to sexual immorality and infatuation before that happens; it does not fall under punishment. ʿUmar also used to banish wine-drinkers to Khaybar as an additional punishment.

Among the strongest incitements to sexual immorality is the melodious recitation of poetry composed by those whose hearts are diseased with infatuation and a love of sexual immorality and its preliminaries. When a singer sings such verses, he stirs diseased hearts to love sexual immorality. The heart’s disease is then inflamed and its affliction intensified. Even if a heart is free of this, such singing introduces disease into it. As one of the early generations said:

“Singing is an incantation for unlawful sexual intercourse.”

---

## Page 7871

An incantation for a snake is what draws it out of its hole; an incantation against the evil eye and venom is what brings about recovery. An incantation for unlawful sexual intercourse is what invites it and draws this vile conduct and foul deed out of a man, just as wine is the mother of evils. Ibn Masʿūd said:

“Singing causes hypocrisy to grow in the heart just as water causes plants to grow.”

Allah Most High said to Iblīs:

“Incite whoever you can among them with your voice, rally against them your cavalry and infantry, and share with them in wealth and children.”

His inciting them with his voice takes place through singing—as some of the early generations explained—and through other sounds, such as wailing and the like. All these sounds stir the heart and the corrupt soul toward such things, setting them into rapid, agitated motion, until Satan toys with these people even more than children play with a ball. The soul is in motion; if it comes to rest, it does so by Allah’s permission. Otherwise, it remains continually in motion.

Some have likened it to a ball on a smooth, level surface, continually moving upon it. In a hadith traced back to the Prophet:

“The heart is more restless than a pot at a rolling boil.”

In another hadith:

“The heart is like a feather in an open wilderness, moved about by the wind.”

In Sahīh al-Bukhārī, Sālim narrates from Ibn ʿUmar, who said:

“The oath of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was: ‘No, by the Turner of hearts.’”

In Sahīh Muslim, ʿAbd Allāh ibn ʿAmr reported that he heard the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

“O Allah, Director of hearts, direct our hearts toward obedience to You.”

And in al-Tirmidhī

---

## Page 7872

it is narrated from Abū Sufyān, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would frequently say: ‘O Turner of hearts, keep my heart steadfast upon Your religion.’ I said: ‘O Messenger of Allah, we believe in you and in what you have brought. Do you fear for us?’ He replied: ‘Yes. Hearts are between two of Allah’s fingers; He turns them however He wills.’”

As for His words, Most High:

“A man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse marries none but a woman who does likewise or a polytheist woman; and a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse is married by none but a man who does likewise or a polytheist man. That is forbidden to the believers.”

When Allah Most High prescribed punishment for the man and woman who commit unlawful sexual intercourse, He forbade believers to marry either of them, so that they and their sins and evil deeds would be shunned. As He, Most High, said:

“And shun impurity.”

He declared that whoever sits in the company of someone committing such evil is like that person, saying:

“You would then be like them.”

Such a person is his counterpart. Allah Most High has said:

“Gather those who did wrong and their counterparts,”

meaning their associates, companions, those like them, and their peers. Hence it is said: “The listener shares in the backbiter’s sin.”

A group of people who were drinking wine were brought before ʿUmar ibn ʿAbd al-ʿAzīz. Among them was someone who had been sitting with them while fasting. He said: “Begin the flogging with him. Have you not heard Allah say, ‘Do not sit with them’?”

If merely sitting with them and keeping their company temporarily while they commit evil makes a person like them, what, then, of constant companionship?

A husband is also called an ʿashīr (intimate companion), as in the hadith narrated by Ibn ʿAbbās from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“I saw the Fire, and most of its inhabitants were women

---

## Page 7873

who were ungrateful. It was asked: ‘Ungrateful to Allah?’ He replied: ‘They are ungrateful to their husbands and ungrateful for kindness.’”

Allah thus states that none does this except a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse or a polytheist. The polytheist has no faith to restrain him from sexual immorality and associating with those who practise it. As for the man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse, his immorality draws him to this, even though he is not a polytheist.

The verse also indicates that a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse is not a believer in the unqualified sense, even though he is not a disbelieving polytheist. As stated in the authentic hadith:

“A man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse is not a believer while committing it.”

This is because Allah states that such a man marries none but a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse or a polytheist woman. Then He, Most High, says:

“That is forbidden to the believers.”

We therefore know that faith prevents and restrains a person from this, and that whoever does it is either a polytheist or a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse and is not among those believers whose faith prevents them from doing so.

This is because a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse defiles a man’s marriage bed, and marrying her entails continually living with an immoral woman and keeping her company. Allah has commanded that evil and those who practise it be shunned as long as they persist in it. The same consideration applies to a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse: if he does not defile his wife’s marriage bed, he is nevertheless an evil companion for her. As al-Shaʿbī said: “Whoever marries his cherished daughter to an openly sinful man has severed her ties of kinship.” This exposes a woman to harm in both her religious and worldly life.

Marrying a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse is therefore more harmful with respect to the marriage bed, whereas marrying a man who commits it is more harmful in that he is the woman’s master, her possessor, and the one who rules over her. Thus a free, chaste woman remains captive to an immoral man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse

---

## Page 7874

who neglects her rights and mistreats her. For this reason, the jurists agree that suitability in religious commitment must be taken into account and that its absence establishes a right to annul the marriage. They differ, however, over whether a marriage is valid without it. These are two well-known positions in the school of Ahmad and elsewhere.

A man who marries a woman while she continues to commit unlawful sexual intercourse has accepted sharing her with other men and has accepted for himself the role of a procurer and a husband who condones his wife’s sexual immorality. If a woman marries a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse with other women, he does not reserve his semen for her alone; rather, he deposits it in her and in prostitutes besides her. She is thus like a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse and takes a secret lover. One purpose of marriage is to reserve a man’s semen for his wife, whereas this man does not do so. Allah, Glorified is He, has stipulated that men must be chaste, not given to unlawful sexual intercourse. He says:

“And all women beyond these are lawful for you, provided that you seek them with your wealth in marriage, not in unlawful sexual intercourse.”

This point must not be overlooked, for the Quran has expressly stated it and set it out as a binding prescription, as the Most High says:

“A surah which We have sent down and made binding.”

As for the prohibition on marrying a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse, jurists among Ahmad’s followers and others have discussed it, and reports concerning it have been transmitted from the early generations. Although the jurists have disputed this matter, those who permit it have no reliable basis for doing so. Some have claimed that this verse is abrogated by His words:

“And chaste women”

---

## Page 7875

and have claimed that a prostitute is among the “chaste women.” Yet those verses constitute evidence against them, for chastity is the very least that this description entails. If free status is also stipulated, that further completes chastity and virtue. If someone prohibits marriage to a slave woman lest his child be born enslaved, how can he permit marriage to a prostitute who attributes to him a child who is not his? How much graver is the corruption of his marriage bed than the enslavement of his child!

The same applies to those who claim that “marriage” here means sexual intercourse, so that the meaning is that a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse has intercourse only with a woman who does likewise or with a polytheist, and that a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse has intercourse only with a man who does likewise or with a polytheist. This is an even stronger argument against them: whoever has intercourse with such a woman or a polytheist woman under a marriage contract is himself guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse. The same applies to a woman with whom a man guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse has intercourse. For a person is condemned for his or her own act of unlawful sexual intercourse. Thus, even if he forces himself upon her, or she inserts his penis into herself while he is asleep, punishment falls upon the perpetrator alone, not the other party. This issue is discussed at length in the books of jurisprudence.

The point here is His statement:

“A man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse marries none but a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse or a polytheist.”

This indicates that a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse marries only a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse or a polytheist, and that this is forbidden to believers. This is not merely because he is immoral, but specifically because he commits unlawful sexual intercourse. Likewise, in the woman’s case, it is not merely because of her immorality, but specifically because of her unlawful sexual intercourse. The evidence is that He regards a woman as guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse if she marries a man guilty of it, just as He regards a husband as guilty of it if he marries a woman guilty of it. This applies when both are Muslims who believe unlawful sexual intercourse to be forbidden. If they are both polytheists, that should be recognized.

The import is that a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse may not be married until he repents, by bringing himself into conformity with His stipulation of chastity. And if a woman is

---

## Page 7876

a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse and does not reserve herself for her husband alone, but has intercourse with him and with others, then her husband too is guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse: he and the others share sexual access to her, just as men who commit unlawful sexual intercourse share the same woman. For this reason, he must deny paternity of a child who is not his. Thus, whoever marries a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse is himself guilty of it—meaning that he marries her—and whoever marries a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse is herself guilty of it—meaning that she marries him.

Many men who commit unlawful sexual intercourse confine themselves to particular women who do likewise: a woman becomes a man’s mistress and intimate companion, and he has intercourse with no other woman. When a man commits unlawful sexual intercourse, he does not keep his wife chaste. If he does not keep her chaste, she longs for another man and has unlawful sexual intercourse with him. This is commonly the case with the wives of men who commit unlawful sexual intercourse or sodomize boys. Their wives commit unlawful sexual intercourse to satisfy their needs and desires, and to spite their husbands thereby. Since their husbands do not restrain themselves from women other than their wives, the wives likewise do not restrain themselves from men other than their husbands.

For this reason, it is said:

“Be chaste, and your wives and sons will be chaste; treat your fathers well, and your sons will treat you well.”

Recompense corresponds to the deed: as you deal with others, so will you be dealt with. One punishment for an evil deed is another evil deed that follows it. If a man willingly marries a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse, he accepts that his wife should commit it. Allah Most High has placed affection and mercy between spouses, so each desires for himself or herself what he or she desires for the other. Thus, if a woman willingly marries a man who commits unlawful sexual intercourse, she approves of his conduct. Likewise, if a man willingly marries a woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse, he approves of her conduct. Whoever approves of unlawful sexual intercourse is like one who commits it, for the foundation of an act is the will to perform it. Hence the report states: “Whoever is absent when a sin is committed, yet approves of it

---

## Page 7877

is like one who witnessed it or committed it.”

The hadith also states:

“A person follows the religion of his close companion.”

The closest companionship is that between spouses. Moreover, Allah has instilled in human beings the well-known sense of protective jealousy: a man finds another man having intercourse with his wife more objectionable than his own sense of honor makes him find committing unlawful sexual intercourse himself. If he is not averse to his wife being sexually immoral, and condones her conduct, how would he be averse to committing unlawful sexual intercourse himself? Hence, no man who condones his wife’s sexual immorality or acts as a procurer is found to abstain from unlawful sexual intercourse. A man who commits it has his own desire to satisfy, whereas a man who condones his wife’s conduct has no desire of his own satisfied by another man’s unlawful intercourse with her. If he lacks the faith to abhor another man’s unlawful intercourse with his wife, how could he have the faith to restrain himself from unlawful sexual intercourse?

Whoever regards it as permissible to let his wife commit unlawful sexual intercourse regards the gravest form of unlawful sexual intercourse as permissible. Whoever assists in this is like one who commits it, and whoever allows it to continue when able to stop it has approved of it. Whoever marries a woman who has not repented has accepted that she will commit unlawful sexual intercourse, since he cannot prevent her from doing so—for women’s scheming is indeed formidable.

For this reason, if a man’s wife commits manifest sexual immorality, he may put pressure on her so that she ransoms herself from him. This is expressly stated by Ahmad and others, because, through her unlawful sexual intercourse, she has sought release from the marriage and acted in a way that undermines it. He cannot remain with her until she repents. Yet her unlawful sexual intercourse does not, in itself, forfeit her dower, as indicated by the Prophet’s words—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—to the husband who had engaged in mutual imprecation. When the man asked, “What about my money?” he replied:

“You have no claim to any money from her. If your accusation against her is true, it was in return for the intercourse you lawfully enjoyed with her; and if you have falsely accused her

---

## Page 7878

then it is even further beyond your reach.” This is because, if she commits unlawful sexual intercourse, she may repent. Her conduct does, however, permit him to put pressure on her until she either ransoms herself from him, if she chooses separation, or repents.

Generally, a man has unlawful sexual intercourse with someone other than his wife only because he is attracted to that person. He then continues having unlawful intercourse with whoever attracts him, leaving his wife suspended between marriage and separation: neither unmarried nor truly enjoying married life. This drives her towards unlawful sexual intercourse, motivated by a desire to retaliate against her husband, outwit him and provoke his anger. For if he does not remain faithful to her in her absence, she will not remain faithful to him in his. She has a right to sexual relations with him, just as he has a right to sexual relations with her. If he transgresses by going beyond what Allah has permitted him, he has not kept himself chaste.

Moreover, the sexual desire of a man who commits unlawful intercourse becomes preoccupied with the prostitutes he chooses. His desire for lawful intercourse is therefore diminished, and his protective jealousy is insufficient to safeguard his wife’s chastity. While married to him, she thus becomes like an adulteress who has taken a secret lover. These are important considerations that should not be disregarded.

Accordingly, a woman who engages in sexual acts with other women is guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse, as stated in the hadith:

“The unlawful sexual intercourse of women is their sexual activity with one another.”

A man who commits the act of the people of Lot with a male slave or anyone else is guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse, and a woman who marries him is likewise guilty of it. Thus, none marries him but a woman guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse or a polytheist. For this reason, many wives of men who engage in such acts have unlawful intercourse with men other than their husbands. A wife may even have intercourse with the man with whom her husband commits that act, to spite her husband and satisfy her own desire.

Likewise, a woman married to an effeminate man who is penetrated as she herself is penetrated is married to a man guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse. Indeed, he is the worse of the two, for in addition to committing unlawful intercourse, he has become effeminate and is subject to a curse specifically for his effeminacy, distinct from the curse that befalls him for committing the act of the people of Lot.

---

## Page 7879

For the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, cursed those who commit the act of the people of Lot. It is also authentically reported from him in the Sahīh that he cursed effeminate men and women who imitate men, and said:

“Expel them from your homes.”

How, then, can a woman be permitted to marry an effeminate man whose sexual desire has shifted to his anus? He is penetrated as a woman is penetrated, and his desire to penetrate diminishes, just as a man who commits unlawful intercourse with someone other than his wife loses his desire for her. If he has no protective jealousy concerning himself, his protective jealousy concerning his wife and others diminishes. Hence, an effeminate man may have little protective jealousy concerning his child, his slave or anyone in his care.

If a woman accepts an effeminate man or a man who commits the act of the people of Lot, she follows his way and thus becomes guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse—and worse still. For it is easier for a woman to submit to intercourse than for a man to submit to being penetrated. If she accepts such conduct in her husband, she accepts it in herself.

The wording of this verse, namely the words of the Most High:

“A man guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse marries none but a woman guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse,”

and the remainder of the verse, encompasses all of this: either through the general scope of its wording, or through its indication and a fortiori implication, which is stronger than its explicit meaning. At the very least, it encompasses this by analogy, as we have explained when discussing the prescribed punishment for a man who commits the act of the people of Lot and similar matters. And Allah knows best.

As for the words of the Most High:

“Wicked women are for wicked men, and wicked men are for wicked women; good women are for good men, and good men are for good women.”

The Most High states that wicked women are for wicked men. Thus, a wicked woman cannot be for a good man, since that would contradict the exclusivity expressed here. Thus, no

---

## Page 7880

wicked woman guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse marries anyone but a wicked man guilty of the same. He also states that good men are for good women. Thus, a good man cannot be for a wicked woman, since that would contradict the exclusivity expressed here. He has stated that all wicked women are for wicked men, leaving no wicked woman for a good man, nor any good man for a wicked woman.

He also states that all good women are for good men, leaving no good woman for a wicked man. This exclusivity therefore applies on both sides, in agreement with His words:

“A man guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse marries none but a woman guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse or a polytheist woman; and a woman guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse is married by none but a man guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse or a polytheist. And that is forbidden to the believers.”

This is why some of the early generations said: “No prophet’s wife ever committed adultery.” The opening portion of this surah was revealed concerning those who spread the slander and what they said about ‘Ā’ishah. Thus, when those allegations were made against her and suspicion arose, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, consulted those whom he consulted about divorcing her, before the revelation declaring her innocence came down. For it would not be fitting for him to have a wife who was not good. It has also been reported:

“No dayyūth will enter Paradise.”

A dayyūth is a man who condones immorality in his family. Hence, protective jealousy against adultery is something Allah loves and has commanded. Indeed, the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Are you astonished at Sa‘d’s protective jealousy? I have greater protective jealousy than he, and Allah has greater protective jealousy than I. For this reason, He has forbidden shameful deeds, whether open or concealed.”

For this reason, Allah has permitted a man who accuses a woman of adultery, if he is her husband, to invoke li‘ān (sworn imprecation): he testifies four times, swearing by Allah that he is telling the truth. Allah has made this avert the prescribed punishment for slander from him, just as producing four witnesses to the accusation would do, because he needs to accuse her in view of Allah’s command to exercise

---

## Page 7881

protective jealousy, and because she has wronged him by defiling his marriage bed. If she has become pregnant through adultery, he must invoke li‘ān (sworn imprecation) to disavow the false attribution of paternity, lest a child who is not his be attributed to him.

The established Sunnah of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, requires the separation of spouses who invoke li‘ān, whether their separation takes effect through their mutual imprecation, requires a judge’s decree, or takes effect upon the husband’s completion of his imprecation. For one of them is accursed or wicked, and their remaining together thereafter would mean joining a wicked, accursed person to a good one.

Sahīh Muslim contains the report narrated by ‘Imrān ibn Husayn:

“A woman cursed a she-camel of hers. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, ordered that its load be removed and that it be released. He said, ‘A cursed she-camel shall not accompany us.’”

The two Sahīh collections also relate that, when he passed through the dwellings of Thamūd, he said:

“Do not enter the dwellings of these people who were punished unless you are weeping. If you are not weeping, do not enter them, lest what befell them should befall you.”

Thus, he forbade passing through their dwellings except in a state of fear that would guard against punishment.

Such is also the Sunnah concerning association with wrongdoers, those who commit unlawful sexual intercourse, those who engage in religious innovation and wickedness, and those who commit other sins: no one should associate or mingle with them except in a manner that keeps him safe from the punishment of Allah, Mighty and Majestic. At the very least, he must, as far as he is able, denounce their wrongdoing, detest them, and abhor what they are engaged in, as stated in the hadith:

“Whoever among you sees an evil, let him change it with his hand. If he is unable, then with his tongue; and if he is unable

---

## Page 7882

then with his heart; and that is the weakest level of faith.” Allah Most High says:

“Allah sets forth an example for those who believe: the wife of Pharaoh”—to the end of the verse.

Likewise, He relates how Joseph, the man of truth, administered the storehouses of the land for the ruler of Egypt, serving a disbelieving people.

A believer associates with the wicked only in two circumstances. The first is when he is compelled to do so. The second is when doing so serves a religious benefit that outweighs the harm of such association, or when refraining from it would cause greater harm to his religion. He thus averts the greater of two harms by bearing the lesser, and secures the greater benefit by bearing the lesser harm. In fact, a person under compulsion averts the harm that would otherwise occur by bearing the lesser of the two harms—namely, the act he is compelled to perform. Allah Most High says:

“Except for one who is compelled while his heart remains secure in faith.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Do not compel your slave girls into prostitution.”

Then He says:

“If anyone compels them, then Allah, after they have been compelled, is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”

Allah Most High says:

“When the angels take the souls of those who have wronged themselves, they ask, ‘What was your situation?’ They reply, ‘We were oppressed in the land.’ The angels say, ‘Was Allah’s earth not spacious enough for you to emigrate elsewhere?’ Their refuge will be Hell—what an evil destination!

“Except for those men, women, and children who are helpless, unable to find a means or a way.

“For such people, it is hoped that Allah will pardon them. Allah is Ever-Pardoning, All-Forgiving.”

He also says:

“What is the matter with you that you do not fight in the cause of Allah and for the oppressed men, women, and children?”—to the end of the verse.

---

## Page 7883

This verse thus indicates the prohibition on marrying someone who engages in unlawful sexual intercourse. Marriage is a particular form of association, pairing, intimacy, and companionship. Hence each spouse is called the other’s mate, companion, partner, and intimate associate. In its original linguistic sense, marriage denotes coming together and joining. Their hearts come together when the marriage contract is concluded, and mutual affection and compassion arise between them that did not exist before. The contract alone establishes the marriage prohibitions arising from affinity—except in the case of a stepdaughter—as well as mutual inheritance rights, the widow’s waiting period, and other consequences.

The intermediate stage is their being alone together in one place. This form of marital association establishes entitlement to the full bridal gift, as the caliphs ruled. The final stage is their coming together in sexual intercourse. Even if this occurs without a marriage contract, it is a weak union; indeed, the union of hearts is greater than the mere union of two bodies through unlawful intercourse.

His words:

“Pure women are for pure men”

indicate this through both their meaning and their wording. They also indicate the prohibition on associating and pairing with the wicked, as do other texts, such as His words:

“Gather those who committed wrongdoing and their mates,”

meaning those like them and their counterparts. The term zawj (mate or pair) has a broader scope than marriage in its familiar sense. Allah Most High says:

“He grants daughters to whom He wills, and grants sons to whom He wills.”

“Or He grants them both sons and daughters.”

He also says:

“And when souls are paired.”

He also says:

“Of every delightful kind,”

and “noble.”

He also says:

“And of all things We created pairs.”

He also says:

“He placed in it two of each kind.”

He also says:

“And We created you in pairs.”

He also says: “Take aboard it, of every kind, a pair

---

## Page 7884

of two.” He also says:

“Indeed, among your spouses and your children”

Although the verse explicitly mentions the wife, who is one’s companion, and the child born of her, its meaning extends to every counterpart, associate, and partner, and to every offshoot and follower. Thus:

“Praise be to Allah, who has never taken a son, who has no partner in His dominion, and who has no protector due to weakness.”

And:

“Blessed is He who sent down the Criterion upon His servant, that he may be a warner to all peoples—He to whom belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth, who has never taken a son and has no partner in His dominion, and who created everything and determined it precisely.”

Companionship, marriage ties, and bonds of brotherhood are permissible only with those who obey Allah Most High, and in accordance with Allah’s intent. This is indicated by the hadith recorded in the Sunan collections:

“Keep company only with a believer, and let none but a God-fearing person eat your food.”

They also record:

“A person follows the religion of his close friend, so let each of you consider whom he befriends.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Abū Hurayrah narrates that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“If the slave woman of any of you commits unlawful sexual intercourse, let him administer the prescribed flogging to her. If she does so again, let him administer the prescribed flogging to her. Then, if she does so again, let him sell her, even for a braided rope.”

The word dafīr means “rope.” The narrator was unsure whether he commanded that she be sold after the third or fourth occasion.

This is a command from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to sell the slave woman after administering the prescribed punishment to her twice or three times, even for the smallest sum. Imam Ahmad said:

“If he does not sell her, he has failed to comply with the command of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

---

## Page 7885

Most slave women who do this are kept for service, not for sexual enjoyment. What, then, of a slave woman kept for sexual enjoyment? If a man must relinquish ownership of a slave woman who commits unlawful sexual intercourse, what, then, of a wife who does so? A male slave or bondsman is analogous to a slave woman. All of this is supported by Muslim’s report in his Sahīh from ‘Alī ibn Abī Tālib that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,

“cursed anyone who commits an offense or shelters an offender.”

This entails a curse upon anyone who shelters an offender, whether the offense is unlawful sexual intercourse, theft, or something else, and whether the shelter is provided through slave ownership, marriage, or otherwise. At the very least, doing so constitutes a failure to condemn wrongdoing.

Section

The believer needs to test anyone whom he wishes to take as a companion or associate, whether through marriage or otherwise. Allah Most High says:

“When believing women come to you as emigrants, test them. Allah knows best their faith.”

And so the verse continues. Likewise, according to the more correct of the two views, a man may marry a woman with whom he has had unlawful sexual intercourse only after repentance, as indicated by the Quran, the Sunnah, and the reports from the early generations. But if he wishes to test whether her repentance is genuine, how should he proceed?

‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Umar said—and this is also Ahmad’s expressly stated position—that he should solicit her for intercourse. If she agrees, her repentance is not genuine; if she refuses, she has repented.

Another group said:

“This test

---

## Page 7886

involves asking her to commit an indecent act. She may break her repentance, and his own soul may urge him actually to commit the act. Satan may make it seem attractive to them both, especially if they love one another, have previously committed the act together repeatedly, and have tasted one another’s pleasures. She may then break her repentance and comply with what he wants from her.”

Those who hold the first view reply:

A request intended to test her is not intended to bring about the act itself, so it does not constitute a command to do what Allah has forbidden. He can avoid soliciting unlawful intercourse outright, instead alluding to it while intending something else. Using indirect expressions when needed is permissible; indeed, it is obligatory in many situations. As for her breaking her repentance, if she might break it with him, she might also break it with someone else. The point is that she should resist anyone who solicits her. If she does not resist him, she will not resist others.

As for Satan making the act attractive to him, this risk is present in every good undertaking that also involves something a person loves. Thus, if someone wishes to take a believer as a companion, or a believer wishes to take someone as a companion, and that person has been reported to engage in wickedness but is said to have repented—or such conduct has been alleged against him, whether truthfully or falsely—he should test him in a way that reveals his righteousness or wickedness, his truthfulness or dishonesty. Likewise, if he wishes to appoint someone to a position of authority, he should test him.

Thus, when ‘Umar ibn ‘Abd al-‘Azīz was impressed by Ibn Abī Mūsā’s bearing, he instructed his servant to test him. The servant said to him:

“You know my standing with the Commander of the Faithful. How much will you give me if I recommend that he appoint you to office?”

---

## Page 7887

So he offered him a large sum, and ‘Umar realized that he was unfit for office. The same applies in business dealings, and to testing boys and slaves known or reported to engage in sexual misconduct when a man wishes to purchase one of them. For an effeminate man is like a prostitute, and his repentance is like hers. People’s character may be ascertained through others’ testimony, through adverse or favorable assessments, or through testing and examination.

Section

Just as Allah has emphasized the gravity of sexual immorality, He has emphasized the gravity of falsely accusing someone of it—that is, slanderous accusations of illicit intercourse. He then said:

“And those who accuse chaste women and then do not produce four witnesses—lash them with eighty lashes.”

He then mentioned a man’s accusation against his wife and the mutual oaths of imprecation He prescribed in that case. He then related the account of those responsible for the slander, explaining the good this holds for the person falsely accused and lied about, and the sin incurred by the accuser. He also explained that, upon hearing such things, believers must think well of their fellow believers and say, “This is an obvious falsehood,” for the evidence offered for it is manifestly false. He then stated that it was an allegation without proof, saying:

“Why did they not produce four witnesses to it? Since they have not produced the witnesses, it is they who are the liars in Allah’s sight.”

He then stated that, were it not for His favor and mercy toward them, He would have punished them for what they had said.

---

## Page 7888

And His words:

“When you passed it from tongue to tongue and said with your mouths what you had no knowledge of”

explain the reason for the punishment: passing falsehood from tongue to tongue and voicing it. These are two prohibited kinds of speech: speaking falsehood and speaking without knowledge.

Then He, Glorified is He, said:

“Why, when you heard it, did you not say, ‘It is not for us to speak of this. Glory be to You! This is a tremendous slander’?”

The earlier passage urges them to think well of others, while this one forbids them to voice slanderous accusations of illicit intercourse. Relevant to the former are His words:

“Avoid much suspicion; indeed, some suspicion is sinful.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said:

“Beware of suspicion, for suspicion is the falsest of speech.”

Likewise, His words, Exalted is He,

“The believing men and women thought well of one another,”

show the goodness of this kind of thinking, which Allah has commanded. It is established in al-Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to ‘Ā’ishah:

“I do not think that so-and-so and so-and-so know anything of this matter of ours.”

This indicates that some suspicion is permissible, as al-Bukhārī argued on the basis of this report. However, when one knows that a Muslim possesses faith that restrains him from committing sexual immorality, one must think well of him, not ill.

The verse forbids passing such things from tongue to tongue and forbids a person to say what he has no knowledge of, in keeping with His words, Exalted is He:

“Do not pursue that of which you have no knowledge.”

Allah, Exalted is He, has prescribed punishments for committing sexual immorality and falsely accusing others of it unlike those He has prescribed for any other sins. For He prescribed stoning for it, and He Himself—Exalted is He—stoned the people of

---

## Page 7889

Lot, since they were the first to commit the abomination of sodomy. He prescribed eighty lashes as the punishment for falsely accusing someone of sexual immorality. Punishment for accusations of other offences is subject to juristic discretion; some scholars permit it to reach eighty lashes, a view held by many of them. As ‘Alī said:

“No one who prefers me over Abū Bakr and ‘Umar shall be brought before me without my flogging him with the prescribed punishment for a slanderer.”

And as ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn ‘Awf said:

“When he drinks, he speaks incoherently, and when he speaks incoherently, he makes slanderous accusations. The prescribed punishment for drinking is eighty lashes, and the prescribed punishment for a slanderer is eighty lashes.”

Allah, Exalted is He, says in the verse:

“Indeed, those who love that immorality should spread among the believers will have a painful punishment in this world and the Hereafter.”

This condemns anyone who loves that, whether this remains in the heart alone or is also expressed through speech and bodily actions. It condemns anyone who speaks of sexual immorality or reports it out of a desire for it to occur among the believers—whether out of envy or hatred, or out of love and desire for immorality itself. Both constitute love of immorality and hatred of the believers. Thus, whoever loves to practise it speaks of it.

The scholars disapproved of amorous poetry that encourages it. Likewise, mentioning it constitutes forbidden backbiting, whether in verse or prose. Imitating those who practise it is also forbidden, just as urging others to engage in it is forbidden. An action may be encouraged through commands at one time and through narratives at another. For the wicked—fornicators and those who practise sodomy—these two means serve the same purpose as recounting the stories of the prophets and the righteous serves for believers. The former draw lessons from jealous concern for them, while the latter draw lessons from delusion. For the people of disbelief, wickedness and disobedience recount stories of

---

## Page 7890

those like themselves, in whom they find examples and models to follow. Among the passages concerning this is His saying, Exalted is He:

“And among people are those who purchase idle talk to lead others astray from Allah’s path without knowledge and to make a mockery of it.”

It has been said that this refers to singing, and it has also been said that it refers to stories of the unbelieving Persian kings.

In short, every narrative or command that encourages people to obey Allah and discourages them from disobeying Him is itself an act of obedience to Him. Everything that encourages them to disobey Him and discourages them from obeying Him is itself an act of disobedience to Him.

As for speaking of sexual immorality and its practitioners in ways that the Sharia requires or recommends—such as warning against it and them, condemning it and them, mentioning what makes it abhorrent and repellent, speaking of its practitioners in general where this is permissible, and directing lawful criticism at them both in their presence and in their absence—all of this is good: sometimes obligatory and sometimes recommended. The same applies to descriptions of such immorality and its practitioners involving passionate love, when presented in the prescribed manner that leads people to refrain from what Allah has forbidden and to hate what He hates.
