Thus Allah has recounted to us in the Quran the stories of the prophets, the believers and the God-fearing, as well as the stories of the wicked and the unbelievers, so that we may learn from both: loving the former and their path and following their example, while hating the latter and their path and avoiding their deeds. Allah has related how His prophets and righteous servants spoke of sexual immorality

---

## Page 7891

and matters connected with it in a condemnatory manner that offers lessons to be learned. He, Exalted is He, says:

“And Lot, when he said to his people, ‘Do you commit an immorality that no one in all the worlds has ever committed before you?’”

The account continues to its conclusion in several places in His Book. Here Lot—who was Allah’s messenger—addressed those who committed this immorality, rebuking them for it by saying:

“Do you commit immorality?”

This question expresses disapproval and prohibition: it denounces, censures, and forbids. It is like someone saying to another: “Do you do such-and-such? Do you not fear Allah?” Then he said:

“Do you indeed approach men with desire instead of women?”

This is a second question, containing emphatic censure and rebuke. This does not constitute slanderous accusation or disparagement. Likewise, His saying:

“The people of Lot denied the messengers.”

The account continues to its conclusion. Lot confronted them with censure and rebuke for committing immorality. Those who committed it then threatened him and his followers with expulsion from the town. Such is the way of the wicked: whenever someone among them forbids their wrongdoing, they seek to banish and expel him.

Allah has punished those who commit the sexual immorality of Lot’s people with what they intended to inflict upon the God-fearing: He commanded the banishment of those who commit unlawful sexual intercourse and of effeminate men. Thus, the Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, established the banishment of both. And He, Glorified be He, removed the God-fearing from among them when the punishment descended.

Likewise, He, Exalted is He, related in the story of Joseph:

“The woman in whose house he was sought to seduce him,”

through to His saying:

“So He turned their scheming away from him. Indeed, He is the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.”

And in what He related thereafter are Joseph’s words, beginning with his saying: “What was the matter with

---

## Page 7892

the women who cut their hands?” This is a lesson that restrains souls from disobeying Allah and leads them to hold fast to piety. He likewise makes this clear at the end of the surah, saying:

“Indeed, in their stories there is a lesson for people of understanding.”

Nevertheless, some people, including women, enjoy listening to this surah because it mentions passionate love and related matters, out of their love for such things and their desire for sexual immorality. Some even deliberately have women and others listen to it out of a love for evil. They incline toward such things, yet do not choose to hear the punishment and prohibitions concerning them in Sūrat al-Nūr. One of the early generations even said:

“Whatever I gained in Sūrat Yūsuf, I spent in Sūrat al-Nūr.”

Allah, Exalted is He, says:

“We send down of the Quran that which is a healing and a mercy for the believers.”

Then He says:

“But it only increases the wrongdoers in loss.”

He also says:

“Whenever a surah is revealed, some of them say, ‘Which of you has this increased in faith?’ As for those who believe, it has increased them in faith, and they rejoice.”

“But as for those in whose hearts is a disease, it has added impurity to their impurity, and they died as disbelievers.”

Thus, anyone who enjoys listening to such accounts in order to arouse blameworthy love, but dislikes listening to such admonitions because he is unwilling to resist and dispel that love, is blameworthy. This category also includes recounting the circumstances of disbelievers and the wicked, and anything else that encourages disobedience to Allah and turns people away from His path.

---

## Page 7893

This category also includes listening to the words of adherents of religious innovation and reading their books, for anyone whom this harms and draws toward their path and toward disobedience to Allah. This category thus brings together misleading doubts and desires. Allah, Exalted is He, condemns such people in passages such as His words:

“They inspire one another with alluring speech by way of deception.”

And His words:

“As for poets, the erring follow them.”

And His words:

“Shall I inform you upon whom the devils descend?”

This verse and those that follow it; and His words:

“Among people are those who purchase idle talk to lead others astray from Allah’s path without knowledge and to make a mockery of it.”

And His words:

“Arrogant concerning it, conversing by night, speaking evil.”

And His words:

“If they see the path of righteousness, they do not take it as their path; but if they see the path of error, they take it as their path.”

And His words:

“If you obey most of those on earth, they will lead you astray from Allah’s path.”

And the rest of the verse. There are many such passages in the Quran. Those who disobey Allah are numerous in the world; indeed, they are the majority, as Allah, Exalted is He, says:

“If you obey most of those on earth, they will lead you astray from Allah’s path.”

And the rest of the verse. Souls harbour blameworthy doubts and desires, expressed in words and deeds, whose extent only Allah knows. Those who harbour them call others to them, coerce those who defy them, and make them seem attractive to those who obey them. They are therefore the enemies and rivals of the messengers. Allah’s messengers call people to obey Allah, enjoin this upon them through encouragement and warning, and strive to uphold it. They forbid people to disobey Allah, caution them against it through encouragement and warning, and struggle against those who do so. These people, by contrast, call others to disobey Allah

---

## Page 7894

and command them to do so, using encouragement and warning in word and deed, and strive to that end. Allah, Exalted is He, says:

“The hypocrites, men and women, are alike: they enjoin what is wrong, forbid what is right, and are tight-fisted. They have forgotten Allah, so He has forgotten them. Indeed, the hypocrites are the defiantly disobedient.”

Then He says:

“The believers, men and women, are allies of one another. They enjoin what is right, forbid what is wrong, establish prayer, give zakat, and obey Allah and His Messenger. It is they upon whom Allah will have mercy.”

Allah, Exalted is He, also says:

“Those who believe fight in the cause of Allah, while those who disbelieve fight in the cause of false gods.”

There are many such passages in the Quran. Allah, Glorified is He, has commanded us to enjoin what is right and forbid what is wrong. Enjoining something must be preceded by knowing it: whoever does not know what is right cannot enjoin it. Likewise, forbidding what is wrong must be preceded by knowing it: whoever does not know it cannot forbid it.

Allah has also obligated us to do what is right and abandon what is wrong. Loving and practising the former, and hating and abandoning the latter, require prior knowledge of both, so that one can properly intend to do what is right and abandon what is wrong. Such an intention must be preceded by knowledge. Anyone who has no knowledge of something cannot conceivably love or hate it, do it or refrain from it. However, doing something and enjoining it require sufficiently detailed knowledge to enable one to do it and enjoin it, when the command specifies its details.

For this reason, Allah has obligated each person to learn the duties He has commanded, such as the manner of prayer, fasting, Hajj, jihad, and enjoining what is right

---

## Page 7895

and forbidding what is wrong. When His command specifies particular attributes, one must know that they are present. Just as we are not obedient when we know that obedience is absent, we are not obedient unless we do not know that it is present. Rather, ignorance of its presence is like knowledge of its absence, in that both constitute disobedience. Thus, when exchanging commodities subject to the rules of usury for others of the same kind, not knowing that the quantities are equal is like knowing that they are unequal. If we do not know that they are equal, it is as though we knew that they were unequal.

As for knowing what one must abandon and forbid, a general knowledge of it may suffice in some circumstances. A person needs to recognize what is wrong and oppose it. He may also need evidence that makes this clear, answers to the counterarguments advanced by those who practise it, and the means to resist their desires and intentions. This requires firm resolve and the ability to act accordingly, neither of which is possible without patience. As Allah, Exalted is He, says:

“By time! Surely mankind is in loss, except those who believe, do righteous deeds, and urge one another to truth and urge one another to patience.”

The first step is to mention such statements and actions in a manner that condemns them, forbids them, and explains the corruption they contain. For opposing wrong in the heart and with the tongue precedes opposing it by physical action. This is the Quran’s approach when Allah, Exalted is He, recounts the statements and actions of disbelievers, the defiantly disobedient, and sinners. He mentions them in a manner that expresses condemnation and hatred of those statements and actions and those responsible for them, explains their corruption and their opposites, and warns against them. Likewise, what He recounts concerning people of knowledge and faith—including His prophets and His righteous allies—is presented with praise and love, explaining its goodness and benefit and encouraging it. An example is His saying, Exalted is He: “And they say, ‘He has taken

---

## Page 7896

a son for Himself—the Most Merciful.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honoured servants.”

“And they say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’”

“You have certainly made a monstrous assertion.”

“The heavens almost rupture at it, the earth splits apart, and the mountains collapse in devastation,”

“because they attribute a son to the Most Merciful.”

“It is not fitting for the Most Merciful to take a son.”

“There is no one in the heavens and the earth who will not come to the Most Merciful as a servant.”

“He has fully accounted for them and numbered them precisely.”

“And each of them will come to Him alone on the Day of Resurrection.”

“And the Jews say, ‘Ezra is the son of Allah,’” and the following verses.

Such passages are very numerous. Whoever loves their statements and actions is one of them: either a disbeliever or a wicked person, according to what he says and does. Whoever is the opposite is not one of them and incurs no punishment for refraining from such conduct. However, he is not rewarded merely for its absence. Rather, he is rewarded for his intention and resolve to refrain from it. This is preceded by knowing its ugliness and hating it for Allah’s sake. This knowledge, intention, and hatred are part of the faith for which he is rewarded, and constitute its lowest degree. As the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever among you sees a wrong, let him change it with his hand,”

to the end of the hadith. Opposing it in the heart consists in hating and detesting it, which can only follow knowledge of it and its ugliness. After that comes opposing it with the tongue, and then with the hand. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“And that is the weakest degree of faith,”

concerning someone who sees a wrong.

But if he sees it without knowing that it is wrong and without detesting it, this faith is not present in his heart when the wrong occurs and he sees it, in circumstances in which he is obliged to hate it

---

## Page 7897

and detest it. Knowing its ugliness requires striving against the disbelievers and hypocrites when they are present. When the wrong is absent, this is not required. Whoever opposes it when it occurs is rewarded, whereas someone in whose presence it has not occurred is not rewarded until he opposes it.

Likewise, many people may turn away from the objectionable words and deeds included in this, just as they turn away from striving against the disbelievers and hypocrites and from enjoining right and forbidding wrong. Although such people are among those who have forsaken evil deeds, they are not among those who strive to eliminate them until there is no more fitnah and religion is entirely for Allah.

Reflect on this, for these two attitudes often coexist in many people: hatred of disbelief and its adherents and of wickedness and its practitioners, alongside an aversion to admonishing them and striving against them. Similarly, someone may love what is right and those who practise it, yet have no desire to enjoin it or strive for it with his life and wealth.

Allah Most High has said:

“The believers are only those who have believed in Allah and His Messenger and then have never doubted, and have striven with their wealth and their lives in the cause of Allah. It is they who are the truthful.”

And He Most High has said:

“Say, ‘If your fathers, your sons, your brothers, your spouses, your kindred, wealth you have acquired, trade whose decline you fear, and dwellings you delight in are dearer to you than Allah and His Messenger and striving in His cause, then wait until Allah brings about His command. Allah does not guide the defiantly disobedient people.’”

And His words:

“You will not find people who believe in Allah and the Last Day showing affection to those who oppose Allah and His Messenger, even if they were their fathers or their sons

---

## Page 7898

or their brothers or their kindred. Those are the ones in whose hearts He has inscribed faith and whom He has strengthened with a spirit from Him”—the verse continues.

Many people—indeed, most—dislike striving against wrongdoing more than they dislike wrongdoing itself, especially when it becomes widespread and is reinforced by doubts and desires. They may incline towards it at times and turn away from it at others, so that a person’s soul becomes self-reproaching after having been one that urged him to evil. Then, if he advances to a higher state in forsaking evil deeds and his soul becomes tranquil, abandoning wrong and reprehensible deeds, it still has no desire to strive, to persevere against the enemy in that cause, or to endure hurtful words and deeds. This is a separate matter, addressed in His words:

“Have you not seen those who were told, ‘Restrain your hands, establish prayer, and give zakah’? But when fighting was prescribed for them, a group of them feared people as they feared Allah, or even more.”

The verses continue as far as His words:

“And Allah is ever a Keeper over all things.”

Intercession means assistance, for the one who assists becomes a second alongside the one assisted. Whoever assists in righteousness or piety has a share in it, and whoever assists in sin and aggression bears a share of it. This applies to what people do with their hearts, tongues, and hands to assist in righteousness and piety or to assist in sin and aggression. It includes striving with one’s life and wealth for either of these two causes, as Allah Most High says earlier:

“O you who believe, take your precautions, then go forth in groups or go forth all together,”

through to His words:

“Indeed, Satan’s plotting is ever weak.”

---

## Page 7899

From this, the distinction in hearing and sight between faith and its effects and disbelief and its effects becomes clear, as does the distinction between the righteous believer and the disbeliever and evildoer.

Believers hear accounts of the people of faith and behold them with knowledge and understanding, love and reverence for them, their accounts, and their legacy—just as the Companions saw the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and heard what he conveyed from Allah. The disbeliever and hypocrite, however, hear and see with hatred and ignorance, as Allah Most High says:

“Those who disbelieve almost make you stumble with their looks when they hear the Reminder.”

He also says:

“But when a decisive surah is sent down and fighting is mentioned in it, you see those in whose hearts is disease looking at you like someone swooning at the approach of death.”

And He says:

“They were unable to hear, nor did they see.”

And He says:

“So they became blind and deaf. Then Allah turned to them in forgiveness, but then many of them became blind and deaf again.”

Concerning the believers, Allah Most High says:

“And those who, when reminded of their Lord’s signs, do not turn a deaf ear and a blind eye to them.”

Concerning the disbelievers, He says:

“Why, then, do they turn away from the Reminder?”

There are very many verses on this subject. Likewise, looking upon the adornments of worldly life is a trial. Allah Most High says:

“Do not extend your gaze towards what We have granted some groups of them to enjoy—the splendour of worldly life—by which We test them. Your Lord’s provision is better and more lasting.”

In Surah al-Tawbah, He says:

“So let neither their wealth nor their children impress you.”

The verse continues. He also says:

“Tell the believing men to lower their gaze.”

The verse continues. And He says:

“And do not let your eyes pass beyond them, desiring the adornment of worldly life.”

And He says:

“Do they not look

---

## Page 7900

at camels, how they were created?” The verses continue.

He also says:

“Say: Look at what is in the heavens and the earth.”

And He says:

“Have they not looked at what is before them and behind them of the heaven and earth?”

The verse continues. Likewise, Satan said:

“I see what you do not see.”

And He says:

“When the two armies came within sight of each other…”

The verses continue. And He says:

“When Allah showed them to you in your dream as few in number…”

The verse continues.

Looking at worldly enjoyments with love and reverence for this world and its people is forbidden. Looking at created things above and below, with reflection and a view to drawing lessons, is commanded and encouraged. As for looking upon such things in the course of jihad, enjoining right and forbidding wrong, in order to repel and remove those people’s evil, this is commanded. Likewise, looking to draw lessons is, in general, prescribed by religious law. Thus, looking at the very same object may be commanded, either to draw lessons or out of hatred for it, whereas looking at it out of aversion to jihad is forbidden. The same distinction applies to allegiance and enmity.

A person may fall into temptation through a forbidden gaze while supposing that he is looking to draw a lesson. Conversely, he may be commanded to engage in jihad yet suppose that the looking it entails would be a source of temptation, like those of whom Allah Most High says:

“Among them is one who says: Give me leave, and do not expose me to temptation.”

The verse continues. It was revealed concerning al-Jadd ibn Qays when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, ordered him to prepare for an expedition against the Romans. He said: “I am infatuated with women, and I fear being tempted by the Roman women, so allow me to remain behind.” Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, it is into temptation that they have fallen, and Hell will surely encompass the disbelievers.”

---

## Page 7901

This and similar examples concern spoken words. As for physical deeds, every act involving a desire for sexual immorality to spread among the believers falls under this warning; indeed, its punishment is more severe. Allah has threatened painful punishment in this world and the Hereafter for merely desiring that sexual immorality should spread. Such a desire may be unaccompanied by any word or deed—so what if words or deeds accompany it? Rather, a person must hate what Allah hates: committing sexual immorality, accusing others of it, and spreading it among the believers. Whoever approves of a people’s conduct will be gathered with them, just as Lot’s wife was gathered with them. She did not commit the indecency of intercourse between men, for a woman cannot do that. Yet because she approved of their conduct, the punishment encompassed her along with them.

It is in this connection that mention is made of those who assist sexual immorality and its spread, such as the procurer who leads women and boys into sexual immorality for the influence he gains or the illicit earnings on which he lives. The same applies to those whose occupations prosper through it, such as singers, wine-drinkers, and contractors for governmental and other establishments. They desire sexual immorality to spread so that they can fend off those believers who denounce it—something they cannot do when it is rare, limited in scale, and concealed.

There is no disagreement among Muslims that whatever encourages disobedience to Allah and discourages obedience to Him is forbidden and unlawful. Conversely, whatever does the opposite is obligatory, as Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, prayer restrains from indecency and wrongdoing, and the remembrance of Allah is greater.”

That is, the obedience to Allah, remembrance of Him, and compliance with His command that prayer contains are greater than its restraining effect.

---

## Page 7902

And concerning wine and gambling, He says:

“And avert you from the remembrance of Allah and from prayer.”

That is, these lead them into disobedience to Him—namely, enmity and hatred. This is among the gravest wrongs from which prayer restrains people. Wine incites indecency and wrongdoing, as experience shows: the wine-drinker feels an urge for sexual intercourse, whether lawful or unlawful. Allah Most High did not mention intercourse because wine does not specifically incite unlawful intercourse. Rather, the wine-drinker engages in whatever intercourse is available to him, whether lawful or unlawful. Intoxication deprives him of the reason by which he could distinguish between the lawful and the unlawful, whereas sound reason restrains a person from engaging in what is unlawful.

Thus, the wine-drinker engages in sexual immorality more frequently than in other wrongdoing. He may even have intercourse with his own daughter, son, or other relatives forbidden to him in marriage. Yet he may content himself with lawful intercourse when it is available. Drinking wine also leads to taking people’s wealth unlawfully through theft, robbery, and the like, because he needs wine and its accompanying indulgences: food, sexual immorality, singing, and other things.

Drinking wine exposes people’s secrets, as the drinker reveals what he conceals within himself. Many people, when they wish to discover the secrets men harbor in their hearts, give them wine to drink. They may also drink with them something that does not intoxicate them.

Furthermore, wine diverts a person from exercising his knowledge and judgment and attending to his welfare in this life and the Hereafter, and from all the affairs he manages through his judgment and reason. Thus, all the matters from which it diverts him

---

## Page 7903

that benefit him, and all the harms wine causes, are encompassed by the words of Allah Most High:

“And avert you from the remembrance of Allah and from prayer.”

Likewise, sowing enmity and hatred is Satan’s ultimate aim. Hence the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Shall I tell you of something higher in rank than prayer, fasting, charity, enjoining what is right, and forbidding what is wrong?” They replied, “Yes, O Messenger of Allah.” He said, “Mending relations between people, for corrupting relations is the razor. I do not mean that it shaves off hair; rather, it shaves away religion.”

We have explained elsewhere that indecencies, injustice, and other sins give rise to enmity and hatred, and that all enmity or hatred is rooted in disobedience to Allah. Satan commands disobedience in order to lead people into something worse, and, so long as he is able, he is never content to stop at any point.

Moreover, enmity and hatred are pure evil, which no rational person loves. Sins, by contrast, offer pleasure, as with wine and indecencies. People desire these things, and Satan entices them into them until he plunges them into an evil they neither desire nor want. Allah Most High has explained what Satan seeks through wine and gambling, without mentioning what people themselves seek. Then, in Surah al-Nūr, He says:

“O you who believe, do not follow in Satan’s footsteps. Whoever follows in Satan’s footsteps—indeed, he commands indecency and wrongdoing.”

---

## Page 7904

And He says in Surah al-Baqarah:

“Do not follow in Satan’s footsteps. Indeed, he is your sworn enemy.”

“He only commands you to evil and indecency, and to say about Allah what you do not know.”

Thus He forbids following Satan’s footsteps—that is, obeying his commands by imitating and following him—and states that he commands indecency, wrongdoing, evil, and speaking about Allah without knowledge. He also says in this surah:

“Satan threatens you with poverty and commands you to indecency, while Allah promises you forgiveness from Him and bounty.”

Satan threatens poverty and commands indecency, wrongdoing, and evil, whereas Allah promises forgiveness and bounty, commands justice, kindness, and giving to relatives, and forbids indecency, wrongdoing, and transgression. He says of His Prophet:

“He commands them to do what is right and forbids them from wrongdoing, makes lawful for them good things and prohibits foul things, and relieves them of their burden and the shackles that bound them.”

And He says of his community:

“They command what is right and forbid wrongdoing.”

He mentions similar things in many places.

Sometimes He mentions wrongdoing alone as the object of prohibition; sometimes He pairs it with indecency; and sometimes He adds transgression to both. Likewise, He sometimes mentions what is right alone as the object of a command, and sometimes mentions other things alongside it, as in His words, Most High:

“There is no good in much of their private conversation, except in the words of those who urge charity, an act of kindness, or reconciliation between people.”

This is because the scope of terms may be general or specific depending on whether they occur alone or together. Consider the terms “poor” and “needy”: when either occurs alone, it encompasses what both denote when used together. When they occur together, however, the meaning of each

---

## Page 7905

differs from that of the other and is narrower than its meaning when used alone. Moreover, a general term may be followed by mention of one of its particular kinds, singling it out for special attention. It has been said that this particular kind is then mentioned both under the general designation and under its own specific designation.

With this understood, the term “wrongdoing” encompasses everything Allah dislikes and forbids: everything He hates. The term “what is right” encompasses everything Allah loves, is pleased with, and commands. Thus, when mentioned separately, “what is right” encompasses everything loved in religion, and “wrongdoing” everything disliked.

When wrongdoing is mentioned alongside indecency, however, indecency is rooted in love and desire, whereas “wrongdoing” is what hearts reject. It might therefore be thought that the attraction involved in indecency excludes it from wrongdoing: although hearts reject it, souls desire it. It may be said that “wrongdoing” encompasses indecency. It may also be said that indecency is singled out because the desire involved in it provides such a powerful incentive. Or it may be said that “wrongdoing” here means what is rejected without qualification, while indecency is mentioned because it is desired and loved.

Likewise, “transgression” is mentioned alongside it because it is further removed from what souls love. This is why the punishment of one who commits it is of a more severe kind than the punishment of one who commits indecency. Transgression arises from the faculty of anger, just as indecency arises from the faculty of desire, and every soul takes pleasure in attaining what it seeks. Thus, indecent acts and transgression are mentioned alongside wrongdoing. As for associating partners with Allah and speaking about Allah without knowledge, these constitute wrongdoing

---

## Page 7906

pure and simple. Souls have no inclination toward either; rather, both arise only from obstinacy and injustice. According to innate human nature, both are sheer wrongdoing and injustice. These traits therefore corrupt the faculties of knowledge and action.

Prayer restrains indecency and wrongdoing. “Whoever follows Satan’s footsteps—he commands indecency and wrongdoing”: this holds whether the pronoun refers to Satan or to the one who follows Satan’s footsteps. For whoever commits indecency and wrongdoing is in the same position in either case: if Satan commanded him, then he follows, obeys, and worships him; and if the perpetrator is himself the one giving the command, commanding an act goes further than merely committing it. Whoever commands others to do such things approves of them for himself.

Among acts of indecency and wrongdoing is listening to Satan’s pipes. The singer is his herald, calling people to obey him, for singing is a charm that incites fornication. Speaking about Allah without knowledge is likewise a form of following Satan’s footsteps:

“Say, ‘Indeed, Allah does not command indecency. Do you say about Allah what you do not know?’”

Such is the condition of people of religious innovation and wickedness, and of many who deem it lawful to establish bonds of brotherhood with women and beardless youths, bring them to gatherings of singing, and profess to love their physical forms for Allah’s sake, along with other things by which many people have been tempted, becoming misguided themselves and misleading others.

Then He, glorified be He, forbade someone wronged by a slanderous accusation of unchastity from withholding the kindness he ought to show his relatives, the needy, and those who repent, and commanded him to pardon

---

## Page 7907

and overlook the wrong; for just as they wish Allah to forgive them, they should pardon, overlook, and forgive. Undoubtedly, maintaining kinship ties is obligatory, giving to the needy is obligatory, and assisting the emigrants is obligatory. One may not withhold obligatory kindness from someone merely because that person has wronged one and impugned one’s honor, just as a man is not deprived of his inheritance or his entitlement to charitable funds and fay’ revenues merely for committing a sin. Certain sins, however, may entail his being deprived of these.

The verse also indicates the obligation to maintain ties, provide financial support, and fulfill other duties toward relatives who are neither heirs with prescribed shares nor residuary heirs. For it is established in the Sahīh, in ‘Ā’ishah’s account of the slander, that Abū Bakr al-Siddīq swore that he would no longer support Mistah ibn Uthāthah, who was among those who spread the slander concerning ‘Ā’ishah. Mistah’s mother was the daughter of Abū Bakr’s maternal aunt. Allah included him among the relatives from whom He forbade withholding support. A prohibition entails that the act is unlawful. Thus, if swearing to refrain from an act is impermissible, that act must be obligatory, since swearing to refrain from a permissible act is permissible.

Section

Allah Most High says:

“Those who accuse chaste women and then fail to produce four witnesses—flog them with eighty lashes.”

And in the same surah He says:

“Those who accuse their wives”,

---

## Page 7908

“then fail to produce four witnesses—flog them with eighty lashes.”

And in the same surah He says:

“Why did they not produce four witnesses to it?”

He thus specified the number of witnesses but left their qualifications unrestricted. He did not stipulate that they must be from among us, or be acceptable to us, or possess upright character, as He stipulated qualifications for witnesses elsewhere.

Scholars therefore disagreed over whether the testimony of four witnesses that entails the prescribed punishment for the person guilty of unlawful sexual intercourse—such as the testimony of sinful and disobedient people and others—averts the prescribed punishment from the accuser. There are two views within Ahmad’s school.

One view is that it averts the prescribed punishment from the accuser, even if it does not establish the prescribed punishment for unlawful sexual intercourse against the accused. This is comparable to a husband testifying four times by Allah against his wife: this averts the prescribed punishment for slander from him, but his wife does not incur the prescribed punishment on that basis alone, since she can avert punishment from herself by testifying four times. If she does not testify, however, should she receive the prescribed punishment, be detained until she confesses or participates in the mutual imprecation, or be released? There is a well-known disagreement among scholars on this issue.

Thus, averting the prescribed punishment from the accuser does not necessarily entail imposing the prescribed punishment for unlawful sexual intercourse upon the accused. Both are prescribed punishments, and prescribed punishments are averted by doubts. The four testimonies provide strong grounds for doubt in the accuser’s favor. If the accused were to confess once, twice, or three times, the prescribed punishment would be averted from the accuser, yet, according to most scholars, those admissions would not make the prescribed punishment obligatory. Likewise, if the accused were not chaste—for example, someone notorious for sexual immorality—the accuser would not receive the prescribed punishment for slander, nor would the accused receive the prescribed punishment for unlawful sexual intercourse on the basis of widespread reports alone,

---

## Page 7909

although each may receive a punishment short of the prescribed penalty. The evidentiary threshold for the prescribed punishment for unlawful sexual intercourse has been set at four witnesses.

Their qualifications must likewise be taken into account: the prescribed punishment for unlawful sexual intercourse cannot be imposed on a Muslim except on the testimony of Muslims. Yet it may be said that He did not stipulate that they must be upright and acceptable, as He stipulated in the verse concerning debt when He said:

“From among those whom you accept as witnesses.”

In the verse concerning bequests, He said:

“Two just men from among you.”

And in the verse concerning taking back a divorced wife, He said:

“And call two just men from among you to witness, and establish the testimony for Allah.”

Allah, glorified be He, has thus commanded us to entrust the testimony we need to people who are upright and acceptable. These are the people who comply with what Allah has commanded them in His words:

“O you who believe, stand firmly for justice as witnesses for Allah, even against yourselves, your parents, or close relatives. Whether one is rich or poor, Allah has a greater claim over both. So do not follow your desires, lest you depart from justice”—to the end of the verse.

And in His words:

“When you speak, be just, even if it concerns a close relative.”

And His words:

“Do not conceal testimony.”

And His words:

“Let witnesses not refuse when they are called upon.”

And His words:

“And those who uphold their testimonies.”

They therefore bear witness justly for Allah, thereby fulfilling the purpose of the person who called upon them to witness.

The second consideration is that their testimony is accepted and heard because they are upright and acceptable. This indicates that these qualities are required both for testimony to be accepted and for it to be given. He, glorified be He, has forbidden accepting the testimony of a sinful person in His words:

“If a sinful person brings you news, verify it”—to the end of the verse. However, this explicitly establishes that when a single sinful person brings a report, it must be verified,

---

## Page 7910

whereas establishing the same for two or more sinful persons requires an additional premise. As for the number of witnesses they mention, scholars agree that it is not required for judgment in certain cases. According to the majority, judgment may sometimes be given without witnesses, as when an oath is refused or referred to the other party, and in similar circumstances. Judgment may also be given on the basis of one witness and an oath, as established by the Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, for he

“gave judgment on the basis of one witness and an oath.”

Abū Dāwūd and others narrated this from Abū Hurayrah. Muslim narrated from Ibn ‘Abbās:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, gave judgment on the basis of one witness and an oath.”

Others besides these two also narrated it. This is further supported by the fact that Allah did not stipulate this qualification when testimony is given, either in the verse concerning unlawful sexual intercourse or in the verse concerning accusations of it. Rather, He said:

“Call four witnesses from among you to testify against them.”

And He said:

“And those who accuse chaste women and then fail to produce four witnesses.”

He commanded verification only when a single sinful person brings a report; He did not command it when two sinful persons bring a report. For a report from two engenders a degree of belief that a report from one does not.

For this reason, the scholars said: If the judge has doubts about the witnesses, he should separate them and question them about the place, time, and circumstances of what they witnessed, how they acquired their knowledge of it, and other matters that reveal whether their accounts agree or differ.

As for the words of the Most High:

“And never accept their testimony,”

this explicitly establishes that the testimony of these accusers must never be accepted, whether they testify singly or in a group. Indeed, the wording of the verse encompasses them both collectively and individually. For the verse was revealed concerning those responsible for the slander, as agreed by scholars, hadith specialists, jurists, and Quranic commentators. And those who accused

---

## Page 7911

‘Ā’ishah were several people, not just one. They accused her when they saw her arrive in the company of Safwān ibn al-Mu‘attal al-Sulamī after the army had set off on its return journey. She had gone to look for a necklace she had lost. The men responsible for her howdah lifted it, believing she was inside because she was so light, although she was not there. When she returned, she found no one from the army, so she remained where she was. Safwān had lagged behind the army. When he saw her, he turned his face away from her and made his mount kneel so that she could ride it. He then took her to the army. His being alone with her was therefore a matter of necessity, just as a woman may travel without a mahram—a male relative whom she can never marry—out of necessity, as when emigrating for the sake of her faith. Examples include the arrival of Umm Kulthūm bint ‘Uqbah ibn Abī Mu‘ayt as an emigrant and this incident involving ‘Ā’ishah.

The verse establishes that the testimony of those who make such accusations is not accepted, whether they testify together or separately. It also establishes that their testimony is accepted after repentance, as is the position of the majority of scholars. Among them were Mistah ibn Uthāthah and Hassān ibn Thābit, as reported from ‘Ā’ishah in the authentic hadith collection. They also included Hamnah bint Jahsh and others.

It is known that neither the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, nor the Muslims after him rejected the testimony of any of them, for they all repented when the Quran was revealed declaring her innocence. Anyone who did not repent at that point would be a disbeliever who rejected the Quran, whereas these people remained Muslims. Allah also forbade cutting off acts of kindness towards them. Had their testimony been rejected after their repentance, this would have become widely known, just as ‘Umar’s rejection of Abū Bakrah’s testimony became widely known. The affair involving ‘Ā’ishah was far more momentous than that involving al-Mughīrah. Yet anyone who

---

## Page 7912

rejects the testimony of an accuser after repentance may say: “I reject the testimony of someone who has received the prescribed punishment for accusing another of illicit intercourse, whereas these people were not punished.” Those holding the former view offer several responses.

First, it is reported in the Sunan collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, inflicted the prescribed punishment on those people. Second, this condition is not recognized by the apparent meaning of the Quran, nor do they themselves maintain it, as established in its proper place.

Third, those who made the prescribed punishment a condition did so on the following grounds: “An accuser may be telling the truth or lying. The accused person’s failure to demand the prescribed punishment may therefore be because the accuser is telling the truth. But when the punishment is demanded and the accuser fails to produce four witnesses, his falsehood becomes evident.”

It is well known that the falsehood of those who accused ‘Ā’ishah became more evident than that of anyone else. For Allah Himself declared her innocence through His words, which He sent down from above the seven heavens and which are recited. If their testimony is accepted after their repentance, then the testimony of others who made such accusations against someone other than her is all the more deserving of acceptance.

The incident in which ‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb adjudicated among the Emigrants and the Helpers concerning al-Mughīrah also provides evidence for both rulings. Three men testified that he had committed illicit intercourse, while the fourth refrained from testifying, so ‘Umar flogged those three and rejected their testimony. This supports both rulings, just as the incident involving ‘Ā’ishah established that their testimony was accepted after repentance and flogging; for two of the three repented, so ‘Umar accepted

---

## Page 7913

their testimony, as did the Muslims. The third man, Abū Bakrah, despite being among the best of them, did not repent. Since he did not repent, the Muslims did not accept his testimony, although he was one of the righteous Muslims. ‘Umar had told him, “Repent, and I will accept your testimony.”

But since the Quran has made clear that accusers who fail to produce four witnesses must never have their testimony accepted, and then says:

“And it is they who are defiantly disobedient,”

“except for those who repent,”

it is evident that His words, “And it is they who are defiantly disobedient,” describe them in condemnatory terms beyond the rejection of their testimony already mentioned.

As for the meaning of the “uprightness” required of these witnesses, it is uprightness in religion and propriety of conduct. Uprightness consists in fulfilling obligations, avoiding major sins, and not persisting in minor sins. “Propriety of conduct” consists in observing what lends a person dignity and grace, and avoiding what sullies and disgraces him. A person possessing these qualities is an upright witness and one of the righteous and virtuous.

However, nothing in the Book of Allah or the Sunnah of His Messenger indicates that, in every place and time, no one may be called to witness a will or the revocation of a divorce unless he possesses these qualities. Rather, this describes a believer who has perfected his faith by fulfilling his obligations, even if he has not fulfilled all recommended acts. Such a person is one of Allah’s pious allies.

Moreover, those who hold this view may understand obligations to mean the five daily prayers and similar duties. Yet a person may owe duties to Allah and His servants that none can enumerate

---

## Page 7914

except Allah Most High, including duties whose neglect is a greater sin than drinking wine or unlawful sexual intercourse. Nevertheless, they have not regarded such neglect as impugning a person’s uprightness, either because they fail to appreciate how numerous the obligations are, or because they focus on avoiding sins rather than fulfilling obligations. This is not how matters stand in the Sharia. In short, this must be taken into account in matters of reward and punishment, praise and blame, allegiance and enmity. This is a matter of great importance.

As for the assertion, “Muslims are presumed upright,” it is false. Rather, the default condition of the children of Adam is injustice and ignorance, as Allah Most High says:

“But man undertook it. Indeed, he was unjust and ignorant.”

Merely uttering the two testimonies of faith does not move a person from injustice and ignorance to justice. And (the rules governing testimony hinge on the witness being acceptable, or being an upright person who strives for fairness and justice in his words and deeds, and for truthfulness in his testimony and reports. This is often found despite shortcomings in many of those qualities, just as the qualities they stipulate are often found without this. We have frequently encountered both kinds of people.

It may, however, be said that those qualities give grounds for expecting truthfulness, justice and the fulfilment of the purpose of testimony, and serve as evidence and signs of this. For the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in the hadith agreed to be authentic:

“Adhere to truthfulness, for truthfulness leads to righteousness, and righteousness leads to Paradise.”

And so on to the end of the hadith.

---

## Page 7915

Thus, truthfulness necessarily entails righteousness, just as lying necessarily entails wickedness. When the antecedent—striving to be truthful—is present, its necessary consequence—righteousness—is present. When the necessary consequence—righteousness—is absent, the antecedent—truthfulness—is absent. When lying, the antecedent, is present, wickedness, its necessary consequence, is present. When the necessary consequence—wickedness—is absent, the antecedent—lying—is absent. Hence, a man’s lack of righteousness is taken as evidence of his lying, and his freedom from wickedness as evidence of his truthfulness.

The upright person described by the jurists is someone free of wickedness, meaning the commission of a major sin or persistence in a minor sin. When these are absent in him, so too is the lying that leads him to such wickedness. The evildoer is someone who lacks righteousness; when his righteousness is absent, his truthfulness is absent as well. The inference drawn from this hadith rests on the premise that what prompts righteousness necessarily entails righteousness, and what prompts wickedness necessarily entails wickedness.

Error is thus like forgetfulness, and deliberate action is like lying. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 7916

In the course of discussing matters relating to this verse and others, Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—said concerning the words of Allah Most High:

“Indeed, those who falsely accuse chaste, unsuspecting, believing women are cursed in this world and the Hereafter, and theirs will be a tremendous punishment.”

As for the detailed answer, it has three aspects.

First: According to many scholars, this verse concerns the Prophet’s wives specifically, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Hushaym narrated from al-‘Awwām ibn Hawshab, who said: A shaykh from Banū Kāhil narrated to us:

Ibn ‘Abbās explained Sūrat al-Nūr. When he reached the verse, “Indeed, those who falsely accuse chaste, unsuspecting, believing women,” through to its end, he said:

“This concerns ‘Ā’ishah and the Prophet’s wives specifically, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. It is unqualified; it contains no provision for repentance. But whoever accuses a believing woman of unchastity, Allah has made repentance available to him.”

Then he recited, “Those who accuse chaste women and then fail to produce four witnesses,” through to His words, “Except those who repent thereafter and mend their ways.” Thus, He made repentance available to the latter, but not to the former.

The narrator said: A man was about to rise and kiss his head because of the excellence of his explanation.

---

## Page 7917

Abū Sa‘īd al-Ashajj also said:

‘Abd Allāh ibn Khirāsh narrated to us from al-‘Awwām, from Sa‘īd ibn Jubayr, that Ibn ‘Abbās said:

“The verse, ‘Indeed, those who falsely accuse chaste, unsuspecting women,’ was revealed specifically concerning ‘Ā’ishah, while the curse applies to the hypocrites generally.”

Ibn ‘Abbās thus explained that this verse was revealed specifically concerning those who accuse ‘Ā’ishah and the Mothers of the Believers of unchastity, because accusing them impugns and disparages the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Accusing a woman of unchastity harms her husband, just as it harms her son, for it imputes to him tolerance of sexual immorality in his household and portrays his marital bed as defiled. His wife’s adultery causes him immense harm. For this reason, the Lawgiver permitted him to accuse her if she commits adultery and allowed him to avert the prescribed punishment through mutual imprecatory oaths, while granting no one else permission to accuse a woman of unchastity under any circumstances. Indeed, the shame and disgrace some people suffer when their families are accused may be greater than what they would suffer if they themselves were accused.

For this reason, according to one of the two expressly transmitted positions from him, Imām Ahmad held that anyone who accuses a chaste woman—such as a slave woman or a non-Muslim woman under Muslim protection—whose husband or child is legally protected against accusations of unchastity is subject to the prescribed punishment for accusing her, because of the disgrace he brings upon her child and husband, who enjoy that protection. The other position transmitted from him, which is also the view of the majority, is that no prescribed punishment is due: the accusation harms them, but does not constitute an accusation of unchastity against them, and the full prescribed punishment is incurred only by such an accusation.

In the Prophet’s case, however, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, such harm is equivalent to accusing him of unchastity. Whoever intends to disparage the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, by disparaging his wives is a hypocrite. This is what Ibn ‘Abbās meant when he said, “The curse applies to the hypocrites generally.”

A number of scholars agreed with Ibn ‘Abbās. Imām Ahmad and al-Ashajj narrated from Khasīf

---

## Page 7918

who said:

I asked Sa‘īd ibn Jubayr, “Which is graver: adultery or accusing a chaste woman of unchastity?”

He replied, “No; rather, adultery.”

I said, “Yet Allah Most High says: ‘Indeed, those who accuse chaste, unsuspecting, believing women are cursed in this world and the Hereafter.’”

He replied, “This applied specifically to ‘Ā’ishah.”

Ahmad also narrated through his chain of transmission that Abū al-Jawzā’ said concerning this verse—“Indeed, those who accuse chaste, unsuspecting, believing women are cursed in this world and the Hereafter”—“This verse applies specifically to the Mothers of the Believers.”

Al-Ashajj narrated through his chain of transmission that al-Dahhāk said concerning this verse, “They are the wives of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.”

Ma‘mar narrated from al-Kalbī: “This verse refers only to the wives of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. As for anyone who accuses a Muslim woman of unchastity, he is a transgressor, as Allah Most High has said, unless he repents.”

The reasoning behind this is that merely accusing someone of unchastity does not warrant Allah’s curse in this world and the Hereafter. The definite article in His words, “chaste, unsuspecting, believing women,” would therefore identify particular women already in view. Those in view here are the wives of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, since the passage concerns the incident of the slander and those who maligned the Mother of the Believers, ‘Ā’ishah. Alternatively, the general wording is restricted to the occasion that prompted it, on the strength of evidence requiring such a restriction.

This view is supported by the fact that Allah, glorified is He, attached this threat to accusing chaste, unsuspecting, believing women of unchastity, whereas He said at the beginning of the surah:

“Those who accuse chaste women and then fail to produce four witnesses—flog them with eighty lashes.”

And so the verse continues. Thus, He made the prescribed punishment, the rejection of testimony, and the designation as a transgressor consequences of merely accusing chaste women of unchastity. It must therefore be the case that

---

## Page 7919

chaste, unsuspecting, believing women possess a distinction beyond that of women who are merely chaste. This—and Allah knows best—is because the faith of the Prophet’s wives, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is attested to: they are the Mothers of the Believers and the wives of His Prophet in this world and the Hereafter. Of Muslim women generally, however, what is usually known is only their outward profession of faith.

Moreover, Allah, glorified is He, said concerning the account of ‘Ā’ishah:

“And the one among them who took the leading part in it will have a great punishment.”

His singling out the one who took the leading part, rather than the others, indicates that the great punishment applies specifically to him. He also said:

“Had it not been for Allah’s favour upon you and His mercy in this world and the Hereafter, a great punishment would have afflicted you for what you had engaged in.”

Thus, it is understood that the great punishment does not afflict everyone who makes an accusation of unchastity, but only the one who takes the leading part in it. Here, however, He said:

“And they will have a great punishment.”

It is therefore understood that the one who accuses the Mothers of the Believers thereby disparages His Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and takes the leading part in the slander. This describes the hypocrite Ibn Ubayy. And Allah knows best.

According to this view, this verse also constitutes evidence consistent with that other verse: since accusing the Mothers of the Believers causes harm to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, the perpetrator is cursed in this world and the Hereafter. This is why Ibn ‘Abbās said, “There is no repentance for this,” because the repentance of one who harms the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is not accepted. Or he may mean that, if such a person repents of the accusation, his repentance is not accepted until he embraces Islam anew.

On this understanding, accusing them of unchastity constitutes hypocrisy that renders the offender liable to death, if it is intended to harm the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or is done with the knowledge that they are his wives in the Hereafter. For no prophet’s wife has ever committed adultery.

---

## Page 7920

Further evidence that accusing them of unchastity harms the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the report recorded in the two Sahīh collections in the account of the slander. ‘Ā’ishah said:

“Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stood up and asked to be excused for taking action against ‘Abd Allāh ibn Ubayy ibn Salūl. Standing on the pulpit, Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, ‘O Muslims! Who will excuse me for taking action against a man whose abuse of my household has reached me? By Allah, I know nothing but good of my wife. They have also mentioned a man of whom I know nothing but good, and he never entered my home except in my company.’

“Sa‘d ibn Mu‘ādh al-Ansārī stood up and said, ‘I will excuse you, O Messenger of Allah. If he belongs to al-Aws, we will strike off his head. If he belongs to our brothers of al-Khazraj, give us your command, and we will carry it out.’

“Sa‘d ibn ‘Ubādah then stood up. He was the chief of al-Khazraj and a righteous man, but tribal zeal carried him away. He said to Sa‘d ibn Mu‘ādh, ‘By Allah’s life, you shall not kill him, nor will you be able to kill him!’

“Usayd ibn Hudayr, the cousin of Sa‘d ibn Mu‘ādh, then stood up and said to Sa‘d ibn ‘Ubādah, ‘You are wrong! By Allah’s life, we shall certainly kill him! You are a hypocrite, arguing on behalf of the hypocrites.’

“The two tribes, al-Aws and al-Khazraj, became so agitated that they were on the verge of fighting one another, while Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was standing on the pulpit. Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, continued calming them until they fell silent, and he too fell silent.”

Another authentic narration states that this verse concerns the wives of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, specifically. Others say that it refers to the wives of the believers generally. Abū Salamah said:

“Accusing

---

## Page 7921

chaste women of unchastity is among the sins that incur punishment.” He then recited:

“Indeed, those who accuse chaste women,” and the rest of the verse.

‘Umar ibn Qays said: “Accusing a chaste woman of unchastity nullifies ninety years of deeds.” Al-Ashajj transmitted both reports.

This is the view held by many. Its basis is the apparent meaning of the wording: it is general and must therefore be applied generally, since there is no reason to restrict it. There is agreement that it is not confined to the particular occasion of its revelation, because the ruling concerning the Prophet’s wives other than ‘Ā’ishah falls within its general scope, although they were not involved in that occasion. Moreover, the wording is plural, whereas the occasion here concerned a single woman.

Furthermore, restricting the Quran’s general statements to the occasions of their revelation is invalid. Most verses were revealed in response to circumstances that called for them, yet it is known that none has been confined to its particular occasion.

The distinction between the two verses is that, at the beginning of the surah, Allah mentioned the prescribed sanctions to be implemented by legally accountable people: flogging, rejection of testimony, and declaring the offender to be an evildoer. Here, however, He mentioned the punishment inflicted by Allah, glorified is He: being cursed in both this world and the Hereafter, and a tremendous punishment.

It has been reported through several chains from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and from his Companions that:

“Accusing chaste women of unchastity is one of the major sins.”

One wording in the Sahīh reads:

“Accusing chaste, unsuspecting believing women of unchastity.”

Those holding this view then differed. Abū Hamzah al-Thumālī said: “We have been informed that it was revealed concerning the polytheists of Mecca, when a treaty existed between them and Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Whenever a woman left to join Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in Medina

---

## Page 7922

as an emigrant, the polytheists of Mecca would accuse her of unchastity, saying, ‘She has only left to commit fornication.’”

On this interpretation, the verse concerns those who accuse believing women of unchastity to turn them away from faith, intending thereby to disparage the believers and repel people from Islam, as Ka‘b ibn al-Ashraf did. Accordingly, whoever does this is a disbeliever and is like one who insults the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

His statement that it was revealed during the treaty period means—and Allah knows best—that he was referring to people like those polytheists bound by a treaty. Otherwise, this verse was revealed during the days of the slander, which occurred during the campaign against Banū al-Mustaliq, before the Battle of the Trench, whereas the truce came years later.

Others understood the verse according to its apparent, general meaning, because the occasion of its revelation was the accusation against ‘Ā’ishah, and those who accused her included both believers and hypocrites. The particular occasion of revelation must fall within the general wording, and there is no reason to restrict its scope.

The answer, on this understanding, is that He, glorified is He, says here:

“They have been cursed in this world and the Hereafter.”

The verb is passive, and the one pronouncing the curse is not named. In the other verse, however, He says:

“Indeed, those who offend Allah and His Messenger—Allah has cursed them in this world and the Hereafter.”

When the agent is not named, it is possible that those cursing them are others besides Allah, whether angels or human beings. It is also possible that Allah curses them at one time and some of His creatures curse them at another. Or Allah Himself may curse some of them—those whose accusations constitute an attack on the religion—while His creatures curse the others. If the one pronouncing the curse is a created being, his curse may carry the meaning of

---

## Page 7923

supplication against them, or it may mean that they distance them from Allah’s mercy.

This is supported by the fact that when a man accuses his wife of unchastity, the two undergo the prescribed exchange of oaths, and in his fifth oath the husband says: “May Allah’s curse be upon him if he is lying.” He thus prays that Allah curse him if his accusation is false. Likewise, Allah commanded His Messenger to invoke His curse upon those who disputed with him concerning the Messiah after knowledge had come to him: they were to supplicate earnestly and invoke Allah’s curse upon the liars. This is one way in which an accuser is cursed. Other ways include his being flogged, having his testimony rejected, and being declared a transgressor. These punish him and exclude him from places of security and acceptance, which are manifestations of Allah’s mercy. This differs from the case of someone whom Allah declares He has cursed in this world and the Hereafter: Allah’s curse upon him entails the loss of all support and his removal from the means of attaining mercy in both abodes.

Further supporting this distinction is His statement:

“Indeed, those who offend Allah and His Messenger—Allah has cursed them in this world and the Hereafter and prepared for them a humiliating punishment.”

The Quran speaks of a humiliating punishment being prepared only in relation to disbelievers, as in His statements:

“Those who are miserly, urge others to be miserly, and conceal what Allah has given them of His bounty. And We have prepared for the disbelievers a humiliating punishment.”

“Take your precautions. Indeed, Allah has prepared for the disbelievers a humiliating punishment.”

“So they incurred wrath upon wrath, and for the disbelievers there is a humiliating punishment.”

“We only grant them respite so that they may increase in sin, and for them there is a humiliating punishment.”

“And those who disbelieve and reject Our signs—for them there is a humiliating punishment.”

“And when he learns of Our signs

---

## Page 7924

anything, he makes a mockery of them. For such people there is a humiliating punishment.”

“We have sent down clear signs, and for the disbelievers there is a humiliating punishment.”

“They have taken their oaths as a shield and barred others from Allah’s path, so for them there is a humiliating punishment.”

As for His statement, Exalted is He:

“Whoever disobeys Allah and His Messenger and transgresses His limits, He will admit him into a Fire, abiding therein forever, and for him there is a humiliating punishment.”

This concerns—and Allah knows best—someone who denies the prescribed obligations and treats them with contempt. Moreover, He did not state that the punishment had been prepared for him.

A great punishment, however, has been mentioned as a warning to believers in His statement:

“Had it not been for a prior decree from Allah, a great punishment would have touched you for what you took.”

And His statement:

“Were it not for Allah’s bounty upon you and His mercy in this world and the Hereafter, a great punishment would have touched you for what you engaged in.”

Concerning the one who wages war, He says:

“That is a disgrace for them in this world, and for them in the Hereafter there is a great punishment.”

Concerning the murderer:

“Allah is angry with him, has cursed him, and has prepared for him a great punishment.”

And His statement:

“Do not use your oaths to deceive one another, lest a foot slip after being firmly planted, and you taste the evil consequences of having barred others from Allah’s path, and for you there is a great punishment.”

He, Glorified is He, has also said:

“Whomever Allah humiliates, none can honour.”

This is because humiliation entails abasement, contempt and disgrace—something beyond the pain of punishment itself. An honourable man may be punished without being humiliated. Thus, when He says in this verse:

“And He has prepared for them a humiliating punishment,”

it is clear that this belongs to the same kind of punishment with which He has threatened the disbelievers and hypocrites. But when He says in the other passage: “And for them there is a punishment

---

## Page 7925

that is great,” it may be of the same kind as the punishment mentioned in His statement:

“A great punishment would have touched you for what you engaged in.”

Another point that clarifies the distinction is that He, Glorified is He, said in that passage:

“And He has prepared for them a humiliating punishment.”

Punishment has only been prepared for the disbelievers. Hell was created for them, for they will inevitably enter it and will never be brought out of it. As for believers who commit major sins, they may enter it if Allah forgives them; and if they enter it, they will be brought out of it, even if only after some time. He, Glorified is He, said:

“And guard yourselves against the Fire prepared for the disbelievers.”

He, Glorified is He, thus commanded the believers not to consume usury, to fear Allah, and to guard themselves against the Fire prepared for the disbelievers. This shows that there is reason to fear that they may enter the Fire if they consume usury and commit sins, even though it has been prepared for the disbelievers, not for them.

Hence, the hadith states:

“As for the inhabitants of the Fire who truly belong there, they will neither die nor live therein. But there are people who have sins and will be scorched by the Fire; then Allah will bring them out of it.”

Similarly, Paradise has been prepared for the Allah-fearing, who spend in prosperity and adversity, even though children do not enter it by virtue of their fathers’ deeds, while some people enter it through intercession and others through mercy. In the Hereafter, Allah will bring other beings into existence for whatever space remains in it and admit them into it. This is because a thing is prepared for those who merit and deserve it, and who have the strongest claim to it; yet others may enter along with them as their dependants or for another reason. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 7926

Shaykh al-Islām also said:

Section

Allah Most High said:

“O you who believe, do not enter houses other than your own until you have sought permission and greeted their inhabitants,”

up to His words:

“Tell the believing men to lower their gaze.”

It is authentically established that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Permission to enter is required only on account of what one might see.”

The prohibited looking is looking at private parts, or looking with desire even at what is not a private part. Allah, Glorified is He, mentioned two kinds of seeking permission. He mentioned one in this verse, and the second in the two verses towards the end of the surah: permission sought by children and slaves. Allah Most High said:

“O you who believe, let those whom your right hands possess and those among you who have not reached puberty ask your permission on three occasions: before the dawn prayer, when you put aside your clothes at midday, and after the night prayer. These are three times of privacy for you. Beyond these times, there is no blame on you or on them.”

He thus commanded children and slaves to seek permission when waking from sleep and when intending to go to sleep,

---

## Page 7927

and at the time of the midday rest; for it is at these times that private parts may be exposed, as Allah Most High said:

“These are three times of privacy for you.”

This indicates that neither a slave capable of discernment nor a child capable of discernment may look at a man’s private parts, just as a man may not look at the private parts of a child, a slave, or anyone else. Their entering without permission outside these times is permitted on the basis of His words:

“Beyond these times, there is no blame on you or on them, as you move about among one another.”

This indicates that concessions are allowed regarding those who move about among you, male and female, which are not allowed regarding others. One who moves about in this way is someone who enters without permission, as a cat does, and as a child or slave does. If this applies to a child capable of discernment, then it applies all the more to one who has not yet attained discernment.

Concessions are also allowed regarding ritual purity. A group of jurists among Ahmad’s followers and others have said concerning children, cats, and others that if impurity gets on them, the affected area becomes pure through saliva passing over it and does not need washing. This is because they are among those who move about among people, as the Messenger stated concerning cats, despite knowing that they eat mice. There were no sources of water in Medina to which cats went, such that one could say that a cat’s mouth had become pure by its going to the water. It is thus understood that these mouths do not require washing to become pure. Thus, the permission mentioned at the beginning of the surah must be sought before entering

---

## Page 7928

the house in all cases. The distinction made at the end of the surah is due to necessity, since a slave or child frequently comes and goes and needs to enter the house at any time. Requiring permission on every occasion would therefore cause hardship, unlike the case of someone who has reached puberty.

Allah Most High said:

“Tell the believing men to lower their gaze and guard their private parts. That is purer for them.”

The passage continues up to His words:

“And turn to Allah in repentance, all of you, O believers, so that you may succeed.”

Allah, glorified is He, commanded both men and women to lower their gaze and guard their private parts, just as He commanded them all to repent. He specifically commanded women to cover themselves and not to display their adornment except to their husbands and those whom Allah Most High excepted in the verse. The adornment that appears openly means the outer garments. There is no blame upon a woman for displaying these, provided this involves nothing else prohibited, since displaying them is unavoidable. This is the view of Ibn Masʿūd and others, and it is the well-known position reported from Ahmad.

Ibn ʿAbbās said: “The face and hands are part of the outward adornment.” This is the second position reported from Ahmad, and it is the view of a group of scholars, including al-Shāfiʿī and others.

Allah, glorified is He, commanded women to draw their outer garments down over themselves so that they would not be recognized or harmed. This supports the first view. ʿAbīda al-Salmānī and others mentioned that believing women would draw their outer garments down over themselves from above their heads, leaving only their eyes visible so that they could see the road. It is established in an authentic hadith:

“A woman in a state of ritual consecration is prohibited from wearing a face veil and gloves.”

This is among the indications that the face veil

---

## Page 7929

and gloves were familiar among women who were not in a state of ritual consecration, which entails covering their faces and hands.

Allah Most High prohibited anything that would make hidden adornment known through sound or otherwise. He said:

“And let them not stamp their feet to make known what they conceal of their adornment.”

He also said:

“Let them draw their head coverings over their chests.”

When this was revealed, the believing women took their head coverings, tore them, and let them hang down over their necks. The “neck opening” is a slit running lengthwise down the tunic. Thus, when a woman draws her head covering over this opening, she covers her neck.

She was subsequently commanded to let her outer garment hang down over herself. This applies when she leaves the house; while she is at home, she is not commanded to do so. It is established in an authentic hadith:

“When the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, consummated his marriage to Safiyya, his Companions said: ‘If he draws a veil over her, she is one of the Mothers of the Believers; if he does not place a veil over her, she is one of those whom his right hand possesses.’ So he placed a veil over her.”

Veiling was prescribed for women so that their faces and hands would not be seen. It applies specifically to free women, not slave women. Such was the practice of the believers during the time of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his caliphs: a free woman would veil herself, whereas a slave woman would appear openly. Whenever Umar, may Allah be pleased with him, saw a slave woman wearing a head covering, he would strike her and say: “Are you imitating free women, you wretch?” Thus, a slave woman’s head, hands, and face are left uncovered.

---

## Page 7930

Allah Most High also said:

“As for elderly women who no longer hope for marriage, there is no blame on them if they lay aside their outer garments without displaying their adornment. But it is better for them to refrain.”

He thus permitted an elderly woman who no longer hopes to marry to lay aside her outer garments: she need not drape her outer cloak over herself or veil herself. This, however, is an exception among free women, because the harm present in the case of others is absent in hers. Similarly, He made an exception for male attendants who have no sexual desire, allowing women to display their adornment before them, because they lack the desire that gives rise to temptation.

Likewise, if a slave woman is feared to be a source of temptation, she must draw her outer cloak over herself and veil herself. Others must lower their gaze from her, and she must lower hers. Neither the Book nor the Sunnah gives general permission to look at slave women, or for them to go unveiled and display their adornment. Rather, the Quran did not command them to do what it commanded free women to do, and the Sunnah distinguished between them and free women in practice, not through a generally worded statement. The customary practice among the believers was that free women veiled themselves from men, whereas slave women did not.

The Quran made an exception for elderly free women, not requiring them to veil themselves. It also made an exception for certain men—those without sexual desire—not prohibiting women from displaying their hidden adornment before them, because desire is absent in both these groups. There is therefore all the more reason to make an exception for certain slave women: those whose going unveiled and displaying their adornment gives rise to desire and temptation.

And just as, in the case of unmarriageable male relatives—such as their husbands’ sons and the like—who feel desire and infatuation, it is not permissible

---

## Page 7931

for women to display their hidden adornment before them, so the general wording of the address reflects customary circumstances. Whatever departs from those circumstances is therefore treated differently from otherwise comparable cases. Thus, if a slave woman’s appearing unveiled and being looked at gives rise to temptation, this must be prohibited, just as it must in other cases. The same applies to men among men and women among women.

If a woman were a source of temptation to other women, or a man to other men, the command to lower one’s gaze would apply to the person looking, just as the command to guard one’s chastity applies. Likewise, when slave women and boys are beautiful and looking at them is feared to cause temptation, the same ruling applies to them, as the scholars have stated.

Al-Marwadhī said: “I asked Abū ‘Abd Allāh—meaning Ahmad ibn Hanbal—‘What about a man looking at a male slave?’ He replied, ‘If he fears temptation, he must not look at him. How many a glance has brought affliction into the heart of the one who cast it!’”

Al-Marwadhī also said: “I said to Abū ‘Abd Allāh, ‘A man has repented and says, “Even if my back were lashed with whips, I would not commit a sin,” yet he does not stop looking.’ He replied, ‘What kind of repentance is this?’”

Jarīr said: “I asked the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about an inadvertent glance. He replied, ‘Turn your gaze away.’”

Ibn Abī al-Dunyā said: “My father and Suwayd both narrated to me, saying: Ibrāhīm ibn Hurāsah narrated to me, from ‘Uthmān ibn Sālih, from al-Hasan ibn Dhakwān, who said, ‘Do not keep company with the sons of the wealthy, for their looks resemble those of women, and they are a greater source of temptation than maidens.’”

This is inference and analogical reasoning, pointing from the lesser case to the greater. It used to be said:

---

## Page 7932

“A man should not spend the night in a house with a beardless youth.”

Ibn Abī al-Dunyā related through his chain of transmission that Abū Sahl al-Su‘lūkī said: “There will be people in this community called sodomites, divided into three groups: those who look, those who shake hands, and those who commit the act itself.”

Ibrāhīm al-Nakha‘ī said: “They used to disapprove of keeping company with the wealthy and the sons of kings.” He also said: “Keeping their company is a temptation; they are just like women.”

A young woman of unsurpassed beauty stopped before Bishr al-Hāfī and asked him for directions to Bāb Harb, and he directed her. Then a handsome youth stopped before him and asked him for directions to Bāb Harb, but he lowered his head. The youth repeated his question, and Bishr closed his eyes. He was asked: “Abū Nasr, a young woman came and asked you, and you answered her; yet this youth came and asked you, and you did not speak to him.” He replied: “Yes.
