It is related that Sufyān al-Thawrī said: ‘With a young woman there is one devil, but with a youth there are two devils.’ So I feared for myself on account of his two devils.”

Abū al-Shaykh al-Qazwīnī related through his chain of transmission that Bishr said: “Beware of these youths.” Fath al-Mawsilī said: “I accompanied thirty elders who were counted among the abdāl. Whenever I parted from one of them, he would advise me: ‘Beware of keeping company with youths; beware of associating with youths.’”

Sufyān al-Thawrī would not allow a beardless youth to sit with him. Mālik ibn Anas used to bar beardless youths from attending his sessions to hear hadith. Hishām, who was still beardless, therefore contrived to enter among the crowd, concealed by those around him, and heard sixteen hadith from him. Mālik was informed of this and gave him sixteen lashes. Hishām said: “If only I had heard

---

## Page 7933

a hundred hadith and he had given me a hundred lashes.” He used to say:

“This is knowledge that we received only from bearded men and elders; none but their like shall transmit it from us.”

Yahyā ibn Ma‘īn said: “No beardless youth ever hoped to accompany either me or Ahmad ibn Hanbal on the road.”

Abū ‘Alī al-Rūdhbārī said:

“Abū al-‘Abbās Ahmad ibn al-Mu’addib said to me: ‘Abū ‘Alī, where did the Sufis of our time acquire this familiarity with youths—even if they may come to no harm in many instances?’

“He replied: ‘Far from it! We have seen men stronger in faith than they who, upon seeing a youth approach, would flee from him as they would flee from a lion. Such things depend on the times when spiritual states overwhelm those experiencing them, and those states come under the sway of natural inclinations. How frequent the errors! How frequent the mistakes!’”

Al-Junayd ibn Muhammad said: “A man came to Ahmad ibn Hanbal with a handsome, beardless youth. Ahmad asked him: ‘Who is this young man?’ The man replied: ‘My son.’ Ahmad said: ‘Do not bring him with you again.’ Some of his companions reproached him for this, and Ahmad replied: ‘This is how we saw our teachers behave, and this is what they related to us about their predecessors.’”

Hasan ibn al-Rāzī came to Ahmad with a handsome youth and spoke with him for a while. When he was about to leave, Ahmad said to him:

“Abū ‘Alī, do not walk along the road with this youth.”

He replied: “Abū ‘Abd Allāh, he is my sister’s son.”

Ahmad said: “Even so. Do not let people incur sin on your account.”

Ibn al-Jawzī related through his chain of transmission from

---

## Page 7934

Sa‘īd ibn al-Musayyib, who said: “If you see a man persistently gazing at a beardless youth, regard him with suspicion.”

Several weak reports with chains traced back to the Prophet have been transmitted concerning this, along with a report narrated directly from the Prophet by a Successor that is better than them. This is what Abū Muhammad al-Khallāl related: ‘Umar ibn Shāhīn narrated to us; Muhammad ibn Abī Sa‘īd al-Muqrī narrated to us; Ahmad ibn Hammād al-Missīsī narrated to us; ‘Abbās ibn Mujawwiz narrated to us; Abū Usāmah narrated to us, from Mujālid, from Sa‘īd, from al-Sha‘bī, who said:

“The delegation of ‘Abd al-Qays came to the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Among them was a strikingly handsome beardless youth. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, seated him behind his back and said: ‘David’s sin lay in his gaze.’”

This is a rejected report. As for the reports with chains traced back to the Prophet, they include one related by Ibn al-Jawzī through his chain from Abū Hurayrah, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“Whoever looks at a beardless youth with improper intent, Allah will confine him in the Fire for forty years.”

Al-Khatīb al-Baghdādī also related through his chain from Anas that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Do not sit in the company of the sons of kings, for souls yearn for them as they do not yearn for young maidens.”

There are other weak hadiths of this kind.

The same applies to a woman with another woman, and likewise to a woman’s mahrams (men she may not marry), such as her husband’s son and his son, her brother’s son, her sister’s son, and her male slave—according to those who consider him a mahram. Whenever temptation is feared for him or for her, she should screen herself from view; indeed, she must. These situations in which Allah Most High has commanded screening from view are ones in which temptation is likely. For this reason, He, Most High, said:

---

## Page 7935

“That is purer for them.”

Purity and cleanliness may be attained without this, but this is purer. When looking and appearing before others lack purity and cleanliness because of desire in the heart and pleasure taken in looking, refraining from looking and screening oneself from view are all the more obligatory. There can be no purity without safeguarding one’s private parts against sexual immorality. Such safeguarding includes protecting them from unlawful vaginal or anal intercourse and lesser sexual acts, from direct bodily contact, from being touched by others, from being exposed to others, and from others looking at them. One must therefore protect one’s private parts from the gaze and touch of others.

For this reason, in the hadith narrated by Bahz ibn Hakīm from his father, from his grandfather, the latter asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Messenger of Allah, regarding our private parts, what may we expose and what must we keep covered?”

He replied: “Guard your private parts except from your wife or those whom your right hand possesses.”

He asked: “What about when people are crowded together?”

He replied: “If you can ensure that no one sees them, then let no one see them.”

He asked: “What if one of us is alone?”

He replied: “Allah is more entitled to your modesty than people are.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also “forbade a woman to lie in direct bodily contact with another woman under a single covering, and a man to lie in direct bodily contact with another man under a single covering.” He “forbade walking about naked” and “forbade a man to look at another man’s private parts and a woman to look at another woman’s private parts.”

He said: “Whoever believes in Allah and the Last Day must not enter the bathhouse except wearing a waist-wrapper.”

In another version: “Any woman of my community who believes in Allah and the Last Day must not enter the bathhouse except wearing a waist-wrapper.”

---

## Page 7936

The scholars have said that women are permitted to enter the bathhouse when needed, just as men are, provided they lower their gaze and keep their private parts covered. This includes, for example, a woman who is ill, has recently given birth, or must perform a ritual bath that she cannot perform anywhere except in the bathhouse. If, however, she has become accustomed to the bathhouse and finds it difficult to give it up, there are two opinions within the school of Ahmad and elsewhere concerning whether she may continue: one forbids it, while the other permits it. The latter is the position of Abū Hanīfah and was preferred by Ibn al-Jawzī.

Just as lowering the gaze includes refraining from looking at those parts of another person’s body that must be covered, and at other forbidden sights, it also includes refraining from looking into people’s homes. A person’s home conceals his body just as his clothing does. Allah, glorified be He, mentioned lowering the gaze and guarding one’s private parts after the verse concerning seeking permission to enter. This is because homes provide concealment, like the clothes worn on the body. He likewise mentioned both kinds of covering together in His words, exalted be He:

“And Allah has made shade for you from what He has created, and has made shelters for you in the mountains, and has made garments for you to protect you from the heat, and garments to protect you in battle.”

Each provides protection against harm, whether from harmful exposure to heat, sunlight, and cold, or from people through their gaze, their hands, and other means. At the beginning of Sūrat al-Nahl, He mentioned the fundamental blessings, mentioning here what wards off cold, since cold is among the things that can destroy life. Later in the surah, He mentioned the completion of His blessings and what wards off heat, since heat is among the things that cause discomfort. Then He said:

“Thus He perfects His blessing upon you, so that you may

---

## Page 7937

submit to Him.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Abū Hurayrah reported that he heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

“If someone peers into your house without your permission, and you flick a pebble at him and put out his eye, you incur no blame.”

This specific ruling clarifies the general prohibition reported in the Sahīh collection from ‘Abdullāh ibn Mughaffal, who saw a man flicking pebbles and said: “Do not flick pebbles, for the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade it.” The Prophet said: “It neither catches game nor wounds an enemy, but it can break a tooth and put out an eye.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Sahl ibn Sa‘d reported:

“A man peered through an opening in the Prophet’s door while the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was scratching his head with a hair pick. He said: ‘Had I known that you were looking at me, I would have stabbed you in the eye with it. Seeking permission to enter was prescribed precisely to prevent such looking.’”

Some scholars have considered this a case of repelling an assailant: the onlooker transgresses by looking and is therefore repelled like any other aggressor. Had the matter been as they said, he would have been repelled by the least forceful means first, resorting gradually to stronger measures; and it would not have been permissible to put out his eye at the outset if he would leave only by that means. The texts, however, contradict this, for the Prophet permitted flicking a pebble at him and putting out his eye before ordering him to leave. Likewise, he said:

“Had I known that you were looking at me, I would have stabbed you in the eye with it.”

He thus made the act of looking itself sufficient grounds for stabbing the eye, without mentioning an order to leave. This indicates that it is a punishment for that act: having committed this violation of the sanctity of the householder’s home, the householder may put out his eye with pebbles or a hair pick.

---

## Page 7938

Looking at the parts of the body that must be covered is forbidden and falls under Allah’s words:

“Say, ‘My Lord has only forbidden indecencies’”

and His words:

“Do not approach indecencies.”

Although “indecencies” most obviously refers to vaginal or anal intercourse and the associated acts of touching, looking, and so forth—as in the account of Lot:

“Do you commit an indecency such as no one in all the worlds has committed before you?”

“Do you commit indecency while you can see?”

and His words:

“Do not approach unlawful sexual intercourse. Indeed, it is an indecency”—

the term “indecency” also includes uncovering the parts of the body that must be covered, even without sexual contact. Thus Allah says:

“When they commit an indecency, they say, ‘We found our fathers doing it.’”

This indecency was their practice of circumambulating the House naked. They used to say, “We will not circumambulate in clothes in which we have disobeyed Allah.” The Hums, however, circumambulated in their clothes. Anyone else who obtained clothes from the Hums circumambulated in them; otherwise, he circumambulated naked. If he circumambulated in his own clothes, they became forbidden to him, so he discarded them. They were therefore called liqāʾ (discarded garments).

Likewise, if a woman could not obtain clothes, she placed one hand over her genitals and the other over her anus and circumambulated, saying:

“Today some of it, or all, is bare;
Whatever shows, I do not permit.”

Allah called this an indecency. His words in this context:

“Say, ‘My Lord has only forbidden indecencies, whether open or hidden’”

also encompass uncovering and displaying the parts of the body that must be covered. This is further confirmed by the fact that describing the sexual act in explicit language is called obscenity and obscene speech. Exposing the intimate parts and the act to sight is therefore like exposing them to hearing.

---

## Page 7939

Both forms of exposure are termed “description,” as the Prophet, peace be upon him, said:

“A woman must not describe another woman to her husband so vividly that it is as though he were looking at her.”

Thus one says, “So-and-so describes another person,” and “A garment describes the skin beneath it.” Making such things apparent to hearing or sight is permissible when needed. Indeed, it is recommended if something recommended or obligatory cannot otherwise be accomplished. Examples include the Prophet’s words to Māʿiz, “Did you fuck her?” and his saying:

“If anyone raises the tribal rallying cry of the pre-Islamic era, tell him to bite his father’s member, and do not use euphemisms.”

The point is that indecency encompasses both the reprehensible act itself and exposing that act and the bodily parts involved. Likewise, it encompasses what is indecent even when undertaken through a marriage contract, as Allah Most High says:

“Do not marry women whom your fathers married, except what has already passed. Indeed, it was an indecency, a hateful thing, and an evil way.”

He thus declares this marriage an indecency. It has also been said that this is one of the hidden indecencies. It is therefore clear that indecency encompasses indecent contracts just as it encompasses engaging in indecent sexual acts, for His words,

“Do not marry women whom your fathers married,”

encompass both the marriage contract and intercourse.

His words,

“Whether open or hidden,”

are comprehensive, encompassing many kinds of speech and action. Allah Most High commands that the private parts be guarded without qualification, saying:

“And guard their private parts,”

and:

“And those who guard their private parts, except with their spouses or those whom their right hands possess,”

and the subsequent verses. He also says:

“And the men who guard their private parts and the women who do so.”

Guarding the private parts is like what is expressed in His words, “Those who observe the limits set by Allah.” Guarding them means keeping them from whatever is unlawful.

---

## Page 7940

As for the eyes, they must be opened and used to see. A person may unexpectedly see something without intending to, so it is impossible to keep one’s gaze lowered at all times. Allah Most High therefore commanded His servants to restrain their gaze, just as Luqmān commanded his son to moderate his voice. As for His words,

“Indeed, those who lower their voices in the presence of the Messenger of Allah,”

and the rest of the verse, He praises them for lowering their voices without qualification in the presence of His Messenger. They are commanded to do so in such circumstances and forbidden to raise their voices in his presence, peace be upon him. Lowering one’s voice without qualification in the presence of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, is a particular, praiseworthy form of restraint. A person can keep his voice lowered at all times, but he has not been commanded to do so. Rather, he is commanded to raise his voice in certain circumstances, either as an obligation or as a recommended act. Hence His words:

“And moderate your voice.”

Restraint in voice and sight encompasses what enters the heart and what leaves it: through hearing, things enter the heart, and through the voice, they leave it. Likewise, He mentions both organs together in His words:

“Have We not given him two eyes, and a tongue and two lips?”

Through the eyes and sight, the heart learns about things, while the tongue and voice express what is within it. The former is the heart’s scout, its informant and spy; the latter is its interpreter.

Then Allah Most High says:

“That is better for you and purer.”

He also says:

“Take charity from their wealth, by which you purify and cleanse them.”

And He says:

“Allah only intends to remove impurity from you, O members of the household, and to purify you thoroughly.”

And in the verse concerning seeking permission to enter, He says:

---

## Page 7941

“And if you are told, ‘Go back,’ then go back; that is purer for you.”

And He says:

“Ask them from behind a screen. That is purer for your hearts and their hearts.”

And He says:

“Give charity before your private consultation. That is better for you and purer.”

The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“O Allah, cleanse my heart of my sins with water, snow, and hail.”

And in the funeral supplication he said:

“Wash him with water, snow, and hail, and cleanse him of his sins as a white garment is cleansed of dirt.”

Purity—and Allah knows best—is freedom from sins, which are filth. Spiritual purification encompasses both purity, meaning freedom from sins, and growth through righteous deeds. This is like the pairing of forgiveness with mercy, deliverance from punishment with attainment of reward, and freedom from evil with attainment of good. Purification, then, is from filth and impurities. Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, the polytheists are impure.”

And He says:

“So shun the filth of idols.”

And He says:

“Wine, gambling, stone altars, and divining arrows are nothing but filth from Satan’s handiwork.”

And He says of the hypocrites:

“So turn away from them; indeed, they are filth.”

And He says concerning the people of Lot:

“And We saved him from the town that practised foul deeds.”

Those who practised sodomy said of Lot and his household:

“Expel them from your town! They are people who keep themselves pure.”

Mujāhid said: “They keep themselves pure from anal intercourse with men.” When entering the privy, one says:

“I seek refuge in You from evil and all that is foul,” and from the filthy, impure, wicked

---

## Page 7942

one whose companions are wicked. This impurity arises from associating partners with Allah, hypocrisy, indecencies, wrongdoing, and the like. It is removed only through repentance involving the abandonment of indecency and other such acts. Whoever repents of them has purified himself; otherwise, he remains defiled, even if he bathes with water to remove major ritual impurity. That bath removes his state of major ritual impurity, but it does not remove the impurity of the indecency that has defiled his heart and inner self. Such impurity cannot be removed by bathing with water; it can only be removed by bathing in the water of sincere repentance maintained until death.

This is the meaning of what Ibn Abī al-Dunyā and others narrated:

Suwayd ibn Sa‘īd narrated to us, saying: Muslim ibn Khālid narrated to us, from Ismā‘īl ibn Kathīr, from Mujāhid, who said:

“Even if someone who commits that act—meaning the act of the people of Lot—were to bathe with every drop of water in the heavens and every drop on earth, he would remain impure.”

Ibn al-Jawzī also narrated this. In his Book Condemning Sodomy, al-Qāsim ibn Khalaf narrated, through his chain of transmission, that al-Fudayl ibn ‘Iyād said:

“Even if a man who practises sodomy were to bathe with every drop that descended from the sky, he would meet Allah impure.”

Abū Muhammad al-Khallāl also narrated this from al-‘Abbās al-Hāshimī, tracing it back to the Prophet.

There is also the report transmitted by Ibrāhīm, from ‘Alqamah, from Ibn Mas‘ūd:

“If the two men engaging in sodomy were to bathe with the water of the sea, it would not suffice them unless they repented.”

Attributing statements of this kind to the Prophet is unsound; they are known only as sayings of the early generations.

Likewise, it is related from Abū Hurayrah and Ibn ‘Abbās that they said:

“The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, addressed us and said in his sermon: ‘Whoever has intercourse with a woman in her anus

---

## Page 7943

or with a boy or a man will be raised on the Day of Resurrection more foul-smelling than a carcass, distressing people with his stench until Allah casts him into the Fire of Hell. Allah will render his deeds worthless and accept from him neither payment nor compensation. He will be placed in a chest of fire, which will be nailed shut over him with iron nails that pierce his face and body.’”

Abū Hurayrah said: “This applies to someone who has not repented.” For whoever refrains from sodomy purifies himself, as the Quran indicates. Whoever practises it, therefore, has not purified himself of it and is thus defiled, since impurity is the opposite of purity. Impurity, however, has different forms, with different rulings.

This is where some jurists have erred. When they saw that the Quran requires someone in a state of major ritual impurity to purify himself, in Allah’s words:

“If you are in a state of major ritual impurity, then purify yourselves,”

they said: “A person in a state of major ritual impurity must therefore be impure.” Yet an authentic hadith from Abū Hurayrah establishes that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“A believer does not become impure.”

He said this when Abū Hurayrah slipped away from him because he was in a state of major ritual impurity and felt reluctant to sit with him. The impurity that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, denied here was impurity requiring purification with water, which Abū Hurayrah had thought affected him.

Major ritual impurity prevents the angels from entering a house containing someone in that state. Ahmad said: “If someone in a state of major ritual impurity puts his hand into a small quantity of water, he renders the water impure.” Some of his followers thought he meant physical impurity, whereas he meant impurity in a legal sense. A derivative cannot have a stronger ruling than its source, nor can water be subject to a more severe ruling than the body. At most, the same impediment affecting the body can affect the water. A person’s bodily state of major ritual impurity prevents him from praying; likewise, the water remains pure but cannot be used for ablution for prayer.

---

## Page 7944

As for zakāh, its meaning encompasses growth and increase, as with crops, although purification may also fall within its meaning. When something is cleansed of what corrupts it, it flourishes, grows, becomes sound and increases, just as crops flourish when cleared of weeds. Allah Most High says:

“Were it not for Allah’s grace and mercy upon you, not one of you would ever have been purified. But Allah purifies whom He wills.”

“He said, ‘Have you killed an innocent soul who had killed no one?’”

He also says:

“Successful indeed is the one who purifies his soul.”

And He says:

“Then go back; that is purer for you.”

Going back is a righteous deed that increases the believer in goodness and purity. He also says:

“That is purer for your hearts and their hearts.”

This avoids occasions for suspicion, which is a form of avoiding sins, staying away from them and keeping them at bay. He thus declares that this is purer for the hearts of both groups.

As for the verse we are discussing, namely His words:

“Tell the believing men to lower their gaze and guard their chastity. That is purer for them,”

lowering the gaze and guarding one’s chastity involve keeping away from the impurity of sins, as well as performing righteous deeds through which a person grows in goodness. Such conduct is purer, and zakāh encompasses purification: it involves both refraining from evil deeds and performing good deeds. For this reason, it is sometimes explained as purification and sometimes as increase and growth. Its meaning encompasses both, even when purification is mentioned alongside it, as in His words:

“Take charity from their wealth to cleanse them and purify them thereby.”

Charity brings about purification from sins and brings about zakāh, which consists in righteous deeds, just as lowering the gaze and guarding one’s chastity are purer for them

---

## Page 7945

and both are attained through avoiding sins and guarding one’s faculties, as well as through repentance and charity, which is doing good. These two are fear of Allah and doing good, and:

“Indeed, Allah is with those who fear Him and those who do good.”

Al-Tirmidhī narrated, and graded as authentic, the report that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was asked, “What most often causes people to enter Hell?” He replied, “The two bodily openings: the mouth and the private parts.” He was also asked, “What most often causes people to enter Paradise?” He replied, “Fear of Allah and good character.”

Fear of Allah includes guarding one’s chastity and lowering one’s gaze. Good character includes kindness to others and refraining from harming them, which requires patience. Kindness to others springs from mercy, and Allah Most High says:

“And they urge one another to patience and urge one another to mercy.”

He, Glorified be He, mentions zakāh here, just as He mentioned it earlier in His words:

“Were it not for Allah’s grace and mercy upon you, not one of you would ever have been purified.”

Avoiding sins brings about zakāh, which consists in the removal of evil and the attainment of good. The successful are those who fulfil their obligations and refrain from what is forbidden, as He describes them at the beginning of Surah al-Baqarah:

“Alif Lām Mīm. This is the Book about which there is no doubt, a guidance for those who fear Allah,”

and the verses that follow. He also says:

“Successful indeed is the one who purifies his soul.”

Thus, since He has declared that these people are successful, and that the successful are those who fear Allah—

“Who believe in the unseen, establish prayer, and spend out of what We have provided for them”—

and has declared that whoever purifies his soul is successful, this indicates that

---

## Page 7946

zakāh encompasses the matters mentioned at the beginning of Surah al-Baqarah.

As for His words:

“Have you not seen those who claim purity for themselves?”

and His words:

“So do not claim purity for yourselves; He knows best who fears Him,”

people’s self-commendation here consists in their declaring themselves pure and believing this, not in actually making themselves pure.

Allah Most High relates that Abraham said:

“Our Lord, send among them a Messenger from themselves who will recite Your verses to them, teach them the Book and wisdom, and purify them.”

He also says:

“Certainly Allah conferred a great favor upon the believers,”

to the end of the verse; and:

“It is He who has sent among the unlettered a Messenger from themselves,”

to the end of the verse. In several places, He, Glorified be He, thus reminds His servants of His favor in sending the Messenger. These are the four tasks with which He sent him: reciting His verses to them, purifying them, teaching them the Book, and teaching them wisdom.

He has also singled out the teaching of the Book and wisdom for mention, as in His words:

“And what He has sent down to you of the Book and wisdom, by which He instructs you.”

And His words:

“And remember what is recited in your houses of the verses of Allah and wisdom.”

This is because reciting the verses to them and purifying them apply to all believers. Recitation means conveying His words, Most High, to them, and this is indispensable for every believer. Purifying them means making their souls pure through righteous deeds arising from the verses they have heard and that have been recited to them. The first is their hearing; the second is their obedience. The believers say, “We hear and we obey.”

The first is their knowledge; the second is their action. Faith consists of words and deeds. Thus, when they hear Allah’s verses, they take them to heart, love them, and act upon them. They are not like those of whom He said:

“The likeness of those who disbelieve is that of

---

## Page 7947

one who calls out to what hears nothing but calls and cries. Deaf, mute and blind, they do not understand.” When they act upon these verses, they thereby purify themselves and become successful believers.

Allah says:

“Allah will raise those of you who believe and those who have been given knowledge in rank.”

Of their opposites, He says:

“The Bedouins are stronger in disbelief and hypocrisy, and more likely not to know the limits of what Allah has revealed to His Messenger.”

He thus describes them as greater in disbelief, hypocrisy and ignorance—the opposites of faith and knowledge. Listening to Allah’s verses and purifying oneself through them are obligatory for everyone. Every servant must hear the message that his Master has sent to him through His Messenger. This is the obligatory listening that forms the foundation of faith. One must also purify oneself by doing what is commanded and abandoning what is forbidden. Both are indispensable.

As for knowledge of the Book and wisdom, this is a collective obligation. Not every individual is required to be a scholar of the Book, knowing its wording and meaning, or to possess knowledge of all wisdom. Rather, the believers collectively are charged with this obligation, just as they are charged with jihad. Indeed, this obligation takes precedence over that of jihad and is more emphatic, for it is the foundation of jihad. Without it, they would not know what they were fighting for. Hence, the Messenger and the believers undertook this before undertaking jihad. Jihad is the pinnacle of religion, its branch and its culmination, whereas this is its root, foundation, pillar and head.

The purpose of the message is the performance of all obligatory and recommended deeds. There is no doubt that listening to Allah’s Book, believing in it, regarding as forbidden what it forbids and as lawful what it permits, acting upon its unambiguous passages, and believing in its ambiguous passages are obligatory

---

## Page 7948

for everyone. This is the recitation mentioned in His words:

“Those to whom We have given the Book recite it as it should be recited; it is they who believe in it.”

He thus states that those who recite it as it should be recited believe in it. This was also the understanding of the early generations of this community—the Companions, the Successors and others. His words, “as it should be recited,” are like His words:

“Strive for Allah as you ought to strive for Him.”

“Fear Allah as He should be feared.”

As for memorizing the entire Quran, understanding all its meanings and knowing the whole Sunnah, this is not obligatory for everyone. Rather, each servant must memorize as much of the Quran, understand as much of its meanings and know as much of the Sunnah as he needs. Whether he must listen to the entire Quran is a matter of disagreement. Yet this knowledge of wisdom required of every servant is not the knowledge of the Book and wisdom that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, taught his Companions and his community. That requires knowing the bounds of what Allah revealed to His Messenger in its wording, meanings, actions and purposes. This is not obligatory for everyone.

Allah’s words,

“So do not claim yourselves to be pure; He knows best who fears Him,”

are evidence that purification is piety, and piety encompasses both matters. Indeed, abandoning evil deeds necessarily entails performing good deeds, for man is ever active and ever intent on action. He abandons the intention to commit evil deeds and their performance only by intending and performing good deeds, since the soul cannot be devoid of both intentions. Rather, man is by nature one who wills and acts. This indicates that what brings this about is

---

## Page 7949

purification and piety, through which a person merits Paradise. As reported in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever undertakes to safeguard for me what is between his jaws and what is between his legs, I undertake to guarantee him Paradise.”

Whoever purifies himself has indeed succeeded and will enter Paradise. Purification entails attaining good and removing evil. When good is attained and evil removed—in knowledge and action—a person gains light, guidance, knowledge and other benefits. Through action, he gains love, a heart that turns to Allah, reverential fear and other virtues.

This is for the person who abandons these prohibited things and carries out what is commanded. Through this, he also gains strength and authority. These are the qualities of perfection: knowledge, action, ability and sound intent. Reports have come down concerning this, including that whoever lowers his gaze gains light and love in his heart, as scholars who act upon their knowledge have themselves experienced.

In the Musnad of Ahmad: ‘Attāb narrated to us from ‘Abd Allah—that is, Ibn al-Mubārak—who said: Yahyā ibn Ayyūb informed us, from ‘Ubayd Allah ibn Zahr, from ‘Alī ibn Yazīd, from al-Qāsim, from Abū Umāmah, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No Muslim looks at a woman’s beauty and then lowers his gaze without Allah granting him, in return, worship whose sweetness he experiences.”

Abū Bakr ibn al-Anbārī also narrated it in his Amālī through Ibn Abī Maryam, from Yahyā ibn Ayyūb, with the same chain. His wording is:

“Whoever looks at a woman and immediately lowers his gaze, Allah will grant him worship whose sweetness he experiences.”

Abū Nu‘aym also narrated it in al-Hilyah:

---

## Page 7950

My father narrated to us: Ibrāhīm ibn Muhammad ibn al-Hasan narrated to us: Muhammad ibn Ya‘qūb narrated to us, saying: Abū al-Yamān narrated to us: Abū Mahdī Sa‘īd ibn Sinān narrated to us, from Abū al-Zāhiriyyah, from Kathīr ibn Murrah, from Ibn ‘Umar, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The first glance is unintentional, the second deliberate, and the third scrutiny. A believer’s looking at a woman’s beauty is a poisoned arrow from the arrows of Iblīs. Whoever refrains from it out of fear of Allah and hope for what is with Him, Allah Most High will reward him with worship whose delight he experiences.”

Abū Ja‘far al-Kharā’itī narrated it in Kitāb I‘tilāl al-Qulūb (The Book of Ailments of the Hearts): ‘Alī ibn Harb narrated to us: Ishāq ibn ‘Abd al-Wāhid narrated to us: Hushaym narrated to us: ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ishāq narrated to us, from Muhārib ibn Dithār, from Jibillah, from Hudhayfah ibn al-Yamān, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Looking at a woman is a poisoned arrow from the arrows of Iblīs. Whoever refrains from it out of fear of Allah, Allah will reward him with faith whose sweetness he finds in his heart.”

Abū Muhammad al-Khallāl also narrated it through ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ishāq, from al-Nu‘mān ibn Sa‘d, from ‘Alī; this version also mentions the arrow.

Abū Nu‘aym also narrated it: ‘Abd Allah ibn Muhammad—that is, Abū al-Shaykh—narrated to us: Ibn ‘Afīr narrated to us, saying: Shu‘ayb ibn Salamah narrated to us: ‘Ismah ibn Muhammad narrated to us, from Mūsā—that is, Ibn ‘Uqbah—from al-Qāsim ibn Muhammad, from ‘Ā’ishah, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No servant restrains his gaze from a woman’s beauty, though he could look at her if he wished, without Allah placing in his heart worship whose sweetness he experiences.”

Ibn Abī al-Fawāris also narrated through

---

## Page 7951

Ibn al-Jawzī, from Muhammad ibn al-Musayyab, who said: ‘Abd Allah narrated to us, saying: al-Hasan narrated to me, from Mujāhid, who said:

“Lowering one’s gaze from what Allah has forbidden gives rise to love of Allah.”

Muslim narrated in his Sahīh through Yūnus ibn ‘Ubayd, from ‘Amr ibn Sa‘īd, from Abū Zur‘ah ibn ‘Amr ibn Jarīr, from his grandfather, Jarīr ibn ‘Abd Allah al-Bajalī, who said:

“I asked the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, about an inadvertent glance, and he instructed me to turn my gaze away.”

Imam Ahmad also narrated it from Hushaym, from Yūnus, with the same chain. Abū Dāwūd, al-Tirmidhī and al-Nasā’ī likewise narrated it through him. Al-Tirmidhī said: “Good and authentic.”

In another version, he said:

“Lower your gaze.”

That is, look towards the ground. Turning one’s gaze away is broader, since it may be towards the ground or in another direction.

Abū Dāwūd said: Ismā‘īl ibn Mūsā al-Fazārī narrated to us: Sharīk narrated to us, from Rabī‘ah al-Iyādī, from ‘Abd Allah ibn Buraydah, from his father, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to ‘Alī: ‘O ‘Alī, do not follow one glance with another. The first is allowed to you, but the next is not.’”

Al-Tirmidhī also narrated it through Sharīk and said: “Singular; we know it only through his narration.”

In the Sahīh, Abū Sa‘īd reported that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Beware of sitting along the roads.”

They said: “O Messenger of Allah, we cannot do without our gathering places where we sit.”

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “If you insist, then give the road its due.”

They asked: “What is the road’s due

---

## Page 7952

, O Messenger of Allah?”

He replied: “Lowering the gaze, refraining from causing harm, returning greetings, enjoining what is right and forbidding what is wrong.”

Abū al-Qāsim al-Baghawī narrated from Abū Umāmah, who said: “I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say: ‘Guarantee me six things, and I will guarantee you Paradise: when any of you speaks, he must not lie; when entrusted with something, he must not betray that trust; and when he makes a promise, he must not break it. Lower your gaze, restrain your hands and guard your chastity.’”

Such looking leads to corruption of the heart. One of the early generations said: “Looking is a poisoned arrow aimed at the heart.” Thus Allah commanded people to guard their chastity, just as He commanded them to lower their gaze, since looking prompts such misconduct.

Al-Tabarānī narrated through ‘Ubayd Allah ibn Yazīd, from al-Qāsim, from Abū Umāmah, who attributed the following to the Prophet:

“You must lower your gaze, guard your chastity and set your faces aright, or your faces will surely be darkened.”

Al-Tabarānī said: Ahmad ibn Zuhayr al-Tustarī narrated to us, saying: We read before Muhammad ibn Hafs ibn ‘Umar, the blind Quran reciter: Yahyā ibn Abī Kathīr narrated to us: Hazīm ibn Sufyān narrated to us, from ‘Abd al-Rahmān ibn Ishāq, from al-Qāsim ibn ‘Abd al-Rahmān, from his father, from Ibn Mas‘ūd, who said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: ‘Looking is one of Satan’s poisoned arrows. Whoever gives it up out of fear of Allah, Allah will grant him in its place faith whose sweetness he will find in his heart.’”

In the authentic hadith of Abū Hurayrah, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The adultery of the eyes is looking.”

He then related the rest of the hadith. Al-Bukhārī narrated it with the beginning of its chain omitted, and Muslim with a connected chain. They also used to forbid

---

## Page 7953

a man from fixing his gaze on beardless youths, and they questioned the religious integrity of anyone who did so. Many scholars held that a woman may not look at non-mahram men at all, whether with desire or without it.

Shaykh al-Islām said:

As for light, knowledge and wisdom, these are indicated by Allah’s words in the account of Joseph:

“When he reached maturity, We granted him wisdom and knowledge. Thus do We reward those who do good.”

These are granted to everyone who does good. In this surah, He mentioned the Verse of Light after the commands to lower the gaze, guard one’s chastity and repent of the lapses in these matters that the son of Adam inevitably commits.

Abū ‘Abd al-Rahmān al-Sulamī said:

I heard Abū al-Husayn al-Warrāq say: “Whoever lowers his gaze from something forbidden, Allah thereby grants him wisdom upon his tongue, by which he finds guidance and guides others to the path of His pleasure.”

This is because the recompense corresponds to the deed. If a person refrains for Allah’s sake from looking at something he loves, Allah compensates him with something he loves even more. If looking by the light of the eye is objectionable, or is directed at something objectionable, and he refrains from it for Allah’s sake, Allah grants him light in his heart and insight by which he perceives the truth.

Shāh al-Kirmānī said:

“Whoever lowers his gaze from forbidden things, cultivates his inner self through constant awareness that Allah is watching and his outward conduct through following the Sunnah, accustoms himself to eating what is lawful, and restrains himself from desires—his discernment will never fail him.”

When a man’s knowledge becomes sound, so that he recognizes the truth, acts upon it and follows it, he becomes pure, God-fearing and deserving of Paradise.

---

## Page 7954

This is further supported by the well-known hadith of Abū Umāmah transmitted by al-Baghawī:

Tālūt ibn ‘Abbād narrated to us: Fadālah ibn Jubayr narrated to us: I heard Abū Umāmah say: I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

“Guarantee me six things, and I will guarantee you Paradise: when any of you speaks, let him not lie; when entrusted with something, let him not betray that trust; when he makes a promise, let him not break it. Lower your gazes, restrain your hands, and guard your chastity.”

He thus guaranteed Paradise to whoever fulfils these six qualities. The first three free a person from hypocrisy, and the last three from sinful disobedience. Those addressed are Muslims: if a person is not a hypocrite, he is a believer; and if he is not sinfully disobedient, he is God-fearing and therefore deserving of Paradise.

This accords with what Ibn Abī al-Dunyā narrated:

Abū Sa‘īd al-Madanī narrated to us: ‘Umar ibn Sahl al-Māzinī narrated to me, saying: ‘Umar ibn Muhammad ibn Suhbān narrated to me: Safwān ibn Sulaym narrated to me, from Abū Hurayrah, who said: The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Every eye will weep on the Day of Resurrection except an eye that turned away from what Allah has forbidden, an eye that stayed awake in Allah’s cause, and an eye that shed a tear the size of a fly’s head out of fear of Allah.”

The words of Allah, glorified is He:

“Do not gaze longingly at the worldly splendour We have granted some of them to enjoy, through which We test them,”

include looking at wealth, clothing, physical appearances and other worldly enjoyments. As for clothing and physical appearances, these are the two things Allah does not look at, as reported in Sahīh Muslim from Abū Hurayrah, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

---

## Page 7955

“Allah does not look at your outward forms or your wealth; rather, He looks at your hearts and deeds.”

Allah Most High has also said:

“How many generations We destroyed before them who were better in possessions and outward appearance!”

Allah grants enjoyment through physical beauty just as He grants enjoyment through wealth. Both belong to the splendour of worldly life, and both are a temptation to their possessors and associates, sometimes leading a person to ruin in this world and the Hereafter. Those who perish are of two kinds: those who have the means and those who do not. Those without the means are tempted by looking and gazing longingly at these things, while those with the means are tempted by what they have been given, immersed in it and surrounded by something from which they cannot rescue themselves.

A believer may admire the person he sees, even if that person is a hypocrite or an evildoer, just as he may admire what he hears from such people. Allah Most High says:

“When you see them, their bodies impress you, and when they speak, you listen to what they say. They are like propped-up pieces of wood. They think every cry is against them. They are the enemy, so beware of them. May Allah destroy them!”

This is a warning from Allah Most High against looking at them and listening to what they say. They should therefore not be looked at, nor should their words be listened to, for Allah, glorified is He, has stated that their appearance impresses those who look at them, and their speech impresses those who listen.

He then describes the corruption of their hearts and deeds by saying:

“They are like propped-up pieces of wood.”

This is a likeness of their hearts and deeds. Allah Most High also says:

“Among people is one whose speech about worldly life impresses you,”

and the rest of the verse.

Allah Most High says concerning the people of Lot:

“Indeed, in that are signs for those who discern.”

The word tawassum (discernment) derives from simah, meaning a distinguishing mark. He has thus stated

---

## Page 7956

—glorified is He—that He has made the punishments of transgressors signs for those who discern.

Al-Tirmidhī reports that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“Beware of the believer’s discernment, for he sees by the light of Allah.”

He then recited:

“Indeed, in that are signs for those who discern.”

This shows that whoever takes heed of Allah’s punishment of others who committed sexual indecencies is among those who discern. Allah Most High states that He obliterated the sight of those who practised sodomy. Thus, the punishment of those who commit sexual indecencies was the loss of sight, as has been known and witnessed among them. Conversely, those who took heed of their fate and refrained from their deeds were rewarded with light. This accords with the placement of the Verse of Light after the command to lower the gaze.

As for the ability and strength that Allah grants those who fear Him and resist their desires, this is a well-known reality. As has been related:

“Satan fears the shadow of one who abandons his desires.”

An authentic hadith records that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“The strong man is not the one who overpowers others in wrestling; rather, the strong man is the one who controls himself when angry.”

Another narration relates:

“He passed by some people flicking a stone and said, ‘Strength does not lie in this. Rather, strength is for one of you to be filled with rage and then restrain it for Allah’s sake.’”

Or words to that effect. He spoke here of anger because it is very common among human beings and is apparent to others. The sway of desire, however, is generally concealed from people’s eyes, and its devil is hidden. Moreover, it is often possible to find a lawful alternative to

---

## Page 7957

what is unlawful. Otherwise, when desire is inflamed and gains mastery, it may be stronger than anger. Allah Most High has said:

“And mankind was created weak.”

That is, weak in relation to women, unable to bear being without them. In explaining His words:

“Our Lord, do not burden us with what we have no strength to bear,”

passionate love has been mentioned as one such burden. Passionate love leads those afflicted by it to illness and destruction, although anger can also reach that point.

In many places, the Quran indicates that strength and honour belong to those who obey Allah and repent to Him, as in His words in Surah Hūd:

“O my people, seek your Lord’s forgiveness, then repent to Him. He will send abundant rain upon you and add strength to your strength.”

And His words:

“Honour belongs to Allah, His Messenger, and the believers.”

“Do not weaken or grieve, for you will have the upper hand if you are believers.”

If Allah grants light, knowledge, strength, honour, and love for Allah and His Messenger to someone who abandons sins—lowering his gaze, guarding his chastity, and refraining from other things Allah has forbidden—then what do you think of someone who has never even approached sins, never cast a glance at them, and never entertained the thought of committing them, but instead strives in Allah’s cause against those who commit them so that they may abandon them? Are these two alike? Rather, the latter has many times more light, faith, honour, strength, love, authority, and deliverance in this world and the Hereafter than the former. His station is greater and higher, and his light more complete and stronger.

Souls crave sins, and Satan makes them attractive, so that doubts and desires come together in them. Thus, if Allah has made faith beloved to the believer, adorned it in his heart, and made hateful

---

## Page 7958

to him disbelief, defiance, and disobedience, so that he is given love for Allah and His Messenger and all that follows from it in place of misguided desires, and light and guidance in place of desires and doubts, and Allah has supported him with strength and ability—having countered ignorance with knowledge, the desire for evil deeds with the desire for good deeds, and the capacity for evil with the capacity for good within himself alone—then the one who strives in Allah’s cause seeks to accomplish this within both himself and others, dispelling his ignorance of wrongdoing and countering his desire for evil deeds with a desire for good deeds, and so forth.

Jihad is the completion of faith and the pinnacle of deeds, as Allah Most High says:

“The believers are only those who believe in Allah and His Messenger, then never doubt, and strive with their wealth and their lives in Allah’s cause. It is they who are truthful.”

And He says:

“You are the best nation brought forth for mankind”—and the rest of the verse.

And He says:

“Have you made providing water to pilgrims”—and the rest of the verse.

Likewise, this reward belongs to those who strive in Allah’s cause, as Allah Most High says:

“As for those who strive for Our sake, We will surely guide them to Our ways.”

This concerns knowledge and light. And He says:

“Had We commanded them, ‘Kill yourselves’”

up to His words:

“a straight path.”

Killing themselves means some of them killing others, which is part of jihad; leaving their homes means emigration. He then states that, if they did what they were exhorted to do—emigration and jihad—it would be better for them and make them more steadfast. Thus, the passage contains four things: good without qualification; steadfastness, which includes strength and ability; a great reward; and guidance to the straight path.

Allah Most High says:

“O you who believe, if you support Allah, He will support you and make your feet firm.”

And He says: “And Allah will surely help

---

## Page 7959

those who support Him,” up to His words:

“the outcome of all affairs.”

And He says:

“They strive in Allah’s cause and do not fear the blame of any critic.”

As for those who commit acts of sexual immorality, who neither lower their gaze nor guard their chastity, Allah has described them in opposite terms: intoxication, blind wandering, ignorance, lack of reason, lack of sound judgment, hatred, and the obliteration of sight. This is in addition to His descriptions of their filth, defiance, aggression, excess, evil, indecency, corruption, and criminality.

He says of the people of Lot:

“Rather, you are an ignorant people.”

Thus He describes them as ignorant. And He says:

“By your life, they wander blindly in their intoxication.”

And He says:

“Is there not a single right-minded man among you?”

And He says:

“So We obliterated their sight.”

And He says:

“Rather, you are a people who transgress all bounds.”

And He says:

“So see what was the fate of the criminals.”

And He says:

“Indeed, they were an evil, defiantly disobedient people.”

And He says:

“Do you indeed approach men, waylay travelers, and commit evil in your gatherings?”

Up to His words:

“Help me against the corrupt people.”

And up to His words:

“because of their defiant disobedience.”

And His words:

“Marked by your Lord for those who transgress all bounds.”

---

## Page 7960

Section

His words at the end of the verse contain profound lessons:

“And turn to Allah in repentance, all of you, O believers, so that you may succeed.”

Among these is that His command to all believers to repent in this context draws attention to the fact that no believer is entirely free of some of these sins: failing to lower the gaze, guard one’s chastity, and refrain from displaying adornment, and other related matters. Some commit little of this, while others commit much. As stated in the hadith:

“There is no descendant of Adam who has not sinned or contemplated a sin, except John, son of Zechariah.”

This arises only from looking. In the Sunan collections, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:

“All the children of Adam frequently sin, and the best of those who sin are those who repeatedly repent.”

In the Sahīh, Abū Dharr reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah Most High says: ‘My servants, you sin by night and by day, and I forgive all sins without concern. So seek My forgiveness, and I will forgive you.’”

In the two Sahīhs, Ibn ʿAbbās said: “I have seen nothing that more closely resembles minor lapses than what Abū Hurayrah reported: ‘The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Allah has decreed for the son of Adam his share of fornication, which he will inevitably commit. The fornication of the eyes is looking, and the fornication of the tongue is speaking.”’” The hadith continues to its end.

It also contains the words:

“And the soul

---

## Page 7961

longs for this and desires it, and the private parts confirm or belie it.” Al-Bukhārī recorded it with the beginning of its chain of transmission omitted, through Tāwūs from Abū Hurayrah.

Muslim narrated it through Suhayl ibn Abī Sālih, from his father, from Abū Hurayrah, from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, who said:

“The son of Adam’s share of fornication has been decreed for him, and he will inevitably commit it. The fornication of the eyes is looking; the fornication of the ears is listening; the fornication of the tongue is speaking; the fornication of the hands is grasping; and the fornication of the feet is walking. The heart desires and longs, and the private parts confirm or belie this.”

Al-Tirmidhī narrated a hadith from Ibn ‘Abbās concerning His words, “except for minor lapses,” and classified it as singular:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:
‘If You forgive, O Allah, You forgive much;
And which servant of Yours has never lapsed?’”

Another lesson is that those who commit acts of sexual immorality, failing to lower their gaze and guard their chastity, are commanded to repent. They were commanded to do so only so that their repentance would be accepted. Thus, repentance is accepted from them and from all other sinners, as Allah Most High says:

“Do they not know that Allah is the One who accepts repentance from His servants and receives their charitable offerings?”

And Allah Most High says:

“He is the One who accepts repentance from His servants, pardons evil deeds, and knows what you do.”

This applies even when the acts of sexual immorality are aggravated by their severity and frequency—such as intercourse with women within the prohibited degrees of marriage, the practices of the people of Lot, or other such acts—and whether it is the active or the passive partner who repents. Whoever repents, Allah accepts his repentance. This is contrary to the attitude of some people: when they see someone who has committed any of these acts of sexual immorality, they make him despair of Allah’s mercy

---

## Page 7962

to the point that one of them says, “Whoever does any of these things will never prosper.” They have no hope that his repentance will be accepted. It is reported that ‘Alī said, “Among us are such-and-such, and among us are such-and-such, but one who has been sodomized is not one of us.” They say, “Such a person can never become righteous again, even if he repents,” despite his being a Muslim who acknowledges that what he did is forbidden.

They include in this judgment those who have been compelled to engage in any of these acts of sexual immorality, saying, “Had this person possessed any goodness in Allah’s sight, He would not have allowed someone to overpower him, do such a thing to him, and compel him.” Such things are done to many slaves, willingly or by force, and likewise to craftsmen’s hired workers, willingly or by force, as well as others in similar circumstances, such as boys attending Quran schools and others. They have forgotten the words of Allah Most High:

“Do not force your slave girls into prostitution, if they wish to remain chaste, seeking the fleeting gains of this worldly life. If anyone compels them, then after they have been compelled, Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”

These people may not understand what repentance entails. This attitude may be a person’s disposition and practice, or it may be a belief. This is among the gravest forms of misguidance and error, for despairing of Allah’s mercy is on a par with feeling secure from Allah’s plan. Their attitude is the opposite of that of those who regard acts of sexual immorality as lawful: the latter feel secure from Allah’s plan against those who commit them, while the former make those who commit them despair of Allah’s mercy. A person of truly profound religious understanding neither makes people despair of Allah’s mercy nor emboldens them to disobey Him. In the matter of deliberate sins, this is analogous to the attitude of those who follow their desires and religious innovations

---

## Page 7963

for each of them regards those evil deeds as good deeds and thus feels secure from Allah’s plan. Many people believe that the repentance of one who introduces religious innovations is not accepted. Yet Allah Most High has said:

“Indeed, Allah forgives all sins. He is indeed the All-Forgiving, Most Merciful.”

In the two Sahīh collections, Abū Mūsā al-Ashʿarī said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would tell us his names. He said, ‘I am Muhammad, and I am Ahmad, the one who follows the earlier prophets, the Gatherer, the Prophet of Repentance, and the Prophet of Mercy.’”

In another hadith:

“I am the Prophet of Mercy, and I am the Prophet of Battle.”

This is because he was sent with battle—meaning the killing of those who disobey him—with repentance for those who obey him, and with mercy for those who believe him and follow him. He is a mercy to all worlds. The prophets before him were not commanded to fight.

When someone from their communities committed certain sins, he needed, in addition to repentance, to undergo severe punishments. As Allah Most High said:

“And when Moses said to his people, ‘O my people, you have certainly wronged yourselves by taking the calf as an object of worship. So repent to your Maker and kill one another. That is better for you in the sight of your Maker.’ Then He accepted your repentance.”

It is reported from Abū al-ʿĀliyah and others that when one of them committed a sin, he would find the sin and its prescribed expiation written on his door the following morning. Allah then revealed concerning this community:

“And those who, when they commit an act of sexual immorality or wrong themselves, remember Allah and seek forgiveness for their sins,”

up to His words:

“And excellent is the reward of those who do good.”

He thus singled out sexual immorality for mention alongside His words, “wrong themselves,” even though wrongdoing encompasses sexual immorality and other sins, confirming what we have stated

---

## Page 7964

concerning the acceptance of repentance for acts of sexual immorality in all cases, from both men and women who commit them.

It is reported in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah extends His hand by night so that those who sinned by day may repent, and He extends His hand by day so that those who sinned by night may repent, until the sun rises from the west.”

It is also reported in the Sahīh that he said:

“Whoever repents before the sun rises from the west, Allah will accept his repentance.”

It is also reported in the Sunan collections that he said:

“Migration will not cease until repentance ceases, and repentance will not cease until the sun rises from the west.”

It is reported that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Satan said, ‘By Your might, my Lord, I shall never cease leading the children of Adam astray so long as their souls remain in their bodies.’ The Lord Most High replied, ‘By My might, My majesty, and My exalted place, I shall continue to forgive them so long as they seek My forgiveness.’”

Abū Dharr reported that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah says, ‘O son of Adam, so long as you call upon Me and place your hope in Me, I shall forgive you for whatever you have done, and I shall not mind. Son of Adam, were your sins to reach the heights of heaven and you then sought My forgiveness, I would forgive you, and I would not mind. Son of Adam, were you to meet Me with sins nearly filling the earth, and then meet Me without associating any partner with Me, I would come to you with as much forgiveness.’”

Whoever rules out repentance for any of these people, whether by his conduct or his words, falls into one of two cases: either he says, “If one of them repents, his repentance will not be accepted,” or

---

## Page 7965

one of them says, “Allah will never accept my repentance.”

The first view is refuted by Allah’s Book, the Sunnah of His Prophet, and the consensus of the Muslims. Some scholars have, however, discussed the repentance of a murderer and of one who calls others to religious innovations. There is disagreement on this within Ahmad’s school, and within Mālik’s school as well, as mentioned by the author of al-Tamthīl wa-l-Bayān in al-Jāmiʿ and elsewhere. They have also discussed the repentance of a zindīq (heretic) and similar cases.

They may disagree over whether outwardly professed repentance averts punishment, either because its sincerity is not known or because it does not cancel a prescribed punishment that has already become due. Yet no jurist has said that if a zindīq or someone like him sincerely repents before Allah, Allah will not accept his repentance. As for the murderer and the person who leads others astray, the issue concerns the rights that others have against them. Repentance for violations of people’s rights is a separate matter, and this is not the place to discuss it or its details. The point here is simply that Allah accepts repentance from every sin, as the Quran and Sunnah indicate.

As for acts of sexual immorality in particular, I know of no one who has disputed that repentance from them is accepted. The one who commits illicit intercourse and the one with whom it is committed are alike in this: if they repent, Allah accepts their repentance. What Allah relates in the account of the people of Lot makes clear, in particular, that repentance from the act of the people of Lot is open to both participants. They used to commit this indecency with one another, yet He called upon them all to fear Allah and repent of it. Had the repentance of the receptive participant, or anyone else, been unacceptable, He would not have commanded them to do something that would not be accepted. The Most High says:

“The people of Lot rejected the messengers.”

“When their brother Lot said to them, ‘Will you not fear Allah?’”

---

## Page 7966

“I am indeed a trustworthy messenger to you.”

“So fear Allah and obey me.”

Thus, he commanded them to fear Allah, which entails repenting of this indecency. If the address is directed specifically to the active participant, this is only because he is ordinarily the one who feels desire and seeks the act, unlike the receptive participant. The latter was not originally created with a desire for this, although such desire may arise because of an illness that develops, payment he receives from the active participant, or some other purpose.

And Allah, glorified and exalted is He, knows best.

---

## Page 7967

Shaykh al-Islām was asked about the words of Allah Most High:

“Tell the believing men to lower their gaze and guard their chastity. That is purer for them. Indeed, Allah is All-Aware of what they do.”

“And tell the believing women to lower their gaze and guard their chastity, and not display their adornment except what is apparent”—to the end of the verse—and about the hadith of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioning “the fornication of all the bodily organs.”

What ruling applies to a man who touches the hand of a beardless boy? Is this like touching women, in that it invalidates ablution, or not? What ruling applies to a man when beardless youths come to him and he reaches out his hand to this one and that one, taking pleasure in doing so? What has been related concerning the prohibition of looking at the face of a handsome beardless youth?

Is the following reported hadith authentic or not: “Looking at a beautiful face is an act of worship”?

If someone says, “I do not look at a handsome beardless youth for any particular purpose; rather, when I see him, I say: ‘Glory be to Allah! Blessed is Allah, the best of creators!’” is this statement correct or not? Give us a fatwa; may you be rewarded. (*)

He replied—may Allah sanctify his soul, illuminate his grave, have mercy on him, be pleased with him, grant benefit through his knowledge, and resurrect us in his company—:

> **Footnotes:**
> (*) Shaykh Nāsir ibn Hamad al-Fahd stated (pp. 134–136):
> There are two points here:
> First: This fatwa is repeated verbatim at 21/243–259.
> Second: Omissions and textual corruptions have occurred in several places here. These can be identified by comparison with the other occurrence. Examples include:
> 1 - P. 411: “did not take account of it in some instances of intercourse.” The correct wording is “in the context of intercourse,” as at 21/244.
> 2 - P. 412: “Just as it is forbidden to take pleasure in touching women permanently forbidden to one in marriage or a non-mahram woman, [indeed, most scholars hold that this (1) is more sinful than taking pleasure in a non-mahram woman], just as the majority…” The bracketed passage was omitted because the copyist’s eye skipped ahead. It appears at 21/245.
> 3 - P. 413: “or the desire for pleasure through looking, [so if he looked at his mother, sister, or daughter, taking pleasure in looking at her] as he takes pleasure in looking at the face of a non-mahram woman.” The bracketed passage is missing here but appears at 21/245.
> 4 - P. 413: “at the faces of [non-mahram] women.” The addition “non-mahram” appears to have been made by the compiler, may Allah have mercy on him, since it is enclosed in square brackets. It does not appear in the other occurrence.
> 5 - P. 414, line four: “assisting through procuring or otherwise.” The wording at 21/246 is “assisting in sexual immorality through procuring or otherwise.”
> 6 - P. 415: “Those forbidden things—meaning carrion, blood, and pork—if he consumes them while regarding them as lawful, he is liable to discretionary punishment.” The wording has been corrupted. The correct wording, as at 21/247, is “if he consumes them without regarding them as lawful.”
> 7 - P. 417: “But if it occurs in a manner that does not diminish religious commitment and merely provides relaxation, such as looking at flowers, then this belongs to the vain activity that does not help uphold the truth,” as at 21/249.
> 8 - P. 419: “The means leading to corruption: blocking it when no overriding benefit opposes doing so.” The correct wording is “…the means leading to corruption must be blocked,” as at 12/251.
> 9 - P. 422: “For devotion to these forms…” The wording at 21/254 is “and the subject of attachment to forms,” which is clearer.
> 10 - P. 424: “until this is lawful and that is unlawful” (hattā yakūna hādhā halāl wa-hādhā harām). The correct wording is “this is lawful and that is unlawful” (takūna hādhā halālan wa-hādhā harāman), as at 21/255.
> 11 - P. 425: “for it is the heart’s illumination and discernment.” The correct wording is “for it produces illumination of the heart and discernment,” as at 21/256.
> 12 - P. 426: “a sixth quality.” The correct wording is “a fifth,” as at 21/257.
> 13 - P. 426, in the passage: “The servant is recompensed for his deeds with something of the same kind as his deeds. [Thus, for lowering his gaze from what is forbidden, Allah compensates him with something of the same kind that is better than it], thereby releasing the light of his inner sight.” The bracketed passage is missing here but appears at 21/257.
> 14 - P. 426: “and docile mules clattered along carrying them.” The word dhulul (“docile”) is an interpolation, as indicated by 12/257.
> 15 - P. 427: “and explaining the Creator’s distinctness.” The correct wording is “and the Creator’s distinctness from creation,” as at 21/258.
> (1) Meaning taking pleasure in touching a beardless youth.

---

## Page 7968

Praise be to Allah.

If a man touches a beardless youth with desire, there are two opinions within the school of Ahmad and elsewhere:

The first is that, like touching women with desire, it invalidates ablution. This is the well-known position in Mālik’s school, and al-Qādī Abū Ya‘lā mentioned it in Sharh al-Madhhab. It is also one of two positions within al-Shāfi‘ī’s school.

The second is that it does not invalidate ablution. This is the well-known position in al-Shāfi‘ī’s school.

The first opinion is more convincing. Anal intercourse invalidates the acts of worship that vaginal intercourse invalidates, such as fasting, ihrām and devotional retreat, and it requires a ritual bath just as vaginal intercourse does. Thus, in matters of worship, the acts preliminary to either form of intercourse have the same rulings. If a man touches a beardless youth with desire while in ihrām, he must offer a sacrifice, just as he must if he touches a non-mahram woman with desire. Likewise, touching a beardless youth with desire must have the same ruling as touching a woman with desire regarding the invalidation of ablution.

Those who hold that touching him does not invalidate ablution argue: “He was not created as a partner for that.”

The response is: There is no doubt that he was not created for that, and that the abomination of sodomy is among the gravest forbidden acts. Yet this consideration does not determine the rulings governing certain forms of intercourse. If a man engages in anal intercourse, the aforementioned rulings apply, even though the anus was not created as a site

---

## Page 7969

for intercourse, even though the natural aversion to anal intercourse is greater than the aversion to touching.

According to most scholars—including Mālik, Ahmad and others—the invalidation of ablution through touching depends on the actual presence of the underlying rationale: namely, that the touching is motivated by desire. The same consideration applies to ihrām, devotional retreat and other matters. According to this view, wherever touching with desire occurs, the ruling applies. Thus, even if a man touches his daughter, sister or mother with desire, his ablution is invalidated. The same therefore applies to touching a beardless youth.

Al-Shāfi‘ī, and Ahmad according to one report from him, consider instead the presumed presence of desire: women are presumed to be objects of desire, so touching them invalidates ablution, whether with desire or without it. For this reason, touching women whom one is permanently forbidden to marry does not invalidate ablution. Yet if he touches such women with desire, the underlying rationale is actually present. The same applies if he touches a beardless youth with desire.

Taking pleasure in touching a beardless youth—by shaking his hand or the like—is forbidden by the consensus of the Muslims, just as taking pleasure in touching women one is permanently forbidden to marry or a non-mahram woman is forbidden. Likewise, the majority hold that the punishment for sodomy is more severe than the punishment for unlawful intercourse with a non-mahram woman. Both the active and passive partners must therefore be put to death, regardless of either party’s marital status and regardless of whether one is the other’s slave. This is reported from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in the Sunan collections, and his Companions acted upon it, with no known disagreement among them.

Execution is by stoning, just as Allah destroyed the people of Lot. The revealed law likewise prescribes stoning as the means of executing the adulterer: “The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stoned Mā‘iz ibn Mālik, the Ghāmidī woman and the two Jews

---

## Page 7970

and the woman to whom he sent Unays, saying: ‘Go to this man’s wife; if she confesses, stone her.’ So he stoned her.”

Looking with desire at the face of a beardless youth is like looking with desire at the face of a woman whom one is permanently forbidden to marry or a non-mahram woman. This applies whether the desire is for intercourse or for the pleasure of looking, just as one might take pleasure in looking at a non-mahram woman’s face. Everyone knows that this is forbidden; the same applies to looking at the face of a beardless youth, by the consensus of the imams.

To say that looking at the face of a beardless youth is an act of worship is like saying that looking at the faces of non-mahram women, or at one’s female relatives whom one is permanently forbidden to marry—such as one’s daughter, mother or sister—is an act of worship. It is well known that whoever regards such forbidden looking as worship is like someone who regards acts of indecency as worship. Allah Most High says:

“When they commit an indecency, they say, ‘We found our forefathers doing it, and Allah has commanded us to do it.’ Say, ‘Allah does not command indecency. Do you say about Allah what you do not know?’”

It is well known that the physical forms of non-mahram women and women whom one is permanently forbidden to marry may offer lessons and indications pointing to the Creator of the same kind as those found in the physical forms of beardless youths. Would any Muslim say that a person may look in this way at the forms of women throughout the world and of his own female relatives whom he is permanently forbidden to marry, and claim that this is worship? Rather, whoever regards this kind of

---

## Page 7971

looking as worship is a disbeliever and an apostate who must be called upon to repent; if he repents, well and good; otherwise, he is to be put to death. He is like someone who considers assisting a person seeking to commit indecency an act of worship, or regards drinking a small quantity of wine or becoming intoxicated by hashish as worship.

Whoever regards facilitating indecency, by procuring or otherwise, as worship, or regards any act known to be forbidden in Islam as worship, must be called upon to repent; if he repents, well and good; otherwise, he is to be put to death. In doing so, he resembles the polytheists:

“When they commit an indecency, they say, ‘We found our forefathers doing it, and Allah has commanded us to do it.’ Say, ‘Allah does not command indecency. Do you say about Allah what you do not know?’”

Their indecency consisted only in circumambulating the House naked. They would say, “We will not circumambulate in clothes in which we have disobeyed Allah.” Thus, they circumambulated naked only to avoid wearing the clothes in which they had sinned. Yet Allah said what He said about them. What, then, of someone who regards acts of indecency involving sexual desire as worship?

Allah, glorified is He, has commanded in His Book that the gaze be lowered. This is of two kinds: averting the gaze from nakedness, and averting it from objects of sexual desire.

The first includes a man averting his gaze from another person’s nakedness, as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“A man must not look at another man’s nakedness, nor a woman at another woman’s nakedness.”

A person must cover his nakedness, as he said to Mu‘āwiyah ibn Haydah:

“Guard your nakedness from everyone except your wife or those whom your right hand possesses.

---

## Page 7972

” I said, “What if one of us is among his people?” He replied, “If you can avoid letting anyone see it, then let no one see it.” I said, “What if one of us is alone?” He replied, “Allah is more deserving of your modesty than people are.”

It is permissible to uncover one’s nakedness to the extent necessary, as when relieving oneself. Likewise, when a man bathes alone—where he has something to screen him from view—he may bathe naked, as Moses and Job did, and as reported concerning the Prophet’s bathing, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, on the day of the Conquest and in Maymūnah’s hadith.

As for the second kind of looking—such as looking at a non-mahram woman’s concealed adornment—it is more serious than the first, just as wine is more serious than carrion, blood, and pork. Its drinker is subject to the prescribed punishment, whereas someone who consumes those prohibited foods while regarding them as lawful is subject to discretionary punishment. This is because people do not crave these prohibited foods as they crave wine. Likewise, looking at a man’s nakedness is not desired as looking at women and the like is desired.

Looking lustfully at a beardless youth also falls into this category. The scholars unanimously agree that this is forbidden, just as they unanimously agree that it is forbidden to look lustfully at a non-mahram woman or at women whom one is permanently forbidden to marry.

The Creator, glorified is He, is to be glorified upon seeing all His creatures. The creation of a beardless youth is no more wondrous in terms of His power than the creation of a bearded man; nor is the creation of women any more wondrous in

---

## Page 7973

terms of His power than the creation of men. Thus, for someone to single out the sight of a beardless youth for glorifying Allah, rather than the sight of anyone else, is like singling out the sight of a woman rather than a man. This is not because, in his eyes, it offers greater evidence of the Creator’s greatness, but because beauty affects his heart and mind. What he sees may overwhelm him, so that his glorification is prompted by the desire aroused within him. Likewise, when the women saw Joseph:

“They were overwhelmed by him, cut their hands, and exclaimed, ‘Allah forbid! This is no human being; this is none other than a noble angel.’”

It is established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah does not look at your appearance or your wealth; rather, He looks at your hearts and your deeds.”

If Allah does not look at appearances and wealth, but rather at hearts and deeds, how can one favour a person on grounds on which Allah has not favoured him?

The Exalted has also said:

“Do not gaze longingly at the splendour of worldly life We have granted some groups of them to enjoy, so that We may test them thereby.”

And He said of the hypocrites:
